      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`none\*(C'.
* **master, m**  
  .IX Item "master, m"
  Set the master key points. These points will define a second pass mapping. It
  is sometimes called a luminance\*(R" or \*(L"value\*(R" mapping. It can be used with
  **r**, **g**, **b** or **all** since it acts like a
  post-processing \s-1LUT.\s0
* **red, r**  
  .IX Item "red, r"
  Set the key points for the red component.
* **green, g**  
  .IX Item "green, g"
  Set the key points for the green component.
* **blue, b**  
  .IX Item "blue, b"
  Set the key points for the blue component.
* **all**  
  .IX Item "all"
  Set the key points for all components (not including master).
  Can be used in addition to the other key points component
  options. In this case, the unset component(s) will fallback on this
  **all** setting.
* **psfile**  
  .IX Item "psfile"
  Specify a Photoshop curves file (\f(CW`.acv\*(C') to import the settings from.
* **plot**  
  .IX Item "plot"
  Save Gnuplot script of the curves in specified file.

To avoid some filtergraph syntax conflicts, each key points list need to be
defined using the following syntax: \f(CW`x0/y0 x1/y1 x2/y2 ...\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Increase slightly the middle level of blue:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          curves=blue=0/0 0.5/0.58 1/1\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Vintage effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          curves=r=0/0.11 .42/.51 1/0.95\*(Aq:g=\*(Aq0/0 0.50/0.48 1/1\*(Aq:b=\*(Aq0/0.22 .49/.44 1/0.8\*(Aq
  .Ve
  .Sp
  Here we obtain the following coordinates for each components:
    * _red_  
      .IX Item "red"
      \f(CW`(0;0.11) (0.42;0.51) (1;0.95)\*(C'
    * _green_  
      .IX Item "green"
      \f(CW`(0;0) (0.50;0.48) (1;1)\*(C'
    * _blue_  
      .IX Item "blue"
      \f(CW`(0;0.22) (0.49;0.44) (1;0.80)\*(C'
* ·  
  The previous example can also be achieved with the associated built-in preset:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          curves=preset=vintage
  .Ve
* ·  
  Or simply:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          curves=vintage
  .Ve
* ·  
  Use a Photoshop preset and redefine the points of the green component:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          curves=psfile=MyCurvesPresets/purple.acv\*(Aq:green=\*(Aq0/0 0.45/0.53 1/1\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Check out the curves of the \f(CW`cross\_process\*(C' profile using **ffmpeg**
  and **gnuplot**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          ffmpeg -f lavfi -i color -vf curves=cross_process:plot=/tmp/curves.plt -frames:v 1 -f null -
          gnuplot -p /tmp/curves.plt
  .Ve

<a name="datascope"></a>

### datascope

.IX Subsection "datascope"
Video data analysis filter.

This filter shows hexadecimal pixel values of part of video.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set output video size.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
  Set x offset from where to pick pixels.
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  Set y offset from where to pick pixels.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set scope mode, can be one of the following:
    * **mono**  
      .IX Item "mono"
      Draw hexadecimal pixel values with white color on black background.
    * **color**  
      .IX Item "color"
      Draw hexadecimal pixel values with input video pixel color on black
      background.
    * **color2**  
      .IX Item "color2"
      Draw hexadecimal pixel values on color background picked from input video,
      the text color is picked in such way so its always visible.
* **axis**  
  .IX Item "axis"
  Draw rows and columns numbers on left and top of video.
* **opacity**  
  .IX Item "opacity"
  Set background opacity.

<a name="dctdnoiz"></a>

### dctdnoiz

.IX Subsection "dctdnoiz"
Denoise frames using 2D \s-1DCT\s0 (frequency domain filtering).

This filter is not designed for real time.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **sigma, s**  
  .IX Item "sigma, s"
  Set the noise sigma constant.
  .Sp
  This _sigma_ defines a hard threshold of \f(CW`3 * sigma\*(C'; every \s-1DCT\s0
  coefficient (absolute value) below this threshold with be dropped.
  .Sp
  If you need a more advanced filtering, see **expr**.
  .Sp
  Default is \f(CW0.
* **overlap**  
  .IX Item "overlap"
  Set number overlapping pixels for each block. Since the filter can be slow, you
  may want to reduce this value, at the cost of a less effective filter and the
  risk of various artefacts.
  .Sp
  If the overlapping value doesn't permit processing the whole input width or
  height, a warning will be displayed and according borders won't be denoised.
  .Sp
  Default value is _blocksize_-1, which is the best possible setting.
* **expr, e**  
  .IX Item "expr, e"
  Set the coefficient factor expression.
  .Sp
  For each coefficient of a \s-1DCT\s0 block, this expression will be evaluated as a
  multiplier value for the coefficient.
  .Sp
  If this is option is set, the **sigma** option will be ignored.
  .Sp
  The absolute value of the coefficient can be accessed through the _c_
  variable.
* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  Set the _blocksize_ using the number of bits. \f(CW`1&lt;&lt;\f(CIn\f(CW\*(C' defines the
  _blocksize_, which is the width and height of the processed blocks.
  .Sp
  The default value is _3_ (8x8) and can be raised to _4_ for a
  _blocksize_ of 16x16. Note that changing this setting has huge consequences
  on the speed processing. Also, a larger block size does not necessarily means a
  better de-noising.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

Apply a denoise with a **sigma** of \f(CW4.5:

.Vb 1
        dctdnoiz=4.5
.Ve

The same operation can be achieved using the expression system:

.Vb 1
        dctdnoiz=e=gte(c, 4.5*3)\*(Aq
.Ve

Violent denoise using a block size of \f(CW`16x16\*(C':

.Vb 1
        dctdnoiz=15:n=4
.Ve

<a name="deband"></a>

### deband

.IX Subsection "deband"
Remove banding artifacts from input video.
It works by replacing banded pixels with average value of referenced pixels.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **1thr**  
  .IX Item "1thr"
* **2thr**  
  .IX Item "2thr"
* **3thr**  
  .IX Item "3thr"
* **4thr**  
  .IX Item "4thr"
  Set banding detection threshold for each plane. Default is 0.02.
  Valid range is 0.00003 to 0.5.
  If difference between current pixel and reference pixel is less than threshold,
  it will be considered as banded.
* **range, r**  
  .IX Item "range, r"
  Banding detection range in pixels. Default is 16. If positive, random number
  in range 0 to set value will be used. If negative, exact absolute value
  will be used.
  The range defines square of four pixels around current pixel.
* **direction, d**  
  .IX Item "direction, d"
  Set direction in radians from which four pixel will be compared. If positive,
  random direction from 0 to set direction will be picked. If negative, exact of
  absolute value will be picked. For example direction 0, -PI or -2*PI radians
  will pick only pixels on same row and -PI/2 will pick only pixels on same
  column.
* **blur, b**  
  .IX Item "blur, b"
  If enabled, current pixel is compared with average value of all four
  surrounding pixels. The default is enabled. If disabled current pixel is
  compared with all four surrounding pixels. The pixel is considered banded
  if only all four differences with surrounding pixels are less than threshold.
* **coupling, c**  
  .IX Item "coupling, c"
  If enabled, current pixel is changed if and only if all pixel components are banded,
  e.g. banding detection threshold is triggered for all color components.
  The default is disabled.

<a name="deblock"></a>

### deblock

.IX Subsection "deblock"
Remove blocking artifacts from input video.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **filter**  
  .IX Item "filter"
  Set filter type, can be _weak_ or _strong_. Default is _strong_.
  This controls what kind of deblocking is applied.
* **block**  
  .IX Item "block"
  Set size of block, allowed range is from 4 to 512. Default is _8_.
* **alpha**  
  .IX Item "alpha"
* **beta**  
  .IX Item "beta"
* **gamma**  
  .IX Item "gamma"
* **delta**  
  .IX Item "delta"
  Set blocking detection thresholds. Allowed range is 0 to 1.
  Defaults are: _0.098_ for _alpha_ and _0.05_ for the rest.
  Using higher threshold gives more deblocking strength.
  Setting _alpha_ controls threshold detection at exact edge of block.
  Remaining options controls threshold detection near the edge. Each one for
  below/above or left/right. Setting any of those to _0_ disables
  deblocking.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set planes to filter. Default is to filter all available planes.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Deblock using weak filter and block size of 4 pixels.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          deblock=filter=weak:block=4
  .Ve
* ·  
  Deblock using strong filter, block size of 4 pixels and custom thresholds for
  deblocking more edges.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          deblock=filter=strong:block=4:alpha=0.12:beta=0.07:gamma=0.06:delta=0.05
  .Ve
* ·  
  Similar as above, but filter only first plane.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          deblock=filter=strong:block=4:alpha=0.12:beta=0.07:gamma=0.06:delta=0.05:planes=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Similar as above, but filter only second and third plane.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          deblock=filter=strong:block=4:alpha=0.12:beta=0.07:gamma=0.06:delta=0.05:planes=6
  .Ve

<a name="decimate"></a>

### decimate

.IX Subsection "decimate"
Drop duplicated frames at regular intervals.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **cycle**  
  .IX Item "cycle"
  Set the number of frames from which one will be dropped. Setting this to
  _N_ means one frame in every batch of _N_ frames will be dropped.
  Default is \f(CW5.
* **dupthresh**  
  .IX Item "dupthresh"
  Set the threshold for duplicate detection. If the difference metric for a frame
  is less than or equal to this value, then it is declared as duplicate. Default
  is \f(CW1.1
* **scthresh**  
  .IX Item "scthresh"
  Set scene change threshold. Default is \f(CW15.
* **blockx**  
  .IX Item "blockx"
* **blocky**  
  .IX Item "blocky"
  Set the size of the x and y-axis blocks used during metric calculations.
  Larger blocks give better noise suppression, but also give worse detection of
  small movements. Must be a power of two. Default is \f(CW32.
* **ppsrc**  
  .IX Item "ppsrc"
  Mark main input as a pre-processed input and activate clean source input
  stream. This allows the input to be pre-processed with various filters to help
  the metrics calculation while keeping the frame selection lossless. When set to
  \f(CW1, the first stream is for the pre-processed input, and the second
  stream is the clean source from where the kept frames are chosen. Default is
  \f(CW0.
* **chroma**  
  .IX Item "chroma"
  Set whether or not chroma is considered in the metric calculations. Default is
  \f(CW1.

<a name="deconvolve"></a>

### deconvolve

.IX Subsection "deconvolve"
Apply 2D deconvolution of video stream in frequency domain using second stream
as impulse.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes to process.
* **impulse**  
  .IX Item "impulse"
  Set which impulse video frames will be processed, can be _first_
  or _all_. Default is _all_.
* **noise**  
  .IX Item "noise"
  Set noise when doing divisions. Default is _0.0000001_. Useful when width
  and height are not same and not power of 2 or if stream prior to convolving
  had noise.

The \f(CW`deconvolve\*(C' filter also supports the **framesync** options.

<a name="deflate"></a>

### deflate

.IX Subsection "deflate"
Apply deflate effect to the video.

This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3x3) average by taking into account
only values lower than the pixel.

It accepts the following options:

* **threshold0**  
  .IX Item "threshold0"
* **threshold1**  
  .IX Item "threshold1"
* **threshold2**  
  .IX Item "threshold2"
* **threshold3**  
  .IX Item "threshold3"
  Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535.
  If 0, plane will remain unchanged.

<a name="deflicker"></a>

### deflicker

.IX Subsection "deflicker"
Remove temporal frame luminance variations.

It accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set moving-average filter size in frames. Default is 5. Allowed range is 2 - 129.
* **mode, m**  
  .IX Item "mode, m"
  Set averaging mode to smooth temporal luminance variations.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **am**  
      .IX Item "am"
      Arithmetic mean
    * **gm**  
      .IX Item "gm"
      Geometric mean
    * **hm**  
      .IX Item "hm"
      Harmonic mean
    * **qm**  
      .IX Item "qm"
      Quadratic mean
    * **cm**  
      .IX Item "cm"
      Cubic mean
    * **pm**  
      .IX Item "pm"
      Power mean
    * **median**  
      .IX Item "median"
      Median
* **bypass**  
  .IX Item "bypass"
  Do not actually modify frame. Useful when one only wants metadata.

<a name="dejudder"></a>

### dejudder

.IX Subsection "dejudder"
Remove judder produced by partially interlaced telecined content.

Judder can be introduced, for instance, by **pullup** filter. If the original
source was partially telecined content then the output of \f(CW`pullup,dejudder\*(C'
will have a variable frame rate. May change the recorded frame rate of the
container. Aside from that change, this filter will not affect constant frame
rate video.

The option available in this filter is:

* **cycle**  
  .IX Item "cycle"
  Specify the length of the window over which the judder repeats.
  .Sp
  Accepts any integer greater than 1. Useful values are:
    * **4**  
      .IX Item "4"
      If the original was telecined from 24 to 30 fps (Film to \s-1NTSC\s0).
    * **5**  
      .IX Item "5"
      If the original was telecined from 25 to 30 fps (\s-1PAL\s0 to \s-1NTSC\s0).
    * **20**  
      .IX Item "20"
      If a mixture of the two.
      .Sp
      The default is **4**.

<a name="delogo"></a>

### delogo

.IX Subsection "delogo"
Suppress a \s-1TV\s0 station logo by a simple interpolation of the surrounding
pixels. Just set a rectangle covering the logo and watch it disappear
(and sometimes something even uglier appear - your mileage may vary).

It accepts the following parameters:

* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  Specify the top left corner coordinates of the logo. They must be
  specified.
* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  Specify the width and height of the logo to clear. They must be
  specified.
* **band, t**  
  .IX Item "band, t"
  Specify the thickness of the fuzzy edge of the rectangle (added to
  _w_ and _h_). The default value is 1. This option is
  deprecated, setting higher values should no longer be necessary and
  is not recommended.
* **show**  
  .IX Item "show"
  When set to 1, a green rectangle is drawn on the screen to simplify
  finding the right _x_, _y_, _w_, and _h_ parameters.
  The default value is 0.
  .Sp
  The rectangle is drawn on the outermost pixels which will be (partly)
  replaced with interpolated values. The values of the next pixels
  immediately outside this rectangle in each direction will be used to
  compute the interpolated pixel values inside the rectangle.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Set a rectangle covering the area with top left corner coordinates 0,0
  and size 100x77, and a band of size 10:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          delogo=x=0:y=0:w=100:h=77:band=10
  .Ve

<a name="deshake"></a>

### deshake

.IX Subsection "deshake"
Attempt to fix small changes in horizontal and/or vertical shift. This
filter helps remove camera shake from hand-holding a camera, bumping a
tripod, moving on a vehicle, etc.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  Specify a rectangular area where to limit the search for motion
  vectors.
  If desired the search for motion vectors can be limited to a
  rectangular area of the frame defined by its top left corner, width
  and height. These parameters have the same meaning as the drawbox
  filter which can be used to visualise the position of the bounding
  box.
  .Sp
  This is useful when simultaneous movement of subjects within the frame
  might be confused for camera motion by the motion vector search.
  .Sp
  If any or all of _x_, _y_, _w_ and _h_ are set to -1
  then the full frame is used. This allows later options to be set
  without specifying the bounding box for the motion vector search.
  .Sp
  Default - search the whole frame.
* **rx**  
  .IX Item "rx"
* **ry**  
  .IX Item "ry"
  Specify the maximum extent of movement in x and y directions in the
  range 0-64 pixels. Default 16.
* **edge**  
  .IX Item "edge"
  Specify how to generate pixels to fill blanks at the edge of the
  frame. Available values are:
    * **blank, 0**  
      .IX Item "blank, 0"
      Fill zeroes at blank locations
    * **original, 1**  
      .IX Item "original, 1"
      Original image at blank locations
    * **clamp, 2**  
      .IX Item "clamp, 2"
      Extruded edge value at blank locations
    * **mirror, 3**  
      .IX Item "mirror, 3"
      Mirrored edge at blank locations
      .Sp
      Default value is **mirror**.
* **blocksize**  
  .IX Item "blocksize"
  Specify the blocksize to use for motion search. Range 4-128 pixels,
  default 8.
* **contrast**  
  .IX Item "contrast"
  Specify the contrast threshold for blocks. Only blocks with more than
  the specified contrast (difference between darkest and lightest
  pixels) will be considered. Range 1-255, default 125.
* **search**  
  .IX Item "search"
  Specify the search strategy. Available values are:
    * **exhaustive, 0**  
      .IX Item "exhaustive, 0"
      Set exhaustive search
    * **less, 1**  
      .IX Item "less, 1"
      Set less exhaustive search.
      .Sp
      Default value is **exhaustive**.
* **filename**  
  .IX Item "filename"
  If set then a detailed log of the motion search is written to the
  specified file.

<a name="despill"></a>

### despill

.IX Subsection "despill"
Remove unwanted contamination of foreground colors, caused by reflected color of
greenscreen or bluescreen.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **type**  
  .IX Item "type"
  Set what type of despill to use.
* **mix**  
  .IX Item "mix"
  Set how spillmap will be generated.
* **expand**  
  .IX Item "expand"
  Set how much to get rid of still remaining spill.
* **red**  
  .IX Item "red"
  Controls amount of red in spill area.
* **green**  
  .IX Item "green"
  Controls amount of green in spill area.
  Should be -1 for greenscreen.
* **blue**  
  .IX Item "blue"
  Controls amount of blue in spill area.
  Should be -1 for bluescreen.
* **brightness**  
  .IX Item "brightness"
  Controls brightness of spill area, preserving colors.
* **alpha**  
  .IX Item "alpha"
  Modify alpha from generated spillmap.

<a name="detelecine"></a>

### detelecine

.IX Subsection "detelecine"
Apply an exact inverse of the telecine operation. It requires a predefined
pattern specified using the pattern option which must be the same as that passed
to the telecine filter.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **first\_field**  
  .IX Item "first_field"
    * **top, t**  
      .IX Item "top, t"
      top field first
    * **bottom, b**  
      .IX Item "bottom, b"
      bottom field first
      The default value is \f(CW`top\*(C'.
* **pattern**  
  .IX Item "pattern"
  A string of numbers representing the pulldown pattern you wish to apply.
  The default value is \f(CW23.
* **start\_frame**  
  .IX Item "start_frame"
  A number representing position of the first frame with respect to the telecine
  pattern. This is to be used if the stream is cut. The default value is \f(CW0.

<a name="dilation"></a>

### dilation

.IX Subsection "dilation"
Apply dilation effect to the video.

This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3x3) maximum.

It accepts the following options:

* **threshold0**  
  .IX Item "threshold0"
* **threshold1**  
  .IX Item "threshold1"
* **threshold2**  
  .IX Item "threshold2"
* **threshold3**  
  .IX Item "threshold3"
  Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535.
  If 0, plane will remain unchanged.
* **coordinates**  
  .IX Item "coordinates"
  Flag which specifies the pixel to refer to. Default is 255 i.e. all eight
  pixels are used.
  .Sp
  Flags to local 3x3 coordinates maps like this:
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
      1 2 3
      4   5
      6 7 8
  .Ve

<a name="displace"></a>

### displace

.IX Subsection "displace"
Displace pixels as indicated by second and third input stream.

It takes three input streams and outputs one stream, the first input is the
source, and second and third input are displacement maps.

The second input specifies how much to displace pixels along the
x-axis, while the third input specifies how much to displace pixels
along the y-axis.
If one of displacement map streams terminates, last frame from that
displacement map will be used.

Note that once generated, displacements maps can be reused over and over again.

A description of the accepted options follows.

* **edge**  
  .IX Item "edge"
  Set displace behavior for pixels that are out of range.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **blank**  
      .IX Item "blank"
      Missing pixels are replaced by black pixels.
    * **smear**  
      .IX Item "smear"
      Adjacent pixels will spread out to replace missing pixels.
    * **wrap**  
      .IX Item "wrap"
      Out of range pixels are wrapped so they point to pixels of other side.
    * **mirror**  
      .IX Item "mirror"
      Out of range pixels will be replaced with mirrored pixels.
      .Sp
      Default is **smear**.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Add ripple effect to rgb input of video size hd720:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -f lavfi -i nullsrc=s=hd720,lutrgb=128:128:128 -f lavfi -i nullsrc=s=hd720,geq=r=128+30*sin(2*PI*X/400+T):g=128+30*sin(2*PI*X/400+T):b=128+30*sin(2*PI*X/400+T)\*(Aq -lavfi \*(Aq[0][1][2]displace\*(Aq OUTPUT
  .Ve
* ·  
  Add wave effect to rgb input of video size hd720:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -f lavfi -i nullsrc=hd720,geq=r=128+80*(sin(sqrt((X-W/2)*(X-W/2)+(Y-H/2)*(Y-H/2))/220*2*PI+T)):g=128+80*(sin(sqrt((X-W/2)*(X-W/2)+(Y-H/2)*(Y-H/2))/220*2*PI+T)):b=128+80*(sin(sqrt((X-W/2)*(X-W/2)+(Y-H/2)*(Y-H/2))/220*2*PI+T))\*(Aq -lavfi \*(Aq[1]split[x][y],[0][x][y]displace\*(Aq OUTPUT
  .Ve

<a name="drawbox"></a>

### drawbox

.IX Subsection "drawbox"
Draw a colored box on the input image.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  The expressions which specify the top left corner coordinates of the box. It defaults to 0.
* **width, w**  
  .IX Item "width, w"
* **height, h**  
  .IX Item "height, h"
  The expressions which specify the width and height of the box; if 0 they are interpreted as
  the input width and height. It defaults to 0.
* **color, c**  
  .IX Item "color, c"
  Specify the color of the box to write. For the general syntax of this option,
  check the **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**. If the special
  value \f(CW`invert\*(C' is used, the box edge color is the same as the
  video with inverted luma.
* **thickness, t**  
  .IX Item "thickness, t"
  The expression which sets the thickness of the box edge.
  A value of \f(CW`fill\*(C' will create a filled box. Default value is \f(CW3.
  .Sp
  See below for the list of accepted constants.
* **replace**  
  .IX Item "replace"
  Applicable if the input has alpha. With value \f(CW1, the pixels of the painted box
  will overwrite the video's color and alpha pixels.
  Default is \f(CW0, which composites the box onto the input, leaving the video's alpha intact.

The parameters for _x_, _y_, _w_ and _h_ and _t_ are expressions containing the
following constants:

* **dar**  
  .IX Item "dar"
  The input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (_w_ / _h_) * _sar_.
* **hsub**  
  .IX Item "hsub"
* **vsub**  
  .IX Item "vsub"
  horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the
  pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_ is 1.
* **in_h, ih**  
  .IX Item "in_h, ih"
* **in_w, iw**  
  .IX Item "in_w, iw"
  The input width and height.
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  The input sample aspect ratio.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  The x and y offset coordinates where the box is drawn.
* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  The width and height of the drawn box.
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  The thickness of the drawn box.
  .Sp
  These constants allow the _x_, _y_, _w_, _h_ and _t_ expressions to refer to
  each other, so you may for example specify \f(CW`y=x/dar\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`h=w/dar\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Draw a black box around the edge of the input image:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawbox
  .Ve
* ·  
  Draw a box with color red and an opacity of 50%:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawbox=10:20:200:60:red@0.5
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The previous example can be specified as:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawbox=x=10:y=20:w=200:h=60:color=red@0.5
  .Ve
* ·  
  Fill the box with pink color:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawbox=x=10:y=10:w=100:h=100:color=pink@0.5:t=fill
  .Ve
* ·  
  Draw a 2-pixel red 2.40:1 mask:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawbox=x=-t:y=0.5*(ih-iw/2.4)-t:w=iw+t*2:h=iw/2.4+t*2:t=2:c=red
  .Ve

<a name="drawgrid"></a>

### drawgrid

.IX Subsection "drawgrid"
Draw a grid on the input image.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  The expressions which specify the coordinates of some point of grid intersection (meant to configure offset). Both default to 0.
* **width, w**  
  .IX Item "width, w"
* **height, h**  
  .IX Item "height, h"
  The expressions which specify the width and height of the grid cell, if 0 they are interpreted as the
  input width and height, respectively, minus \f(CW`thickness\*(C', so image gets
  framed. Default to 0.
* **color, c**  
  .IX Item "color, c"
  Specify the color of the grid. For the general syntax of this option,
  check the **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**. If the special
  value \f(CW`invert\*(C' is used, the grid color is the same as the
  video with inverted luma.
* **thickness, t**  
  .IX Item "thickness, t"
  The expression which sets the thickness of the grid line. Default value is \f(CW1.
  .Sp
  See below for the list of accepted constants.
* **replace**  
  .IX Item "replace"
  Applicable if the input has alpha. With \f(CW1 the pixels of the painted grid
  will overwrite the video's color and alpha pixels.
  Default is \f(CW0, which composites the grid onto the input, leaving the video's alpha intact.

The parameters for _x_, _y_, _w_ and _h_ and _t_ are expressions containing the
following constants:

* **dar**  
  .IX Item "dar"
  The input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (_w_ / _h_) * _sar_.
* **hsub**  
  .IX Item "hsub"
* **vsub**  
  .IX Item "vsub"
  horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the
  pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_ is 1.
* **in_h, ih**  
  .IX Item "in_h, ih"
* **in_w, iw**  
  .IX Item "in_w, iw"
  The input grid cell width and height.
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  The input sample aspect ratio.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  The x and y coordinates of some point of grid intersection (meant to configure offset).
* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  The width and height of the drawn cell.
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  The thickness of the drawn cell.
  .Sp
  These constants allow the _x_, _y_, _w_, _h_ and _t_ expressions to refer to
  each other, so you may for example specify \f(CW`y=x/dar\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`h=w/dar\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Draw a grid with cell 100x100 pixels, thickness 2 pixels, with color red and an opacity of 50%:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawgrid=width=100:height=100:thickness=2:color=red@0.5
  .Ve
* ·  
  Draw a white 3x3 grid with an opacity of 50%:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawgrid=w=iw/3:h=ih/3:t=2:c=white@0.5
  .Ve

<a name="drawtext"></a>

### drawtext

.IX Subsection "drawtext"
Draw a text string or text from a specified file on top of a video, using the
libfreetype library.

To enable compilation of this filter, you need to configure FFmpeg with
\f(CW`--enable-libfreetype\*(C'.
To enable default font fallback and the _font_ option you need to
configure FFmpeg with \f(CW`--enable-libfontconfig\*(C'.
To enable the _text\_shaping_ option, you need to configure FFmpeg with
\f(CW`--enable-libfribidi\*(C'.

_Syntax_
.IX Subsection "Syntax"

It accepts the following parameters:

* **box**  
  .IX Item "box"
  Used to draw a box around text using the background color.
  The value must be either 1 (enable) or 0 (disable).
  The default value of _box_ is 0.
* **boxborderw**  
  .IX Item "boxborderw"
  Set the width of the border to be drawn around the box using _boxcolor_.
  The default value of _boxborderw_ is 0.
* **boxcolor**  
  .IX Item "boxcolor"
  The color to be used for drawing box around text. For the syntax of this
  option, check the **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  .Sp
  The default value of _boxcolor_ is white\*(R".
* **line\_spacing**  
  .IX Item "line_spacing"
  Set the line spacing in pixels of the border to be drawn around the box using _box_.
  The default value of _line\_spacing_ is 0.
* **borderw**  
  .IX Item "borderw"
  Set the width of the border to be drawn around the text using _bordercolor_.
  The default value of _borderw_ is 0.
* **bordercolor**  
  .IX Item "bordercolor"
  Set the color to be used for drawing border around text. For the syntax of this
  option, check the **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  .Sp
  The default value of _bordercolor_ is black\*(R".
* **expansion**  
  .IX Item "expansion"
  Select how the _text_ is expanded. Can be either \f(CW`none\*(C',
  \f(CW`strftime\*(C' (deprecated) or
  \f(CW`normal\*(C' (default). See the **drawtext_expansion, Text expansion** section
  below for details.
* **basetime**  
  .IX Item "basetime"
  Set a start time for the count. Value is in microseconds. Only applied
  in the deprecated strftime expansion mode. To emulate in normal expansion
  mode use the \f(CW`pts\*(C' function, supplying the start time (in seconds)
  as the second argument.
* **fix\_bounds**  
  .IX Item "fix_bounds"
  If true, check and fix text coords to avoid clipping.
* **fontcolor**  
  .IX Item "fontcolor"
  The color to be used for drawing fonts. For the syntax of this option, check
  the **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  .Sp
  The default value of _fontcolor_ is black\*(R".
* **fontcolor\_expr**  
  .IX Item "fontcolor_expr"
  String which is expanded the same way as _text_ to obtain dynamic
  _fontcolor_ value. By default this option has empty value and is not
  processed. When this option is set, it overrides _fontcolor_ option.
* **font**  
  .IX Item "font"
  The font family to be used for drawing text. By default Sans.
* **fontfile**  
  .IX Item "fontfile"
  The font file to be used for drawing text. The path must be included.
  This parameter is mandatory if the fontconfig support is disabled.
* **alpha**  
  .IX Item "alpha"
  Draw the text applying alpha blending. The value can
  be a number between 0.0 and 1.0.
  The expression accepts the same variables _x, y_ as well.
  The default value is 1.
  Please see _fontcolor\_expr_.
* **fontsize**  
  .IX Item "fontsize"
  The font size to be used for drawing text.
  The default value of _fontsize_ is 16.
* **text\_shaping**  
  .IX Item "text_shaping"
  If set to 1, attempt to shape the text (for example, reverse the order of
  right-to-left text and join Arabic characters) before drawing it.
  Otherwise, just draw the text exactly as given.
  By default 1 (if supported).
* **ft\_load\_flags**  
  .IX Item "ft_load_flags"
  The flags to be used for loading the fonts.
  .Sp
  The flags map the corresponding flags supported by libfreetype, and are
  a combination of the following values:
    * _default_  
      .IX Item "default"
    * _no\_scale_  
      .IX Item "no_scale"
    * _no\_hinting_  
      .IX Item "no_hinting"
    * _render_  
      .IX Item "render"
    * _no\_bitmap_  
      .IX Item "no_bitmap"
    * _vertical\_layout_  
      .IX Item "vertical_layout"
    * _force\_autohint_  
      .IX Item "force_autohint"
    * _crop\_bitmap_  
      .IX Item "crop_bitmap"
    * _pedantic_  
      .IX Item "pedantic"
    * _ignore\_global\_advance\_width_  
      .IX Item "ignore_global_advance_width"
    * _no\_recurse_  
      .IX Item "no_recurse"
    * _ignore\_transform_  
      .IX Item "ignore_transform"
    * _monochrome_  
      .IX Item "monochrome"
    * _linear\_design_  
      .IX Item "linear_design"
    * _no\_autohint_  
      .IX Item "no_autohint"
      .Sp
      Default value is default\*(R".
      .Sp
      For more information consult the documentation for the FT_LOAD_*
      libfreetype flags.
* **shadowcolor**  
  .IX Item "shadowcolor"
  The color to be used for drawing a shadow behind the drawn text. For the
  syntax of this option, check the Color\*(R" section in the
  ffmpeg-utils manual.
  .Sp
  The default value of _shadowcolor_ is black\*(R".
* **shadowx**  
  .IX Item "shadowx"
* **shadowy**  
  .IX Item "shadowy"
  The x and y offsets for the text shadow position with respect to the
  position of the text. They can be either positive or negative
  values. The default value for both is 0\*(R".
* **start\_number**  
  .IX Item "start_number"
  The starting frame number for the n/frame_num variable. The default value
  is 0\*(R".
* **tabsize**  
  .IX Item "tabsize"
  The size in number of spaces to use for rendering the tab.
  Default value is 4.
* **timecode**  
  .IX Item "timecode"
  Set the initial timecode representation in hh:mm:ss[:;.]ff\*(R"
  format. It can be used with or without text parameter. _timecode\_rate_
  option must be specified.
* **timecode_rate, rate, r**  
  .IX Item "timecode_rate, rate, r"
  Set the timecode frame rate (timecode only). Value will be rounded to nearest
  integer. Minimum value is 1\*(R".
  Drop-frame timecode is supported for frame rates 30 & 60.
* **tc24hmax**  
  .IX Item "tc24hmax"
  If set to 1, the output of the timecode option will wrap around at 24 hours.
  Default is 0 (disabled).
* **text**  
  .IX Item "text"
  The text string to be drawn. The text must be a sequence of \s-1UTF-8\s0
  encoded characters.
  This parameter is mandatory if no file is specified with the parameter
  _textfile_.
* **textfile**  
  .IX Item "textfile"
  A text file containing text to be drawn. The text must be a sequence
  of \s-1UTF-8\s0 encoded characters.
  .Sp
  This parameter is mandatory if no text string is specified with the
  parameter _text_.
  .Sp
  If both _text_ and _textfile_ are specified, an error is thrown.
* **reload**  
  .IX Item "reload"
  If set to 1, the _textfile_ will be reloaded before each frame.
  Be sure to update it atomically, or it may be read partially, or even fail.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  The expressions which specify the offsets where text will be drawn
  within the video frame. They are relative to the top/left border of the
  output image.
  .Sp
  The default value of _x_ and _y_ is 0\*(R".
  .Sp
  See below for the list of accepted constants and functions.

The parameters for _x_ and _y_ are expressions containing the
following constants and functions:

* **dar**  
  .IX Item "dar"
  input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (_w_ / _h_) * _sar_
* **hsub**  
  .IX Item "hsub"
* **vsub**  
  .IX Item "vsub"
  horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example for the
  pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_ is 1.
* **line_h, lh**  
  .IX Item "line_h, lh"
  the height of each text line
* **main_h, h, H**  
  .IX Item "main_h, h, H"
  the input height
* **main_w, w, W**  
  .IX Item "main_w, w, W"
  the input width
* **max_glyph_a, ascent**  
  .IX Item "max_glyph_a, ascent"
  the maximum distance from the baseline to the highest/upper grid
  coordinate used to place a glyph outline point, for all the rendered
  glyphs.
  It is a positive value, due to the grid's orientation with the Y axis
  upwards.
* **max_glyph_d, descent**  
  .IX Item "max_glyph_d, descent"
  the maximum distance from the baseline to the lowest grid coordinate
  used to place a glyph outline point, for all the rendered glyphs.
  This is a negative value, due to the grid's orientation, with the Y axis
  upwards.
* **max\_glyph\_h**  
  .IX Item "max_glyph_h"
  maximum glyph height, that is the maximum height for all the glyphs
  contained in the rendered text, it is equivalent to _ascent_ -
  _descent_.
* **max\_glyph\_w**  
  .IX Item "max_glyph_w"
  maximum glyph width, that is the maximum width for all the glyphs
  contained in the rendered text
* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  the number of input frame, starting from 0
* **rand(min, max)**  
  .IX Item "rand(min, max)"
  return a random number included between _min_ and _max_
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  The input sample aspect ratio.
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  timestamp expressed in seconds, \s-1NAN\s0 if the input timestamp is unknown
* **text_h, th**  
  .IX Item "text_h, th"
  the height of the rendered text
* **text_w, tw**  
  .IX Item "text_w, tw"
  the width of the rendered text
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  the x and y offset coordinates where the text is drawn.
  .Sp
  These parameters allow the _x_ and _y_ expressions to refer
  each other, so you can for example specify \f(CW`y=x/dar\*(C'.

_Text expansion_
.IX Subsection "Text expansion"

If **expansion** is set to \f(CW`strftime\*(C',
the filter recognizes **strftime()** sequences in the provided text and
expands them accordingly. Check the documentation of **strftime()**. This
feature is deprecated.

If **expansion** is set to \f(CW`none\*(C', the text is printed verbatim.

If **expansion** is set to \f(CW`normal\*(C' (which is the default),
the following expansion mechanism is used.

The backslash character **\e**, followed by any character, always expands to
the second character.

Sequences of the form \f(CW`%{...}\*(C' are expanded. The text between the
braces is a function name, possibly followed by arguments separated by ':'.
If the arguments contain special characters or delimiters (':' or '}'),
they should be escaped.

Note that they probably must also be escaped as the value for the
**text** option in the filter argument string and as the filter
argument in the filtergraph description, and possibly also for the shell,
that makes up to four levels of escaping; using a text file avoids these
problems.

The following functions are available:

* **expr, e**  
  .IX Item "expr, e"
  The expression evaluation result.
  .Sp
  It must take one argument specifying the expression to be evaluated,
  which accepts the same constants and functions as the _x_ and
  _y_ values. Note that not all constants should be used, for
  example the text size is not known when evaluating the expression, so
  the constants _text\_w_ and _text\_h_ will have an undefined
  value.
* **expr_int_format, eif**  
  .IX Item "expr_int_format, eif"
  Evaluate the expression's value and output as formatted integer.
  .Sp
  The first argument is the expression to be evaluated, just as for the _expr_ function.
  The second argument specifies the output format. Allowed values are **x**,
  **X**, **d** and **u**. They are treated exactly as in the
  \f(CW`printf\*(C' function.
  The third parameter is optional and sets the number of positions taken by the output.
  It can be used to add padding with zeros from the left.
* **gmtime**  
  .IX Item "gmtime"
  The time at which the filter is running, expressed in \s-1UTC.\s0
  It can accept an argument: a **strftime()** format string.
* **localtime**  
  .IX Item "localtime"
  The time at which the filter is running, expressed in the local time zone.
  It can accept an argument: a **strftime()** format string.
* **metadata**  
  .IX Item "metadata"
  Frame metadata. Takes one or two arguments.
  .Sp
  The first argument is mandatory and specifies the metadata key.
  .Sp
  The second argument is optional and specifies a default value, used when the
  metadata key is not found or empty.
* **n, frame\_num**  
  .IX Item "n, frame_num"
  The frame number, starting from 0.
* **pict\_type**  
  .IX Item "pict_type"
  A 1 character description of the current picture type.
* **pts**  
  .IX Item "pts"
  The timestamp of the current frame.
  It can take up to three arguments.
  .Sp
  The first argument is the format of the timestamp; it defaults to \f(CW`flt\*(C'
  for seconds as a decimal number with microsecond accuracy; \f(CW`hms\*(C' stands
  for a formatted _[-]HH:MM:SS.mmm_ timestamp with millisecond accuracy.
  \f(CW`gmtime\*(C' stands for the timestamp of the frame formatted as \s-1UTC\s0 time;
  \f(CW`localtime\*(C' stands for the timestamp of the frame formatted as
  local time zone time.
  .Sp
  The second argument is an offset added to the timestamp.
  .Sp
  If the format is set to \f(CW`hms\*(C', a third argument \f(CW\*(C\`24HH\*(C' may be
  supplied to present the hour part of the formatted timestamp in 24h format
  (00-23).
  .Sp
  If the format is set to \f(CW`localtime\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`gmtime\*(C',
  a third argument may be supplied: a **strftime()** format string.
  By default, _YYYY-MM-DD \s-1HH:MM:SS\s0_ format will be used.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Draw Test Text\*(R" with font FreeSerif, using the default values for the
  optional parameters.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontfile=/usr/share/fonts/truetype/freefont/FreeSerif.ttf: text=Test Text\*(Aq"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Draw 'Test Text' with font FreeSerif of size 24 at position x=100
  and y=50 (counting from the top-left corner of the screen), text is
  yellow with a red box around it. Both the text and the box have an
  opacity of 20%.
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          drawtext="fontfile=/usr/share/fonts/truetype/freefont/FreeSerif.ttf: text=Test Text\*(Aq:\e
                    x=100: y=50: fontsize=24: fontcolor=yellow@0.2: box=1: boxcolor=red@0.2"
  .Ve
  .Sp
  Note that the double quotes are not necessary if spaces are not used
  within the parameter list.
* ·  
  Show the text at the center of the video frame:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontsize=30:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=hello world\*(Aq:x=(w-text_w)/2:y=(h-text_h)/2"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Show the text at a random position, switching to a new position every 30 seconds:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontsize=30:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=hello world\*(Aq:x=if(eq(mod(t\e,30)\e,0)\e,rand(0\e,(w-text_w))\e,x):y=if(eq(mod(t\e,30)\e,0)\e,rand(0\e,(h-text_h))\e,y)"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Show a text line sliding from right to left in the last row of the video
  frame. The file _\s-1LONG\_LINE\s0_ is assumed to contain a single line
  with no newlines.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontsize=15:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=LONG_LINE:y=h-line_h:x=-50*t"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Show the content of file _\s-1CREDITS\s0_ off the bottom of the frame and scroll up.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontsize=20:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:textfile=CREDITS:y=h-20*t"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Draw a single green letter g\*(R", at the center of the input video.
  The glyph baseline is placed at half screen height.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontsize=60:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:fontcolor=green:text=g:x=(w-max_glyph_w)/2:y=h/2-ascent"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Show text for 1 second every 3 seconds:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext="fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:fontcolor=white:x=100:y=x/dar:enable=lt(mod(t\e,3)\e,1):text=blink\*(Aq"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Use fontconfig to set the font. Note that the colons need to be escaped.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext=fontfile=Linux Libertine O-40\e:style=Semibold:text=FFmpeg\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Print the date of a real-time encoding (see **strftime**\|(3)):
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          drawtext=fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text=%{localtime\e:%a %b %d %Y}\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Show text fading in and out (appearing/disappearing):
  .Sp
  .Vb 6
          #!/bin/sh
          DS=1.0 # display start
          DE=10.0 # display end
          FID=1.5 # fade in duration
          FOD=5 # fade out duration
          ffplay -f lavfi "color,drawtext=text=TEST:fontsize=50:fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:fontcolor_expr=ff0000%{eif\e\e\e\e: clip(255*(1*between(t\e\e, $DS + $FID\e\e, $DE - $FOD) + ((t - $DS)/$FID)*between(t\e\e, $DS\e\e, $DS + $FID) + (-(t - $DE)/$FOD)*between(t\e\e, $DE - $FOD\e\e, $DE) )\e\e, 0\e\e, 255) \e\e\e\e: x\e\e\e\e: 2 }"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Horizontally align multiple separate texts. Note that **max\_glyph\_a**
  and the **fontsize** value are included in the **y** offset.
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          drawtext=fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text=DOG:fontsize=24:x=10:y=20+24-max_glyph_a,
          drawtext=fontfile=FreeSans.ttf:text=cow:fontsize=24:x=80:y=20+24-max_glyph_a
  .Ve

For more information about libfreetype, check:
&lt;**http://www.freetype.org/**&gt;.

For more information about fontconfig, check:
&lt;**http://freedesktop.org/software/fontconfig/fontconfig-user.html**&gt;.

For more information about libfribidi, check:
&lt;**http://fribidi.org/**&gt;.

<a name="edgedetect"></a>

### edgedetect

.IX Subsection "edgedetect"
Detect and draw edges. The filter uses the Canny Edge Detection algorithm.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **low**  
  .IX Item "low"
* **high**  
  .IX Item "high"
  Set low and high threshold values used by the Canny thresholding
  algorithm.
  .Sp
  The high threshold selects the strong\*(R" edge pixels, which are then
  connected through 8-connectivity with the weak\*(R" edge pixels selected
  by the low threshold.
  .Sp
  _low_ and _high_ threshold values must be chosen in the range
  [0,1], and _low_ should be lesser or equal to _high_.
  .Sp
  Default value for _low_ is \f(CW`20/255\*(C', and default value for _high_
  is \f(CW`50/255\*(C'.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Define the drawing mode.
    * **wires**  
      .IX Item "wires"
      Draw white/gray wires on black background.
    * **colormix**  
      .IX Item "colormix"
      Mix the colors to create a paint/cartoon effect.
    * **canny**  
      .IX Item "canny"
      Apply Canny edge detector on all selected planes.
      .Sp
      Default value is _wires_.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Select planes for filtering. By default all available planes are filtered.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Standard edge detection with custom values for the hysteresis thresholding:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          edgedetect=low=0.1:high=0.4
  .Ve
* ·  
  Painting effect without thresholding:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          edgedetect=mode=colormix:high=0
  .Ve

<a name="eq"></a>

### eq

.IX Subsection "eq"
Set brightness, contrast, saturation and approximate gamma adjustment.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **contrast**  
  .IX Item "contrast"
  Set the contrast expression. The value must be a float value in range
  \f(CW`-2.0\*(C' to \f(CW2.0. The default value is \*(L"1\*(R".
* **brightness**  
  .IX Item "brightness"
  Set the brightness expression. The value must be a float value in
  range \f(CW`-1.0\*(C' to \f(CW1.0. The default value is \*(L"0\*(R".
* **saturation**  
  .IX Item "saturation"
  Set the saturation expression. The value must be a float in
  range \f(CW0.0 to \f(CW3.0. The default value is 1\*(R".
* **gamma**  
  .IX Item "gamma"
  Set the gamma expression. The value must be a float in range
  \f(CW0.1 to \f(CW10.0.  The default value is 1\*(R".
* **gamma\_r**  
  .IX Item "gamma_r"
  Set the gamma expression for red. The value must be a float in
  range \f(CW0.1 to \f(CW10.0. The default value is 1\*(R".
* **gamma\_g**  
  .IX Item "gamma_g"
  Set the gamma expression for green. The value must be a float in range
  \f(CW0.1 to \f(CW10.0. The default value is 1\*(R".
* **gamma\_b**  
  .IX Item "gamma_b"
  Set the gamma expression for blue. The value must be a float in range
  \f(CW0.1 to \f(CW10.0. The default value is 1\*(R".
* **gamma\_weight**  
  .IX Item "gamma_weight"
  Set the gamma weight expression. It can be used to reduce the effect
  of a high gamma value on bright image areas, e.g. keep them from
  getting overamplified and just plain white. The value must be a float
  in range \f(CW0.0 to \f(CW1.0. A value of \f(CW0.0 turns the
  gamma correction all the way down while \f(CW1.0 leaves it at its
  full strength. Default is 1\*(R".
* **eval**  
  .IX Item "eval"
  Set when the expressions for brightness, contrast, saturation and
  gamma expressions are evaluated.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **init**  
      .IX Item "init"
      only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization or
      when a command is processed
    * **frame**  
      .IX Item "frame"
      evaluate expressions for each incoming frame
      .Sp
      Default value is **init**.

The expressions accept the following parameters:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  frame count of the input frame starting from 0
* **pos**  
  .IX Item "pos"
  byte position of the corresponding packet in the input file, \s-1NAN\s0 if
  unspecified
* **r**  
  .IX Item "r"
  frame rate of the input video, \s-1NAN\s0 if the input frame rate is unknown
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  timestamp expressed in seconds, \s-1NAN\s0 if the input timestamp is unknown

_Commands_
.IX Subsection "Commands"

The filter supports the following commands:

* **contrast**  
  .IX Item "contrast"
  Set the contrast expression.
* **brightness**  
  .IX Item "brightness"
  Set the brightness expression.
* **saturation**  
  .IX Item "saturation"
  Set the saturation expression.
* **gamma**  
  .IX Item "gamma"
  Set the gamma expression.
* **gamma\_r**  
  .IX Item "gamma_r"
  Set the gamma_r expression.
* **gamma\_g**  
  .IX Item "gamma_g"
  Set gamma_g expression.
* **gamma\_b**  
  .IX Item "gamma_b"
  Set gamma_b expression.
* **gamma\_weight**  
  .IX Item "gamma_weight"
  Set gamma_weight expression.
  .Sp
  The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option.
  .Sp
  If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current
  value.

<a name="erosion"></a>

### erosion

.IX Subsection "erosion"
Apply erosion effect to the video.

This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3x3) minimum.

It accepts the following options:

* **threshold0**  
  .IX Item "threshold0"
* **threshold1**  
  .IX Item "threshold1"
* **threshold2**  
  .IX Item "threshold2"
* **threshold3**  
  .IX Item "threshold3"
  Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535.
  If 0, plane will remain unchanged.
* **coordinates**  
  .IX Item "coordinates"
  Flag which specifies the pixel to refer to. Default is 255 i.e. all eight
  pixels are used.
  .Sp
  Flags to local 3x3 coordinates maps like this:
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
      1 2 3
      4   5
      6 7 8
  .Ve

<a name="extractplanes"></a>

### extractplanes

.IX Subsection "extractplanes"
Extract color channel components from input video stream into
separate grayscale video streams.

The filter accepts the following option:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set plane(s) to extract.
  .Sp
  Available values for planes are:
    * **y**  
      .IX Item "y"
    * **u**  
      .IX Item "u"
    * **v**  
      .IX Item "v"
    * **a**  
      .IX Item "a"
    * **r**  
      .IX Item "r"
    * **g**  
      .IX Item "g"
    * **b**  
      .IX Item "b"
      .Sp
      Choosing planes not available in the input will result in an error.
      That means you cannot select \f(CW`r\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`g\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`b\*(C' planes
      with \f(CW`y\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`u\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`v\*(C' planes at same time.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Extract luma, u and v color channel component from input video frame
  into 3 grayscale outputs:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i video.avi -filter_complex extractplanes=y+u+v[y][u][v]\*(Aq -map \*(Aq[y]\*(Aq y.avi -map \*(Aq[u]\*(Aq u.avi -map \*(Aq[v]\*(Aq v.avi
  .Ve

<a name="elbg"></a>

### elbg

.IX Subsection "elbg"
Apply a posterize effect using the \s-1ELBG\s0 (Enhanced \s-1LBG\s0) algorithm.

For each input image, the filter will compute the optimal mapping from
the input to the output given the codebook length, that is the number
of distinct output colors.

This filter accepts the following options.

* **codebook_length, l**  
  .IX Item "codebook_length, l"
  Set codebook length. The value must be a positive integer, and
  represents the number of distinct output colors. Default value is 256.
* **nb_steps, n**  
  .IX Item "nb_steps, n"
  Set the maximum number of iterations to apply for computing the optimal
  mapping. The higher the value the better the result and the higher the
  computation time. Default value is 1.
* **seed, s**  
  .IX Item "seed, s"
  Set a random seed, must be an integer included between 0 and
  \s-1UINT32_MAX.\s0 If not specified, or if explicitly set to -1, the filter
  will try to use a good random seed on a best effort basis.
* **pal8**  
  .IX Item "pal8"
  Set pal8 output pixel format. This option does not work with codebook
  length greater than 256.

<a name="entropy"></a>

### entropy

.IX Subsection "entropy"
Measure graylevel entropy in histogram of color channels of video frames.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Can be either _normal_ or _diff_. Default is _normal_.
  .Sp
  _diff_ mode measures entropy of histogram delta values, absolute differences
  between neighbour histogram values.

<a name="fade"></a>

### fade

.IX Subsection "fade"
Apply a fade-in/out effect to the input video.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **type, t**  
  .IX Item "type, t"
  The effect type can be either in\*(R" for a fade-in, or \*(L"out\*(R" for a fade-out
  effect.
  Default is \f(CW`in\*(C'.
* **start_frame, s**  
  .IX Item "start_frame, s"
  Specify the number of the frame to start applying the fade
  effect at. Default is 0.
* **nb_frames, n**  
  .IX Item "nb_frames, n"
  The number of frames that the fade effect lasts. At the end of the
  fade-in effect, the output video will have the same intensity as the input video.
  At the end of the fade-out transition, the output video will be filled with the
  selected **color**.
  Default is 25.
* **alpha**  
  .IX Item "alpha"
  If set to 1, fade only alpha channel, if one exists on the input.
  Default value is 0.
* **start_time, st**  
  .IX Item "start_time, st"
  Specify the timestamp (in seconds) of the frame to start to apply the fade
  effect. If both start_frame and start_time are specified, the fade will start at
  whichever comes last.  Default is 0.
* **duration, d**  
  .IX Item "duration, d"
  The number of seconds for which the fade effect has to last. At the end of the
  fade-in effect the output video will have the same intensity as the input video,
  at the end of the fade-out transition the output video will be filled with the
  selected **color**.
  If both duration and nb_frames are specified, duration is used. Default is 0
  (nb_frames is used by default).
* **color, c**  
  .IX Item "color, c"
  Specify the color of the fade. Default is black\*(R".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Fade in the first 30 frames of video:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fade=in:0:30
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The command above is equivalent to:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fade=t=in:s=0:n=30
  .Ve
* ·  
  Fade out the last 45 frames of a 200-frame video:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          fade=out:155:45
          fade=type=out:start_frame=155:nb_frames=45
  .Ve
* ·  
  Fade in the first 25 frames and fade out the last 25 frames of a 1000-frame video:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fade=in:0:25, fade=out:975:25
  .Ve
* ·  
  Make the first 5 frames yellow, then fade in from frame 5-24:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fade=in:5:20:color=yellow
  .Ve
* ·  
  Fade in alpha over first 25 frames of video:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fade=in:0:25:alpha=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Make the first 5.5 seconds black, then fade in for 0.5 seconds:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fade=t=in:st=5.5:d=0.5
  .Ve

<a name="fftfilt"></a>

### fftfilt

.IX Subsection "fftfilt"
Apply arbitrary expressions to samples in frequency domain

* **dc\_Y**  
  .IX Item "dc_Y"
  Adjust the dc value (gain) of the luma plane of the image. The filter
  accepts an integer value in range \f(CW0 to \f(CW1000. The default
  value is set to \f(CW0.
* **dc\_U**  
  .IX Item "dc_U"
  Adjust the dc value (gain) of the 1st chroma plane of the image. The
  filter accepts an integer value in range \f(CW0 to \f(CW1000. The
  default value is set to \f(CW0.
* **dc\_V**  
  .IX Item "dc_V"
  Adjust the dc value (gain) of the 2nd chroma plane of the image. The
  filter accepts an integer value in range \f(CW0 to \f(CW1000. The
  default value is set to \f(CW0.
* **weight\_Y**  
  .IX Item "weight_Y"
  Set the frequency domain weight expression for the luma plane.
* **weight\_U**  
  .IX Item "weight_U"
  Set the frequency domain weight expression for the 1st chroma plane.
* **weight\_V**  
  .IX Item "weight_V"
  Set the frequency domain weight expression for the 2nd chroma plane.
* **eval**  
  .IX Item "eval"
  Set when the expressions are evaluated.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **init**  
      .IX Item "init"
      Only evaluate expressions once during the filter initialization.
    * **frame**  
      .IX Item "frame"
      Evaluate expressions for each incoming frame.
      .Sp
      Default value is **init**.
      .Sp
      The filter accepts the following variables:
* **X**  
  .IX Item "X"
* **Y**  
  .IX Item "Y"
  The coordinates of the current sample.
* **W**  
  .IX Item "W"
* **H**  
  .IX Item "H"
  The width and height of the image.
* **N**  
  .IX Item "N"
  The number of input frame, starting from 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  High-pass:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fftfilt=dc_Y=128:weight_Y=squish(1-(Y+X)/100)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Low-pass:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fftfilt=dc_Y=0:weight_Y=squish((Y+X)/100-1)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Sharpen:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fftfilt=dc_Y=0:weight_Y=1+squish(1-(Y+X)/100)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Blur:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fftfilt=dc_Y=0:weight_Y=exp(-4 * ((Y+X)/(W+H)))\*(Aq
  .Ve

<a name="fftdnoiz"></a>

### fftdnoiz

.IX Subsection "fftdnoiz"
Denoise frames using 3D \s-1FFT\s0 (frequency domain filtering).

The filter accepts the following options:

* **sigma**  
  .IX Item "sigma"
  Set the noise sigma constant. This sets denoising strength.
  Default value is 1. Allowed range is from 0 to 30.
  Using very high sigma with low overlap may give blocking artifacts.
* **amount**  
  .IX Item "amount"
  Set amount of denoising. By default all detected noise is reduced.
  Default value is 1. Allowed range is from 0 to 1.
* **block**  
  .IX Item "block"
  Set size of block, Default is 4, can be 3, 4, 5 or 6.
  Actual size of block in pixels is 2 to power of _block_, so by default
  block size in pixels is 2^4 which is 16.
* **overlap**  
  .IX Item "overlap"
  Set block overlap. Default is 0.5. Allowed range is from 0.2 to 0.8.
* **prev**  
  .IX Item "prev"
  Set number of previous frames to use for denoising. By default is set to 0.
* **next**  
  .IX Item "next"
  Set number of next frames to to use for denoising. By default is set to 0.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set planes which will be filtered, by default are all available filtered
  except alpha.

<a name="field"></a>

### field

.IX Subsection "field"
Extract a single field from an interlaced image using stride
arithmetic to avoid wasting \s-1CPU\s0 time. The output frames are marked as
non-interlaced.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **type**  
  .IX Item "type"
  Specify whether to extract the top (if the value is \f(CW0 or
  \f(CW`top\*(C') or the bottom field (if the value is \f(CW1 or
  \f(CW`bottom\*(C').

<a name="fieldhint"></a>

### fieldhint

.IX Subsection "fieldhint"
Create new frames by copying the top and bottom fields from surrounding frames
supplied as numbers by the hint file.

* **hint**  
  .IX Item "hint"
  Set file containing hints: absolute/relative frame numbers.
  .Sp
  There must be one line for each frame in a clip. Each line must contain two
  numbers separated by the comma, optionally followed by \f(CW`-\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`+\*(C'.
  Numbers supplied on each line of file can not be out of [N-1,N+1] where N
  is current frame number for \f(CW`absolute\*(C' mode or out of [-1, 1] range
  for \f(CW`relative\*(C' mode. First number tells from which frame to pick up top
  field and second number tells from which frame to pick up bottom field.
  .Sp
  If optionally followed by \f(CW`+\*(C' output frame will be marked as interlaced,
  else if followed by \f(CW`-\*(C' output frame will be marked as progressive, else
  it will be marked same as input frame.
  If line starts with \f(CW`#\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`;\*(C' that line is skipped.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Can be item \f(CW`absolute\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`relative\*(C'. Default is \f(CW\*(C\`absolute\*(C'.

Example of first several lines of \f(CW`hint\*(C' file for \f(CW\*(C\`relative\*(C' mode:

.Vb 10
        0,0 - # first frame
        1,0 - # second frame, use thirds frame top field and second\*(Aqs frame bottom field
        1,0 - # third frame, use fourths frame top field and third\*(Aqs frame bottom field
        1,0 -
        0,0 -
        0,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        0,0 -
        0,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        1,0 -
        0,0 -
.Ve

<a name="fieldmatch"></a>

### fieldmatch

.IX Subsection "fieldmatch"
Field matching filter for inverse telecine. It is meant to reconstruct the
progressive frames from a telecined stream. The filter does not drop duplicated
frames, so to achieve a complete inverse telecine \f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' needs to be
followed by a decimation filter such as **decimate** in the filtergraph.

The separation of the field matching and the decimation is notably motivated by
the possibility of inserting a de-interlacing filter fallback between the two.
If the source has mixed telecined and real interlaced content,
\f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' will not be able to match fields for the interlaced parts.
But these remaining combed frames will be marked as interlaced, and thus can be
de-interlaced by a later filter such as **yadif** before decimation.

In addition to the various configuration options, \f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' can take an
optional second stream, activated through the **ppsrc** option. If
enabled, the frames reconstruction will be based on the fields and frames from
this second stream. This allows the first input to be pre-processed in order to
help the various algorithms of the filter, while keeping the output lossless
(assuming the fields are matched properly). Typically, a field-aware denoiser,
or brightness/contrast adjustments can help.

Note that this filter uses the same algorithms as \s-1TIVTC/TFM\s0 (AviSynth project)
and \s-1VIVTC/VFM\s0 (VapourSynth project). The later is a light clone of \s-1TFM\s0 from
which \f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' is based on. While the semantic and usage are very
close, some behaviour and options names can differ.

The **decimate** filter currently only works for constant frame rate input.
If your input has mixed telecined (30fps) and progressive content with a lower
framerate like 24fps use the following filterchain to produce the necessary cfr
stream: \f(CW`dejudder,fps=30000/1001,fieldmatch,decimate\*(C'.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **order**  
  .IX Item "order"
  Specify the assumed field order of the input stream. Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Auto detect parity (use FFmpeg's internal parity value).
    * **bff**  
      .IX Item "bff"
      Assume bottom field first.
    * **tff**  
      .IX Item "tff"
      Assume top field first.
      .Sp
      Note that it is sometimes recommended not to trust the parity announced by the
      stream.
      .Sp
      Default value is _auto_.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set the matching mode or strategy to use. **pc** mode is the safest in the
  sense that it won't risk creating jerkiness due to duplicate frames when
  possible, but if there are bad edits or blended fields it will end up
  outputting combed frames when a good match might actually exist. On the other
  hand, **pcn\_ub** mode is the most risky in terms of creating jerkiness,
  but will almost always find a good frame if there is one. The other values are
  all somewhere in between **pc** and **pcn\_ub** in terms of risking
  jerkiness and creating duplicate frames versus finding good matches in sections
  with bad edits, orphaned fields, blended fields, etc.
  .Sp
  More details about p/c/n/u/b are available in **p/c/n/u/b meaning** section.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **pc**  
      .IX Item "pc"
      2-way matching (p/c)
    * **pc\_n**  
      .IX Item "pc_n"
      2-way matching, and trying 3rd match if still combed (p/c + n)
    * **pc\_u**  
      .IX Item "pc_u"
      2-way matching, and trying 3rd match (same order) if still combed (p/c + u)
    * **pc\_n\_ub**  
      .IX Item "pc_n_ub"
      2-way matching, trying 3rd match if still combed, and trying 4th/5th matches if
      still combed (p/c + n + u/b)
    * **pcn**  
      .IX Item "pcn"
      3-way matching (p/c/n)
    * **pcn\_ub**  
      .IX Item "pcn_ub"
      3-way matching, and trying 4th/5th matches if all 3 of the original matches are
      detected as combed (p/c/n + u/b)
      .Sp
      The parenthesis at the end indicate the matches that would be used for that
      mode assuming **order**=_tff_ (and **field** on _auto_ or
      _top_).
      .Sp
      In terms of speed **pc** mode is by far the fastest and **pcn\_ub** is
      the slowest.
      .Sp
      Default value is _pc\_n_.
* **ppsrc**  
  .IX Item "ppsrc"
  Mark the main input stream as a pre-processed input, and enable the secondary
  input stream as the clean source to pick the fields from. See the filter
  introduction for more details. It is similar to the **clip2** feature from
  \s-1VFM/TFM.\s0
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW0 (disabled).
* **field**  
  .IX Item "field"
  Set the field to match from. It is recommended to set this to the same value as
  **order** unless you experience matching failures with that setting. In
  certain circumstances changing the field that is used to match from can have a
  large impact on matching performance. Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Automatic (same value as **order**).
    * **bottom**  
      .IX Item "bottom"
      Match from the bottom field.
    * **top**  
      .IX Item "top"
      Match from the top field.
      .Sp
      Default value is _auto_.
* **mchroma**  
  .IX Item "mchroma"
  Set whether or not chroma is included during the match comparisons. In most
  cases it is recommended to leave this enabled. You should set this to \f(CW0
  only if your clip has bad chroma problems such as heavy rainbowing or other
  artifacts. Setting this to \f(CW0 could also be used to speed things up at
  the cost of some accuracy.
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW1.
* **y0**  
  .IX Item "y0"
* **y1**  
  .IX Item "y1"
  These define an exclusion band which excludes the lines between **y0** and
  **y1** from being included in the field matching decision. An exclusion
  band can be used to ignore subtitles, a logo, or other things that may
  interfere with the matching. **y0** sets the starting scan line and
  **y1** sets the ending line; all lines in between **y0** and
  **y1** (including **y0** and **y1**) will be ignored. Setting
  **y0** and **y1** to the same value will disable the feature.
  **y0** and **y1** defaults to \f(CW0.
* **scthresh**  
  .IX Item "scthresh"
  Set the scene change detection threshold as a percentage of maximum change on
  the luma plane. Good values are in the \f(CW`[8.0, 14.0]\*(C' range. Scene change
  detection is only relevant in case **combmatch**=_sc_.  The range for
  **scthresh** is \f(CW`[0.0, 100.0]\*(C'.
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW12.0.
* **combmatch**  
  .IX Item "combmatch"
  When **combatch** is not _none_, \f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' will take into
  account the combed scores of matches when deciding what match to use as the
  final match. Available values are:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      No final matching based on combed scores.
    * **sc**  
      .IX Item "sc"
      Combed scores are only used when a scene change is detected.
    * **full**  
      .IX Item "full"
      Use combed scores all the time.
      .Sp
      Default is _sc_.
* **combdbg**  
  .IX Item "combdbg"
  Force \f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' to calculate the combed metrics for certain matches and
  print them. This setting is known as **micout** in \s-1TFM/VFM\s0 vocabulary.
  Available values are:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      No forced calculation.
    * **pcn**  
      .IX Item "pcn"
      Force p/c/n calculations.
    * **pcnub**  
      .IX Item "pcnub"
      Force p/c/n/u/b calculations.
      .Sp
      Default value is _none_.
* **cthresh**  
  .IX Item "cthresh"
  This is the area combing threshold used for combed frame detection. This
  essentially controls how strong\*(R" or \*(L"visible\*(R" combing must be to be detected.
  Larger values mean combing must be more visible and smaller values mean combing
  can be less visible or strong and still be detected. Valid settings are from
  \f(CW`-1\*(C' (every pixel will be detected as combed) to \f(CW255 (no pixel will
  be detected as combed). This is basically a pixel difference value. A good
  range is \f(CW`[8, 12]\*(C'.
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW9.
* **chroma**  
  .IX Item "chroma"
  Sets whether or not chroma is considered in the combed frame decision.  Only
  disable this if your source has chroma problems (rainbowing, etc.) that are
  causing problems for the combed frame detection with chroma enabled. Actually,
  using **chroma**=_0_ is usually more reliable, except for the case
  where there is chroma only combing in the source.
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **blockx**  
  .IX Item "blockx"
* **blocky**  
  .IX Item "blocky"
  Respectively set the x-axis and y-axis size of the window used during combed
  frame detection. This has to do with the size of the area in which
  **combpel** pixels are required to be detected as combed for a frame to be
  declared combed. See the **combpel** parameter description for more info.
  Possible values are any number that is a power of 2 starting at 4 and going up
  to 512.
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW16.
* **combpel**  
  .IX Item "combpel"
  The number of combed pixels inside any of the **blocky** by
  **blockx** size blocks on the frame for the frame to be detected as
  combed. While **cthresh** controls how visible\*(R" the combing must be, this
  setting controls how much\*(R" combing there must be in any localized area (a
  window defined by the **blockx** and **blocky** settings) on the
  frame. Minimum value is \f(CW0 and maximum is \f(CW`blocky x blockx\*(C' (at
  which point no frames will ever be detected as combed). This setting is known
  as **\s-1MI\s0** in \s-1TFM/VFM\s0 vocabulary.
  .Sp
  Default value is \f(CW80.

_p/c/n/u/b meaning_
.IX Subsection "p/c/n/u/b meaning"

p/c/n
.IX Subsection "p/c/n"

We assume the following telecined stream:

.Vb 2
        Top fields:     1 2 2 3 4
        Bottom fields:  1 2 3 4 4
.Ve

The numbers correspond to the progressive frame the fields relate to. Here, the
first two frames are progressive, the 3rd and 4th are combed, and so on.

When \f(CW`fieldmatch\*(C' is configured to run a matching from bottom
(**field**=_bottom_) this is how this input stream get transformed:

.Vb 3
        Input stream:
                        T     1 2 2 3 4
                        B     1 2 3 4 4   &lt;-- matching reference
        
        Matches:              c c n n c
        
        Output stream:
                        T     1 2 3 4 4
                        B     1 2 3 4 4
.Ve

As a result of the field matching, we can see that some frames get duplicated.
To perform a complete inverse telecine, you need to rely on a decimation filter
after this operation. See for instance the **decimate** filter.

The same operation now matching from top fields (**field**=_top_)
looks like this:

.Vb 3
        Input stream:
                        T     1 2 2 3 4   &lt;-- matching reference
                        B     1 2 3 4 4
        
        Matches:              c c p p c
        
        Output stream:
                        T     1 2 2 3 4
                        B     1 2 2 3 4
.Ve

In these examples, we can see what _p_, _c_ and _n_ mean;
basically, they refer to the frame and field of the opposite parity:

* *&lt;_p_ matches the field of the opposite parity in the previous frame&gt;  
  .IX Item "*&lt;p matches the field of the opposite parity in the previous frame&gt;"
* *&lt;_c_ matches the field of the opposite parity in the current frame&gt;  
  .IX Item "*&lt;c matches the field of the opposite parity in the current frame&gt;"
* *&lt;_n_ matches the field of the opposite parity in the next frame&gt;  
  .IX Item "*&lt;n matches the field of the opposite parity in the next frame&gt;"

u/b
.IX Subsection "u/b"

The _u_ and _b_ matching are a bit special in the sense that they match
from the opposite parity flag. In the following examples, we assume that we are
currently matching the 2nd frame (Top:2, bottom:2). According to the match, a
'x' is placed above and below each matched fields.

With bottom matching (**field**=_bottom_):

.Vb 1
        Match:           c         p           n          b          u
        
                         x       x               x        x          x
          Top          1 2 2     1 2 2       1 2 2      1 2 2      1 2 2
          Bottom       1 2 3     1 2 3       1 2 3      1 2 3      1 2 3
                         x         x           x        x              x
        
        Output frames:
                         2          1          2          2          2
                         2          2          2          1          3
.Ve

With top matching (**field**=_top_):

.Vb 1
        Match:           c         p           n          b          u
        
                         x         x           x        x              x
          Top          1 2 2     1 2 2       1 2 2      1 2 2      1 2 2
          Bottom       1 2 3     1 2 3       1 2 3      1 2 3      1 2 3
                         x       x               x        x          x
        
        Output frames:
                         2          2          2          1          2
                         2          1          3          2          2
.Ve

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

Simple \s-1IVTC\s0 of a top field first telecined stream:

.Vb 1
        fieldmatch=order=tff:combmatch=none, decimate
.Ve

Advanced \s-1IVTC,\s0 with fallback on **yadif** for still combed frames:

.Vb 1
        fieldmatch=order=tff:combmatch=full, yadif=deint=interlaced, decimate
.Ve

<a name="fieldorder"></a>

### fieldorder

.IX Subsection "fieldorder"
Transform the field order of the input video.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **order**  
  .IX Item "order"
  The output field order. Valid values are _tff_ for top field first or _bff_
  for bottom field first.

The default value is **tff**.

The transformation is done by shifting the picture content up or down
by one line, and filling the remaining line with appropriate picture content.
This method is consistent with most broadcast field order converters.

If the input video is not flagged as being interlaced, or it is already
flagged as being of the required output field order, then this filter does
not alter the incoming video.

It is very useful when converting to or from \s-1PAL DV\s0 material,
which is bottom field first.

For example:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i in.vob -vf "fieldorder=bff" out.dv
.Ve

<a name="fifo-afifo"></a>

### fifo, afifo

.IX Subsection "fifo, afifo"
Buffer input images and send them when they are requested.

It is mainly useful when auto-inserted by the libavfilter
framework.

It does not take parameters.

<a name="fillborders"></a>

### fillborders

.IX Subsection "fillborders"
Fill borders of the input video, without changing video stream dimensions.
Sometimes video can have garbage at the four edges and you may not want to
crop video input to keep size multiple of some number.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **left**  
  .IX Item "left"
  Number of pixels to fill from left border.
* **right**  
  .IX Item "right"
  Number of pixels to fill from right border.
* **top**  
  .IX Item "top"
  Number of pixels to fill from top border.
* **bottom**  
  .IX Item "bottom"
  Number of pixels to fill from bottom border.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set fill mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **smear**  
      .IX Item "smear"
      fill pixels using outermost pixels
    * **mirror**  
      .IX Item "mirror"
      fill pixels using mirroring
    * **fixed**  
      .IX Item "fixed"
      fill pixels with constant value
      .Sp
      Default is _smear_.
* **color**  
  .IX Item "color"
  Set color for pixels in fixed mode. Default is _black_.

<a name="find_rect"></a>

### find_rect

.IX Subsection "find_rect"
Find a rectangular object

It accepts the following options:

* **object**  
  .IX Item "object"
  Filepath of the object image, needs to be in gray8.
* **threshold**  
  .IX Item "threshold"
  Detection threshold, default is 0.5.
* **mipmaps**  
  .IX Item "mipmaps"
  Number of mipmaps, default is 3.
* **xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax**  
  .IX Item "xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax"
  Specifies the rectangle in which to search.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Generate a representative palette of a given video using **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i file.ts -vf find_rect=newref.pgm,cover_rect=cover.jpg:mode=cover new.mkv
  .Ve

<a name="cover_rect"></a>

### cover_rect

.IX Subsection "cover_rect"
Cover a rectangular object

It accepts the following options:

* **cover**  
  .IX Item "cover"
  Filepath of the optional cover image, needs to be in yuv420.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set covering mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **cover**  
      .IX Item "cover"
      cover it by the supplied image
    * **blur**  
      .IX Item "blur"
      cover it by interpolating the surrounding pixels
      .Sp
      Default value is _blur_.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Generate a representative palette of a given video using **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i file.ts -vf find_rect=newref.pgm,cover_rect=cover.jpg:mode=cover new.mkv
  .Ve

<a name="floodfill"></a>

### floodfill

.IX Subsection "floodfill"
Flood area with values of same pixel components with another values.

It accepts the following options:

* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
  Set pixel x coordinate.
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  Set pixel y coordinate.
* **s0**  
  .IX Item "s0"
  Set source #0 component value.
* **s1**  
  .IX Item "s1"
  Set source #1 component value.
* **s2**  
  .IX Item "s2"
  Set source #2 component value.
* **s3**  
  .IX Item "s3"
  Set source #3 component value.
* **d0**  
  .IX Item "d0"
  Set destination #0 component value.
* **d1**  
  .IX Item "d1"
  Set destination #1 component value.
* **d2**  
  .IX Item "d2"
  Set destination #2 component value.
* **d3**  
  .IX Item "d3"
  Set destination #3 component value.

<a name="format"></a>

### format

.IX Subsection "format"
Convert the input video to one of the specified pixel formats.
