Libavfilter will try to pick one that is suitable as input to
the next filter.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **pix\_fmts**  
  .IX Item "pix_fmts"
  A '|'-separated list of pixel format names, such as
  pix_fmts=yuv420p|monow|rgb24\*(R".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Convert the input video to the _yuv420p_ format
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=pix_fmts=yuv420p
  .Ve
  .Sp
  Convert the input video to any of the formats in the list
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=pix_fmts=yuv420p|yuv444p|yuv410p
  .Ve

<a name="fps"></a>

### fps

.IX Subsection "fps"
Convert the video to specified constant frame rate by duplicating or dropping
frames as necessary.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **fps**  
  .IX Item "fps"
  The desired output frame rate. The default is \f(CW25.
* **start\_time**  
  .IX Item "start_time"
  Assume the first \s-1PTS\s0 should be the given value, in seconds. This allows for
  padding/trimming at the start of stream. By default, no assumption is made
  about the first frame's expected \s-1PTS,\s0 so no padding or trimming is done.
  For example, this could be set to 0 to pad the beginning with duplicates of
  the first frame if a video stream starts after the audio stream or to trim any
  frames with a negative \s-1PTS.\s0
* **round**  
  .IX Item "round"
  Timestamp (\s-1PTS\s0) rounding method.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * **zero**  
      .IX Item "zero"
      round towards 0
    * **inf**  
      .IX Item "inf"
      round away from 0
    * **down**  
      .IX Item "down"
      round towards -infinity
    * **up**  
      .IX Item "up"
      round towards +infinity
    * **near**  
      .IX Item "near"
      round to nearest
      .Sp
      The default is \f(CW`near\*(C'.
* **eof\_action**  
  .IX Item "eof_action"
  Action performed when reading the last frame.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * **round**  
      .IX Item "round"
      Use same timestamp rounding method as used for other frames.
    * **pass**  
      .IX Item "pass"
      Pass through last frame if input duration has not been reached yet.
      .Sp
      The default is \f(CW`round\*(C'.

Alternatively, the options can be specified as a flat string:
_fps_[:_start\_time_[:_round_]].

See also the **setpts** filter.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  A typical usage in order to set the fps to 25:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fps=fps=25
  .Ve
* ·  
  Sets the fps to 24, using abbreviation and rounding method to round to nearest:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fps=fps=film:round=near
  .Ve

<a name="framepack"></a>

### framepack

.IX Subsection "framepack"
Pack two different video streams into a stereoscopic video, setting proper
metadata on supported codecs. The two views should have the same size and
framerate and processing will stop when the shorter video ends. Please note
that you may conveniently adjust view properties with the **scale** and
**fps** filters.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **format**  
  .IX Item "format"
  The desired packing format. Supported values are:
    * **sbs**  
      .IX Item "sbs"
      The views are next to each other (default).
    * **tab**  
      .IX Item "tab"
      The views are on top of each other.
    * **lines**  
      .IX Item "lines"
      The views are packed by line.
    * **columns**  
      .IX Item "columns"
      The views are packed by column.
    * **frameseq**  
      .IX Item "frameseq"
      The views are temporally interleaved.

Some examples:

.Vb 2
        # Convert left and right views into a frame-sequential video
        ffmpeg -i LEFT -i RIGHT -filter_complex framepack=frameseq OUTPUT
        
        # Convert views into a side-by-side video with the same output resolution as the input
        ffmpeg -i LEFT -i RIGHT -filter_complex [0:v]scale=w=iw/2[left],[1:v]scale=w=iw/2[right],[left][right]framepack=sbs OUTPUT
.Ve

<a name="framerate"></a>

### framerate

.IX Subsection "framerate"
Change the frame rate by interpolating new video output frames from the source
frames.

This filter is not designed to function correctly with interlaced media. If
you wish to change the frame rate of interlaced media then you are required
to deinterlace before this filter and re-interlace after this filter.

A description of the accepted options follows.

* **fps**  
  .IX Item "fps"
  Specify the output frames per second. This option can also be specified
  as a value alone. The default is \f(CW50.
* **interp\_start**  
  .IX Item "interp_start"
  Specify the start of a range where the output frame will be created as a
  linear interpolation of two frames. The range is [\f(CW0-\f(CW255],
  the default is \f(CW15.
* **interp\_end**  
  .IX Item "interp_end"
  Specify the end of a range where the output frame will be created as a
  linear interpolation of two frames. The range is [\f(CW0-\f(CW255],
  the default is \f(CW240.
* **scene**  
  .IX Item "scene"
  Specify the level at which a scene change is detected as a value between
  0 and 100 to indicate a new scene; a low value reflects a low
  probability for the current frame to introduce a new scene, while a higher
  value means the current frame is more likely to be one.
  The default is \f(CW8.2.
* **flags**  
  .IX Item "flags"
  Specify flags influencing the filter process.
  .Sp
  Available value for _flags_ is:
    * **scene_change_detect, scd**  
      .IX Item "scene_change_detect, scd"
      Enable scene change detection using the value of the option _scene_.
      This flag is enabled by default.

<a name="framestep"></a>

### framestep

.IX Subsection "framestep"
Select one frame every N-th frame.

This filter accepts the following option:

* **step**  
  .IX Item "step"
  Select frame after every \f(CW`step\*(C' frames.
  Allowed values are positive integers higher than 0. Default value is \f(CW1.

<a name="frei0r"></a>

### frei0r

.IX Subsection "frei0r"
Apply a frei0r effect to the input video.

To enable the compilation of this filter, you need to install the frei0r
header and configure FFmpeg with \f(CW`--enable-frei0r\*(C'.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **filter\_name**  
  .IX Item "filter_name"
  The name of the frei0r effect to load. If the environment variable
  **\s-1FREI0R\_PATH\s0** is defined, the frei0r effect is searched for in each of the
  directories specified by the colon-separated list in **\s-1FREI0R\_PATH\s0**.
  Otherwise, the standard frei0r paths are searched, in this order:
  _\s-1HOME/\s0.frei0r-1/lib/_, _/usr/local/lib/frei0r-1/_,
  _/usr/lib/frei0r-1/_.
* **filter\_params**  
  .IX Item "filter_params"
  A '|'-separated list of parameters to pass to the frei0r effect.

A frei0r effect parameter can be a boolean (its value is either
y\*(R" or \*(L"n\*(R"), a double, a color (specified as
_R_/_G_/_B_, where _R_, _G_, and _B_ are floating point
numbers between 0.0 and 1.0, inclusive) or a color description as specified in the
**Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**,
a position (specified as _X_/_Y_, where
_X_ and _Y_ are floating point numbers) and/or a string.

The number and types of parameters depend on the loaded effect. If an
effect parameter is not specified, the default value is set.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Apply the distort0r effect, setting the first two double parameters:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          frei0r=filter_name=distort0r:filter_params=0.5|0.01
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply the colordistance effect, taking a color as the first parameter:
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          frei0r=colordistance:0.2/0.3/0.4
          frei0r=colordistance:violet
          frei0r=colordistance:0x112233
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply the perspective effect, specifying the top left and top right image
  positions:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          frei0r=perspective:0.2/0.2|0.8/0.2
  .Ve

For more information, see
&lt;**http://frei0r.dyne.org**&gt;

<a name="fspp"></a>

### fspp

.IX Subsection "fspp"
Apply fast and simple postprocessing. It is a faster version of **spp**.

It splits (I)DCT into horizontal/vertical passes. Unlike the simple post-
processing filter, one of them is performed once per block, not per pixel.
This allows for much higher speed.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **quality**  
  .IX Item "quality"
  Set quality. This option defines the number of levels for averaging. It accepts
  an integer in the range 4-5. Default value is \f(CW4.
* **qp**  
  .IX Item "qp"
  Force a constant quantization parameter. It accepts an integer in range 0-63.
  If not set, the filter will use the \s-1QP\s0 from the video stream (if available).
* **strength**  
  .IX Item "strength"
  Set filter strength. It accepts an integer in range -15 to 32. Lower values mean
  more details but also more artifacts, while higher values make the image smoother
  but also blurrier. Default value is \f(CW0 X \s-1PSNR\s0 optimal.
* **use\_bframe\_qp**  
  .IX Item "use_bframe_qp"
  Enable the use of the \s-1QP\s0 from the B-Frames if set to \f(CW1. Using this
  option may cause flicker since the B-Frames have often larger \s-1QP.\s0 Default is
  \f(CW0 (not enabled).

<a name="gblur"></a>

### gblur

.IX Subsection "gblur"
Apply Gaussian blur filter.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **sigma**  
  .IX Item "sigma"
  Set horizontal sigma, standard deviation of Gaussian blur. Default is \f(CW0.5.
* **steps**  
  .IX Item "steps"
  Set number of steps for Gaussian approximation. Defauls is \f(CW1.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes to filter. By default all planes are filtered.
* **sigmaV**  
  .IX Item "sigmaV"
  Set vertical sigma, if negative it will be same as \f(CW`sigma\*(C'.
  Default is \f(CW`-1\*(C'.

<a name="geq"></a>

### geq

.IX Subsection "geq"
Apply generic equation to each pixel.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **lum_expr, lum**  
  .IX Item "lum_expr, lum"
  Set the luminance expression.
* **cb_expr, cb**  
  .IX Item "cb_expr, cb"
  Set the chrominance blue expression.
* **cr_expr, cr**  
  .IX Item "cr_expr, cr"
  Set the chrominance red expression.
* **alpha_expr, a**  
  .IX Item "alpha_expr, a"
  Set the alpha expression.
* **red_expr, r**  
  .IX Item "red_expr, r"
  Set the red expression.
* **green_expr, g**  
  .IX Item "green_expr, g"
  Set the green expression.
* **blue_expr, b**  
  .IX Item "blue_expr, b"
  Set the blue expression.

The colorspace is selected according to the specified options. If one
of the **lum\_expr**, **cb\_expr**, or **cr\_expr**
options is specified, the filter will automatically select a YCbCr
colorspace. If one of the **red\_expr**, **green\_expr**, or
**blue\_expr** options is specified, it will select an \s-1RGB\s0
colorspace.

If one of the chrominance expression is not defined, it falls back on the other
one. If no alpha expression is specified it will evaluate to opaque value.
If none of chrominance expressions are specified, they will evaluate
to the luminance expression.

The expressions can use the following variables and functions:

* **N**  
  .IX Item "N"
  The sequential number of the filtered frame, starting from \f(CW0.
* **X**  
  .IX Item "X"
* **Y**  
  .IX Item "Y"
  The coordinates of the current sample.
* **W**  
  .IX Item "W"
* **H**  
  .IX Item "H"
  The width and height of the image.
* **\s-1SW\s0**  
  .IX Item "SW"
* **\s-1SH\s0**  
  .IX Item "SH"
  Width and height scale depending on the currently filtered plane. It is the
  ratio between the corresponding luma plane number of pixels and the current
  plane ones. E.g. for \s-1YUV4:2:0\s0 the values are \f(CW`1,1\*(C' for the luma plane, and
  \f(CW`0.5,0.5\*(C' for chroma planes.
* **T**  
  .IX Item "T"
  Time of the current frame, expressed in seconds.
* **p(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "p(x, y)"
  Return the value of the pixel at location (_x_,_y_) of the current
  plane.
* **lum(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "lum(x, y)"
  Return the value of the pixel at location (_x_,_y_) of the luminance
  plane.
* **cb(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "cb(x, y)"
  Return the value of the pixel at location (_x_,_y_) of the
  blue-difference chroma plane. Return 0 if there is no such plane.
* **cr(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "cr(x, y)"
  Return the value of the pixel at location (_x_,_y_) of the
  red-difference chroma plane. Return 0 if there is no such plane.
* **r(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "r(x, y)"
* **g(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "g(x, y)"
* **b(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "b(x, y)"
  Return the value of the pixel at location (_x_,_y_) of the
  red/green/blue component. Return 0 if there is no such component.
* **alpha(x, y)**  
  .IX Item "alpha(x, y)"
  Return the value of the pixel at location (_x_,_y_) of the alpha
  plane. Return 0 if there is no such plane.

For functions, if _x_ and _y_ are outside the area, the value will be
automatically clipped to the closer edge.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Flip the image horizontally:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          geq=p(W-X\e,Y)
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate a bidimensional sine wave, with angle \f(CW`PI/3\*(C' and a
  wavelength of 100 pixels:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          geq=128 + 100*sin(2*(PI/100)*(cos(PI/3)*(X-50*T) + sin(PI/3)*Y)):128:128
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate a fancy enigmatic moving light:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          nullsrc=s=256x256,geq=random(1)/hypot(X-cos(N*0.07)*W/2-W/2\e,Y-sin(N*0.09)*H/2-H/2)^2*1000000*sin(N*0.02):128:128
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate a quick emboss effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=gray,geq=lum_expr=(p(X,Y)+(256-p(X-4,Y-4)))/2\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Modify \s-1RGB\s0 components depending on pixel position:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          geq=r=X/W*r(X,Y)\*(Aq:g=\*(Aq(1-X/W)*g(X,Y)\*(Aq:b=\*(Aq(H-Y)/H*b(X,Y)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Create a radial gradient that is the same size as the input (also see
  the **vignette** filter):
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          geq=lum=255*gauss((X/W-0.5)*3)*gauss((Y/H-0.5)*3)/gauss(0)/gauss(0),format=gray
  .Ve

<a name="gradfun"></a>

### gradfun

.IX Subsection "gradfun"
Fix the banding artifacts that are sometimes introduced into nearly flat
regions by truncation to 8-bit color depth.
Interpolate the gradients that should go where the bands are, and
dither them.

It is designed for playback only.  Do not use it prior to
lossy compression, because compression tends to lose the dither and
bring back the bands.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **strength**  
  .IX Item "strength"
  The maximum amount by which the filter will change any one pixel. This is also
  the threshold for detecting nearly flat regions. Acceptable values range from
  .51 to 64; the default value is 1.2. Out-of-range values will be clipped to the
  valid range.
* **radius**  
  .IX Item "radius"
  The neighborhood to fit the gradient to. A larger radius makes for smoother
  gradients, but also prevents the filter from modifying the pixels near detailed
  regions. Acceptable values are 8-32; the default value is 16. Out-of-range
  values will be clipped to the valid range.

Alternatively, the options can be specified as a flat string:
_strength_[:_radius_]

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Apply the filter with a \f(CW3.5 strength and radius of \f(CW8:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          gradfun=3.5:8
  .Ve
* ·  
  Specify radius, omitting the strength (which will fall-back to the default
  value):
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          gradfun=radius=8
  .Ve

<a name="graphmonitor-agraphmonitor"></a>

### graphmonitor, agraphmonitor

.IX Subsection "graphmonitor, agraphmonitor"
Show various filtergraph stats.

With this filter one can debug complete filtergraph.
Especially issues with links filling with queued frames.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set video output size. Default is _hd720_.
* **opacity, o**  
  .IX Item "opacity, o"
  Set video opacity. Default is _0.9_. Allowed range is from _0_ to _1_.
* **mode, m**  
  .IX Item "mode, m"
  Set output mode, can be _fulll_ or _compact_.
  In _compact_ mode only filters with some queued frames have displayed stats.
* **flags, f**  
  .IX Item "flags, f"
  Set flags which enable which stats are shown in video.
  .Sp
  Available values for flags are:
    * **queue**  
      .IX Item "queue"
      Display number of queued frames in each link.
    * **frame\_count\_in**  
      .IX Item "frame_count_in"
      Display number of frames taken from filter.
    * **frame\_count\_out**  
      .IX Item "frame_count_out"
      Display number of frames given out from filter.
    * **pts**  
      .IX Item "pts"
      Display current filtered frame pts.
    * **time**  
      .IX Item "time"
      Display current filtered frame time.
    * **timebase**  
      .IX Item "timebase"
      Display time base for filter link.
    * **format**  
      .IX Item "format"
      Display used format for filter link.
    * **size**  
      .IX Item "size"
      Display video size or number of audio channels in case of audio used by filter link.
    * **rate**  
      .IX Item "rate"
      Display video frame rate or sample rate in case of audio used by filter link.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set upper limit for video rate of output stream, Default value is _25_.
  This guarantee that output video frame rate will not be higher than this value.

<a name="greyedge"></a>

### greyedge

.IX Subsection "greyedge"
A color constancy variation filter which estimates scene illumination via grey edge algorithm
and corrects the scene colors accordingly.

See: &lt;**https://staff.science.uva.nl/th.gevers/pub/GeversTIP07.pdf**&gt;

The filter accepts the following options:

* **difford**  
  .IX Item "difford"
  The order of differentiation to be applied on the scene. Must be chosen in the range
  [0,2] and default value is 1.
* **minknorm**  
  .IX Item "minknorm"
  The Minkowski parameter to be used for calculating the Minkowski distance. Must
  be chosen in the range [0,20] and default value is 1. Set to 0 for getting
  max value instead of calculating Minkowski distance.
* **sigma**  
  .IX Item "sigma"
  The standard deviation of Gaussian blur to be applied on the scene. Must be
  chosen in the range [0,1024.0] and default value = 1. floor( _sigma_ * **break\_off\_sigma**\|(3) )
  can't be euqal to 0 if _difford_ is greater than 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Grey Edge:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          greyedge=difford=1:minknorm=5:sigma=2
  .Ve
* ·  
  Max Edge:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          greyedge=difford=1:minknorm=0:sigma=2
  .Ve

<a name="haldclut"></a>

### haldclut

.IX Subsection "haldclut"
Apply a Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 to a video stream.

First input is the video stream to process, and second one is the Hald \s-1CLUT.\s0
The Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 input can be a simple picture or a complete video stream.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **shortest**  
  .IX Item "shortest"
  Force termination when the shortest input terminates. Default is \f(CW0.
* **repeatlast**  
  .IX Item "repeatlast"
  Continue applying the last \s-1CLUT\s0 after the end of the stream. A value of
  \f(CW0 disable the filter after the last frame of the \s-1CLUT\s0 is reached.
  Default is \f(CW1.

\f(CW`haldclut\*(C' also has the same interpolation options as **lut3d** (both
filters share the same internals).

More information about the Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 can be found on Eskil Steenberg's website
(Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 author) at &lt;**http://www.quelsolaar.com/technology/clut.html**&gt;.

_Workflow examples_
.IX Subsection "Workflow examples"

Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 video stream
.IX Subsection "Hald CLUT video stream"

Generate an identity Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 stream altered with various effects:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -f lavfi -i B&lt;haldclutsrc&gt;=8 -vf "hue=H=2*PI*t:s=sin(2*PI*t)+1, curves=cross_process" -t 10 -c:v ffv1 clut.nut
.Ve

Note: make sure you use a lossless codec.

Then use it with \f(CW`haldclut\*(C' to apply it on some random stream:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -f lavfi -i mandelbrot -i clut.nut -filter_complex [0][1] haldclut\*(Aq -t 20 mandelclut.mkv
.Ve

The Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 will be applied to the 10 first seconds (duration of
_clut.nut_), then the latest picture of that \s-1CLUT\s0 stream will be applied
to the remaining frames of the \f(CW`mandelbrot\*(C' stream.

Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 with preview
.IX Subsection "Hald CLUT with preview"

A Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 is supposed to be a squared image of \f(CW`Level*Level*Level\*(C' by
\f(CW`Level*Level*Level\*(C' pixels. For a given Hald \s-1CLUT,\s0 FFmpeg will select the
biggest possible square starting at the top left of the picture. The remaining
padding pixels (bottom or right) will be ignored. This area can be used to add
a preview of the Hald \s-1CLUT.\s0

Typically, the following generated Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 will be supported by the
\f(CW`haldclut\*(C' filter:

.Vb 5
        ffmpeg -f lavfi -i B&lt;haldclutsrc&gt;=8 -vf "
           pad=iw+320 [padded_clut];
           smptebars=s=320x256, split [a][b];
           [padded_clut][a] overlay=W-320:h, curves=color_negative [main];
           [main][b] overlay=W-320" -frames:v 1 clut.png
.Ve

It contains the original and a preview of the effect of the \s-1CLUT: SMPTE\s0 color
bars are displayed on the right-top, and below the same color bars processed by
the color changes.

Then, the effect of this Hald \s-1CLUT\s0 can be visualized with:

.Vb 1
        ffplay input.mkv -vf "movie=clut.png, [in] haldclut"
.Ve

<a name="hflip"></a>

### hflip

.IX Subsection "hflip"
Flip the input video horizontally.

For example, to horizontally flip the input video with **ffmpeg**:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i in.avi -vf "hflip" out.avi
.Ve

<a name="histeq"></a>

### histeq

.IX Subsection "histeq"
This filter applies a global color histogram equalization on a
per-frame basis.

It can be used to correct video that has a compressed range of pixel
intensities.  The filter redistributes the pixel intensities to
equalize their distribution across the intensity range. It may be
viewed as an automatically adjusting contrast filter\*(R". This filter is
useful only for correcting degraded or poorly captured source
video.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **strength**  
  .IX Item "strength"
  Determine the amount of equalization to be applied.  As the strength
  is reduced, the distribution of pixel intensities more-and-more
  approaches that of the input frame. The value must be a float number
  in the range [0,1] and defaults to 0.200.
* **intensity**  
  .IX Item "intensity"
  Set the maximum intensity that can generated and scale the output
  values appropriately.  The strength should be set as desired and then
  the intensity can be limited if needed to avoid washing-out. The value
  must be a float number in the range [0,1] and defaults to 0.210.
* **antibanding**  
  .IX Item "antibanding"
  Set the antibanding level. If enabled the filter will randomly vary
  the luminance of output pixels by a small amount to avoid banding of
  the histogram. Possible values are \f(CW`none\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`weak\*(C' or
  \f(CW`strong\*(C'. It defaults to \f(CW\*(C\`none\*(C'.

<a name="histogram"></a>

### histogram

.IX Subsection "histogram"
Compute and draw a color distribution histogram for the input video.

The computed histogram is a representation of the color component
distribution in an image.

Standard histogram displays the color components distribution in an image.
Displays color graph for each color component. Shows distribution of
the Y, U, V, A or R, G, B components, depending on input format, in the
current frame. Below each graph a color component scale meter is shown.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **level\_height**  
  .IX Item "level_height"
  Set height of level. Default value is \f(CW200.
  Allowed range is [50, 2048].
* **scale\_height**  
  .IX Item "scale_height"
  Set height of color scale. Default value is \f(CW12.
  Allowed range is [0, 40].
* **display\_mode**  
  .IX Item "display_mode"
  Set display mode.
  It accepts the following values:
    * **stack**  
      .IX Item "stack"
      Per color component graphs are placed below each other.
    * **parade**  
      .IX Item "parade"
      Per color component graphs are placed side by side.
    * **overlay**  
      .IX Item "overlay"
      Presents information identical to that in the \f(CW`parade\*(C', except
      that the graphs representing color components are superimposed directly
      over one another.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`stack\*(C'.
* **levels\_mode**  
  .IX Item "levels_mode"
  Set mode. Can be either \f(CW`linear\*(C', or \f(CW\*(C\`logarithmic\*(C'.
  Default is \f(CW`linear\*(C'.
* **components**  
  .IX Item "components"
  Set what color components to display.
  Default is \f(CW7.
* **fgopacity**  
  .IX Item "fgopacity"
  Set foreground opacity. Default is \f(CW0.7.
* **bgopacity**  
  .IX Item "bgopacity"
  Set background opacity. Default is \f(CW0.5.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Calculate and draw histogram:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay -i input -vf histogram
  .Ve

<a name="hqdn3d"></a>

### hqdn3d

.IX Subsection "hqdn3d"
This is a high precision/quality 3d denoise filter. It aims to reduce
image noise, producing smooth images and making still images really
still. It should enhance compressibility.

It accepts the following optional parameters:

* **luma\_spatial**  
  .IX Item "luma_spatial"
  A non-negative floating point number which specifies spatial luma strength.
  It defaults to 4.0.
* **chroma\_spatial**  
  .IX Item "chroma_spatial"
  A non-negative floating point number which specifies spatial chroma strength.
  It defaults to 3.0*_luma\_spatial_/4.0.
* **luma\_tmp**  
  .IX Item "luma_tmp"
  A floating point number which specifies luma temporal strength. It defaults to
  6.0*_luma\_spatial_/4.0.
* **chroma\_tmp**  
  .IX Item "chroma_tmp"
  A floating point number which specifies chroma temporal strength. It defaults to
  _luma\_tmp_*_chroma\_spatial_/_luma\_spatial_.

<a name="hwdownload"></a>

### hwdownload

.IX Subsection "hwdownload"
Download hardware frames to system memory.

The input must be in hardware frames, and the output a non-hardware format.
Not all formats will be supported on the output - it may be necessary to insert
an additional **format** filter immediately following in the graph to get
the output in a supported format.

<a name="hwmap"></a>

### hwmap

.IX Subsection "hwmap"
Map hardware frames to system memory or to another device.

This filter has several different modes of operation; which one is used depends
on the input and output formats:

* ·  
  Hardware frame input, normal frame output
  .Sp
  Map the input frames to system memory and pass them to the output.  If the
  original hardware frame is later required (for example, after overlaying
  something else on part of it), the **hwmap** filter can be used again
  in the next mode to retrieve it.
* ·  
  Normal frame input, hardware frame output
  .Sp
  If the input is actually a software-mapped hardware frame, then unmap it -
  that is, return the original hardware frame.
  .Sp
  Otherwise, a device must be provided.  Create new hardware surfaces on that
  device for the output, then map them back to the software format at the input
  and give those frames to the preceding filter.  This will then act like the
  **hwupload** filter, but may be able to avoid an additional copy when
  the input is already in a compatible format.
* ·  
  Hardware frame input and output
  .Sp
  A device must be supplied for the output, either directly or with the
  **derive\_device** option.  The input and output devices must be of
  different types and compatible - the exact meaning of this is
  system-dependent, but typically it means that they must refer to the same
  underlying hardware context (for example, refer to the same graphics card).
  .Sp
  If the input frames were originally created on the output device, then unmap
  to retrieve the original frames.
  .Sp
  Otherwise, map the frames to the output device - create new hardware frames
  on the output corresponding to the frames on the input.

The following additional parameters are accepted:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set the frame mapping mode.  Some combination of:
    * _read_  
      .IX Item "read"
      The mapped frame should be readable.
    * _write_  
      .IX Item "write"
      The mapped frame should be writeable.
    * _overwrite_  
      .IX Item "overwrite"
      The mapping will always overwrite the entire frame.
      .Sp
      This may improve performance in some cases, as the original contents of the
      frame need not be loaded.
    * _direct_  
      .IX Item "direct"
      The mapping must not involve any copying.
      .Sp
      Indirect mappings to copies of frames are created in some cases where either
      direct mapping is not possible or it would have unexpected properties.
      Setting this flag ensures that the mapping is direct and will fail if that is
      not possible.
      .Sp
      Defaults to _read+write_ if not specified.
* **derive\_device** _type_  
  .IX Item "derive_device type"
  Rather than using the device supplied at initialisation, instead derive a new
  device of type _type_ from the device the input frames exist on.
* **reverse**  
  .IX Item "reverse"
  In a hardware to hardware mapping, map in reverse - create frames in the sink
  and map them back to the source.  This may be necessary in some cases where
  a mapping in one direction is required but only the opposite direction is
  supported by the devices being used.
  .Sp
  This option is dangerous - it may break the preceding filter in undefined
  ways if there are any additional constraints on that filter's output.
  Do not use it without fully understanding the implications of its use.

<a name="hwupload"></a>

### hwupload

.IX Subsection "hwupload"
Upload system memory frames to hardware surfaces.

The device to upload to must be supplied when the filter is initialised.  If
using ffmpeg, select the appropriate device with the **-filter\_hw\_device**
option.

<a name="hwupload_cuda"></a>

### hwupload_cuda

.IX Subsection "hwupload_cuda"
Upload system memory frames to a \s-1CUDA\s0 device.

It accepts the following optional parameters:

* **device**  
  .IX Item "device"
  The number of the \s-1CUDA\s0 device to use

<a name="hqx"></a>

### hqx

.IX Subsection "hqx"
Apply a high-quality magnification filter designed for pixel art. This filter
was originally created by Maxim Stepin.

It accepts the following option:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  Set the scaling dimension: \f(CW2 for \f(CW`hq2x\*(C', \f(CW3 for
  \f(CW`hq3x\*(C' and \f(CW4 for \f(CW\*(C\`hq4x\*(C'.
  Default is \f(CW3.

<a name="hstack"></a>

### hstack

.IX Subsection "hstack"
Stack input videos horizontally.

All streams must be of same pixel format and of same height.

Note that this filter is faster than using **overlay** and **pad** filter
to create same output.

The filter accept the following option:

* **inputs**  
  .IX Item "inputs"
  Set number of input streams. Default is 2.
* **shortest**  
  .IX Item "shortest"
  If set to 1, force the output to terminate when the shortest input
  terminates. Default value is 0.

<a name="hue"></a>

### hue

.IX Subsection "hue"
Modify the hue and/or the saturation of the input.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  Specify the hue angle as a number of degrees. It accepts an expression,
  and defaults to 0\*(R".
* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
  Specify the saturation in the [-10,10] range. It accepts an expression and
  defaults to 1\*(R".
* **H**  
  .IX Item "H"
  Specify the hue angle as a number of radians. It accepts an
  expression, and defaults to 0\*(R".
* **b**  
  .IX Item "b"
  Specify the brightness in the [-10,10] range. It accepts an expression and
  defaults to 0\*(R".

**h** and **H** are mutually exclusive, and can't be
specified at the same time.

The **b**, **h**, **H** and **s** option values are
expressions containing the following constants:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  frame count of the input frame starting from 0
* **pts**  
  .IX Item "pts"
  presentation timestamp of the input frame expressed in time base units
* **r**  
  .IX Item "r"
  frame rate of the input video, \s-1NAN\s0 if the input frame rate is unknown
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  timestamp expressed in seconds, \s-1NAN\s0 if the input timestamp is unknown
* **tb**  
  .IX Item "tb"
  time base of the input video

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Set the hue to 90 degrees and the saturation to 1.0:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue=h=90:s=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Same command but expressing the hue in radians:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue=H=PI/2:s=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Rotate hue and make the saturation swing between 0
  and 2 over a period of 1 second:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue="H=2*PI*t: s=sin(2*PI*t)+1"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply a 3 seconds saturation fade-in effect starting at 0:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue="s=min(t/3\e,1)"
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The general fade-in expression can be written as:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue="s=min(0\e, max((t-START)/DURATION\e, 1))"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply a 3 seconds saturation fade-out effect starting at 5 seconds:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue="s=max(0\e, min(1\e, (8-t)/3))"
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The general fade-out expression can be written as:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          hue="s=max(0\e, min(1\e, (START+DURATION-t)/DURATION))"
  .Ve

_Commands_
.IX Subsection "Commands"

This filter supports the following commands:

* **b**  
  .IX Item "b"
* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
* **H**  
  .IX Item "H"
  Modify the hue and/or the saturation and/or brightness of the input video.
  The command accepts the same syntax of the corresponding option.
  .Sp
  If the specified expression is not valid, it is kept at its current
  value.

<a name="hysteresis"></a>

### hysteresis

.IX Subsection "hysteresis"
Grow first stream into second stream by connecting components.
This makes it possible to build more robust edge masks.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be
  copied from first stream.
  By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.
* **threshold**  
  .IX Item "threshold"
  Set threshold which is used in filtering. If pixel component value is higher than
  this value filter algorithm for connecting components is activated.
  By default value is 0.

<a name="idet"></a>

### idet

.IX Subsection "idet"
Detect video interlacing type.

This filter tries to detect if the input frames are interlaced, progressive,
top or bottom field first. It will also try to detect fields that are
repeated between adjacent frames (a sign of telecine).

Single frame detection considers only immediately adjacent frames when classifying each frame.
Multiple frame detection incorporates the classification history of previous frames.

The filter will log these metadata values:

* **single.current\_frame**  
  .IX Item "single.current_frame"
  Detected type of current frame using single-frame detection. One of:
  \`\`tff'' (top field first), \`\`bff'' (bottom field first),
  \`\`progressive'', or \`\`undetermined''
* **single.tff**  
  .IX Item "single.tff"
  Cumulative number of frames detected as top field first using single-frame detection.
* **multiple.tff**  
  .IX Item "multiple.tff"
  Cumulative number of frames detected as top field first using multiple-frame detection.
* **single.bff**  
  .IX Item "single.bff"
  Cumulative number of frames detected as bottom field first using single-frame detection.
* **multiple.current\_frame**  
  .IX Item "multiple.current_frame"
  Detected type of current frame using multiple-frame detection. One of:
  \`\`tff'' (top field first), \`\`bff'' (bottom field first),
  \`\`progressive'', or \`\`undetermined''
* **multiple.bff**  
  .IX Item "multiple.bff"
  Cumulative number of frames detected as bottom field first using multiple-frame detection.
* **single.progressive**  
  .IX Item "single.progressive"
  Cumulative number of frames detected as progressive using single-frame detection.
* **multiple.progressive**  
  .IX Item "multiple.progressive"
  Cumulative number of frames detected as progressive using multiple-frame detection.
* **single.undetermined**  
  .IX Item "single.undetermined"
  Cumulative number of frames that could not be classified using single-frame detection.
* **multiple.undetermined**  
  .IX Item "multiple.undetermined"
  Cumulative number of frames that could not be classified using multiple-frame detection.
* **repeated.current\_frame**  
  .IX Item "repeated.current_frame"
  Which field in the current frame is repeated from the last. One of \`\`neither'', \`\`top'', or \`\`bottom''.
* **repeated.neither**  
  .IX Item "repeated.neither"
  Cumulative number of frames with no repeated field.
* **repeated.top**  
  .IX Item "repeated.top"
  Cumulative number of frames with the top field repeated from the previous frame's top field.
* **repeated.bottom**  
  .IX Item "repeated.bottom"
  Cumulative number of frames with the bottom field repeated from the previous frame's bottom field.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **intl\_thres**  
  .IX Item "intl_thres"
  Set interlacing threshold.
* **prog\_thres**  
  .IX Item "prog_thres"
  Set progressive threshold.
* **rep\_thres**  
  .IX Item "rep_thres"
  Threshold for repeated field detection.
* **half\_life**  
  .IX Item "half_life"
  Number of frames after which a given frame's contribution to the
  statistics is halved (i.e., it contributes only 0.5 to its
  classification). The default of 0 means that all frames seen are given
  full weight of 1.0 forever.
* **analyze\_interlaced\_flag**  
  .IX Item "analyze_interlaced_flag"
  When this is not 0 then idet will use the specified number of frames to determine
  if the interlaced flag is accurate, it will not count undetermined frames.
  If the flag is found to be accurate it will be used without any further
  computations, if it is found to be inaccurate it will be cleared without any
  further computations. This allows inserting the idet filter as a low computational
  method to clean up the interlaced flag

<a name="il"></a>

### il

.IX Subsection "il"
Deinterleave or interleave fields.

This filter allows one to process interlaced images fields without
deinterlacing them. Deinterleaving splits the input frame into 2
fields (so called half pictures). Odd lines are moved to the top
half of the output image, even lines to the bottom half.
You can process (filter) them independently and then re-interleave them.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **luma_mode, l**  
  .IX Item "luma_mode, l"
* **chroma_mode, c**  
  .IX Item "chroma_mode, c"
* **alpha_mode, a**  
  .IX Item "alpha_mode, a"
  Available values for _luma\_mode_, _chroma\_mode_ and
  _alpha\_mode_ are:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Do nothing.
    * **deinterleave, d**  
      .IX Item "deinterleave, d"
      Deinterleave fields, placing one above the other.
    * **interleave, i**  
      .IX Item "interleave, i"
      Interleave fields. Reverse the effect of deinterleaving.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`none\*(C'.
* **luma_swap, ls**  
  .IX Item "luma_swap, ls"
* **chroma_swap, cs**  
  .IX Item "chroma_swap, cs"
* **alpha_swap, as**  
  .IX Item "alpha_swap, as"
  Swap luma/chroma/alpha fields. Exchange even & odd lines. Default value is \f(CW0.

<a name="inflate"></a>

### inflate

.IX Subsection "inflate"
Apply inflate effect to the video.

This filter replaces the pixel by the local(3x3) average by taking into account
only values higher than the pixel.

It accepts the following options:

* **threshold0**  
  .IX Item "threshold0"
* **threshold1**  
  .IX Item "threshold1"
* **threshold2**  
  .IX Item "threshold2"
* **threshold3**  
  .IX Item "threshold3"
  Limit the maximum change for each plane, default is 65535.
  If 0, plane will remain unchanged.

<a name="interlace"></a>

### interlace

.IX Subsection "interlace"
Simple interlacing filter from progressive contents. This interleaves upper (or
lower) lines from odd frames with lower (or upper) lines from even frames,
halving the frame rate and preserving image height.

.Vb 9
           Original        Original             New Frame
           Frame j\*(Aq      Frame \*(Aqj+1\*(Aq             (tff)
          ==========      ===========       ==================
            Line 0  --------------------&gt;    Frame j\*(Aq Line 0
            Line 1          Line 1  ----&gt;   Frame j+1\*(Aq Line 1
            Line 2 ---------------------&gt;    Frame j\*(Aq Line 2
            Line 3          Line 3  ----&gt;   Frame j+1\*(Aq Line 3
             ...             ...                   ...
        New Frame + 1 will be generated by Frame j+2\*(Aq and Frame \*(Aqj+3\*(Aq and so on
.Ve

It accepts the following optional parameters:

* **scan**  
  .IX Item "scan"
  This determines whether the interlaced frame is taken from the even
  (tff - default) or odd (bff) lines of the progressive frame.
* **lowpass**  
  .IX Item "lowpass"
  Vertical lowpass filter to avoid twitter interlacing and
  reduce moire patterns.
    * **0, off**  
      .IX Item "0, off"
      Disable vertical lowpass filter
    * **1, linear**  
      .IX Item "1, linear"
      Enable linear filter (default)
    * **2, complex**  
      .IX Item "2, complex"
      Enable complex filter. This will slightly less reduce twitter and moire
      but better retain detail and subjective sharpness impression.

<a name="kerndeint"></a>

### kerndeint

.IX Subsection "kerndeint"
Deinterlace input video by applying Donald Graft's adaptive kernel
deinterling. Work on interlaced parts of a video to produce
progressive frames.

The description of the accepted parameters follows.

* **thresh**  
  .IX Item "thresh"
  Set the threshold which affects the filter's tolerance when
  determining if a pixel line must be processed. It must be an integer
  in the range [0,255] and defaults to 10. A value of 0 will result in
  applying the process on every pixels.
* **map**  
  .IX Item "map"
  Paint pixels exceeding the threshold value to white if set to 1.
  Default is 0.
* **order**  
  .IX Item "order"
  Set the fields order. Swap fields if set to 1, leave fields alone if
  0. Default is 0.
* **sharp**  
  .IX Item "sharp"
  Enable additional sharpening if set to 1. Default is 0.
* **twoway**  
  .IX Item "twoway"
  Enable twoway sharpening if set to 1. Default is 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Apply default values:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          kerndeint=thresh=10:map=0:order=0:sharp=0:twoway=0
  .Ve
* ·  
  Enable additional sharpening:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          kerndeint=sharp=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Paint processed pixels in white:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          kerndeint=map=1
  .Ve

<a name="lenscorrection"></a>

### lenscorrection

.IX Subsection "lenscorrection"
Correct radial lens distortion

This filter can be used to correct for radial distortion as can result from the use
of wide angle lenses, and thereby re-rectify the image. To find the right parameters
one can use tools available for example as part of opencv or simply trial-and-error.
To use opencv use the calibration sample (under samples/cpp) from the opencv sources
and extract the k1 and k2 coefficients from the resulting matrix.

Note that effectively the same filter is available in the open-source tools Krita and
Digikam from the \s-1KDE\s0 project.

In contrast to the **vignette** filter, which can also be used to compensate lens errors,
this filter corrects the distortion of the image, whereas **vignette** corrects the
brightness distribution, so you may want to use both filters together in certain
cases, though you will have to take care of ordering, i.e. whether vignetting should
be applied before or after lens correction.

_Options_
.IX Subsection "Options"

The filter accepts the following options:

* **cx**  
  .IX Item "cx"
  Relative x-coordinate of the focal point of the image, and thereby the center of the
  distortion. This value has a range [0,1] and is expressed as fractions of the image
  width. Default is 0.5.
* **cy**  
  .IX Item "cy"
  Relative y-coordinate of the focal point of the image, and thereby the center of the
  distortion. This value has a range [0,1] and is expressed as fractions of the image
  height. Default is 0.5.
* **k1**  
  .IX Item "k1"
  Coefficient of the quadratic correction term. This value has a range [-1,1]. 0 means
  no correction. Default is 0.
* **k2**  
  .IX Item "k2"
  Coefficient of the double quadratic correction term. This value has a range [-1,1].
  0 means no correction. Default is 0.

The formula that generates the correction is:

_r\_src_ = _r\_tgt_ * (1 + _k1_ * (_r\_tgt_ / _r\_0_)^2 + _k2_ * (_r\_tgt_ / _r\_0_)^4)

where _r\_0_ is halve of the image diagonal and _r\_src_ and _r\_tgt_ are the
distances from the focal point in the source and target images, respectively.

<a name="lensfun"></a>

### lensfun

.IX Subsection "lensfun"
Apply lens correction via the lensfun library (&lt;**http://lensfun.sourceforge.net/**&gt;).

The \f(CW`lensfun\*(C' filter requires the camera make, camera model, and lens model
to apply the lens correction. The filter will load the lensfun database and
query it to find the corresponding camera and lens entries in the database. As
long as these entries can be found with the given options, the filter can
perform corrections on frames. Note that incomplete strings will result in the
filter choosing the best match with the given options, and the filter will
output the chosen camera and lens models (logged with level info\*(R"). You must
provide the make, camera model, and lens model as they are required.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **make**  
  .IX Item "make"
  The make of the camera (for example, Canon\*(R"). This option is required.
* **model**  
  .IX Item "model"
  The model of the camera (for example, Canon \s-1EOS 100D\*(R"\s0). This option is
  required.
* **lens\_model**  
  .IX Item "lens_model"
  The model of the lens (for example, Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 \s-1IS STM\*(R"\s0). This
  option is required.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  The type of correction to apply. The following values are valid options:
    * **vignetting**  
      .IX Item "vignetting"
      Enables fixing lens vignetting.
    * **geometry**  
      .IX Item "geometry"
      Enables fixing lens geometry. This is the default.
    * **subpixel**  
      .IX Item "subpixel"
      Enables fixing chromatic aberrations.
    * **vig\_geo**  
      .IX Item "vig_geo"
      Enables fixing lens vignetting and lens geometry.
    * **vig\_subpixel**  
      .IX Item "vig_subpixel"
      Enables fixing lens vignetting and chromatic aberrations.
    * **distortion**  
      .IX Item "distortion"
      Enables fixing both lens geometry and chromatic aberrations.
    * **all**  
      .IX Item "all"
      Enables all possible corrections.
* **focal\_length**  
  .IX Item "focal_length"
  The focal length of the image/video (zoom; expected constant for video). For
  example, a 18--55mm lens has focal length range of [18--55], so a value in that
  range should be chosen when using that lens. Default 18.
* **aperture**  
  .IX Item "aperture"
  The aperture of the image/video (expected constant for video). Note that
  aperture is only used for vignetting correction. Default 3.5.
* **focus\_distance**  
  .IX Item "focus_distance"
  The focus distance of the image/video (expected constant for video). Note that
  focus distance is only used for vignetting and only slightly affects the
  vignetting correction process. If unknown, leave it at the default value (which
  is 1000).
* **target\_geometry**  
  .IX Item "target_geometry"
  The target geometry of the output image/video. The following values are valid
  options:
    * **rectilinear (default)**  
      .IX Item "rectilinear (default)"
    * **fisheye**  
      .IX Item "fisheye"
    * **panoramic**  
      .IX Item "panoramic"
    * **equirectangular**  
      .IX Item "equirectangular"
    * **fisheye\_orthographic**  
      .IX Item "fisheye_orthographic"
    * **fisheye\_stereographic**  
      .IX Item "fisheye_stereographic"
    * **fisheye\_equisolid**  
      .IX Item "fisheye_equisolid"
    * **fisheye\_thoby**  
      .IX Item "fisheye_thoby"
* **reverse**  
  .IX Item "reverse"
  Apply the reverse of image correction (instead of correcting distortion, apply
  it).
* **interpolation**  
  .IX Item "interpolation"
  The type of interpolation used when correcting distortion. The following values
  are valid options:
    * **nearest**  
      .IX Item "nearest"
    * **linear (default)**  
      .IX Item "linear (default)"
    * **lanczos**  
      .IX Item "lanczos"

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Apply lens correction with make Canon\*(R", camera model \*(L"Canon \s-1EOS 100D\*(R",\s0 and lens
  model Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 \s-1IS STM\*(R"\s0 with focal length of \*(L"18\*(R" and
  aperture of 8.0\*(R".
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i input.mov -vf lensfun=make=Canon:model="Canon EOS 100D":lens_model="Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 IS STM":focal_length=18:aperture=8 -c:v h264 -b:v 8000k output.mov
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply the same as before, but only for the first 5 seconds of video.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i input.mov -vf lensfun=make=Canon:model="Canon EOS 100D":lens_model="Canon EF-S 18-55mm f/3.5-5.6 IS STM":focal_length=18:aperture=8:enable=lte(t\e,5)\*(Aq -c:v h264 -b:v 8000k output.mov
  .Ve

<a name="libvmaf"></a>

### libvmaf

.IX Subsection "libvmaf"
Obtain the \s-1VMAF\s0 (Video Multi-Method Assessment Fusion)
score between two input videos.

The obtained \s-1VMAF\s0 score is printed through the logging system.

It requires Netflix's vmaf library (libvmaf) as a pre-requisite.
After installing the library it can be enabled using:
\f(CW`./configure --enable-libvmaf --enable-version3\*(C'.
If no model path is specified it uses the default model: \f(CW`vmaf\_v0.6.1.pkl\*(C'.

The filter has following options:

* **model\_path**  
  .IX Item "model_path"
  Set the model path which is to be used for \s-1SVM.\s0
  Default value: \f(CW"vmaf\_v0.6.1.pkl"
* **log\_path**  
  .IX Item "log_path"
  Set the file path to be used to store logs.
* **log\_fmt**  
  .IX Item "log_fmt"
  Set the format of the log file (xml or json).
* **enable\_transform**  
  .IX Item "enable_transform"
  Enables transform for computing vmaf.
* **phone\_model**  
  .IX Item "phone_model"
  Invokes the phone model which will generate \s-1VMAF\s0 scores higher than in the
  regular model, which is more suitable for laptop, \s-1TV,\s0 etc. viewing conditions.
* **psnr**  
  .IX Item "psnr"
  Enables computing psnr along with vmaf.
* **ssim**  
  .IX Item "ssim"
  Enables computing ssim along with vmaf.
* **ms\_ssim**  
  .IX Item "ms_ssim"
  Enables computing ms_ssim along with vmaf.
* **pool**  
  .IX Item "pool"
  Set the pool method (mean, min or harmonic mean) to be used for computing vmaf.
* **n\_threads**  
  .IX Item "n_threads"
  Set number of threads to be used when computing vmaf.
* **n\_subsample**  
  .IX Item "n_subsample"
  Set interval for frame subsampling used when computing vmaf.
* **enable\_conf\_interval**  
  .IX Item "enable_conf_interval"
  Enables confidence interval.

This filter also supports the **framesync** options.

On the below examples the input file _main.mpg_ being processed is
compared with the reference file _ref.mpg_.

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi libvmaf -f null -
.Ve

Example with options:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi libvmaf="psnr=1:enable-transform=1" -f null -
.Ve

<a name="limiter"></a>

### limiter

.IX Subsection "limiter"
Limits the pixel components values to the specified range [min, max].

The filter accepts the following options:

* **min**  
  .IX Item "min"
  Lower bound. Defaults to the lowest allowed value for the input.
* **max**  
  .IX Item "max"
  Upper bound. Defaults to the highest allowed value for the input.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Specify which planes will be processed. Defaults to all available.

<a name="loop"></a>

### loop

.IX Subsection "loop"
Loop video frames.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **loop**  
  .IX Item "loop"
  Set the number of loops. Setting this value to -1 will result in infinite loops.
  Default is 0.
* **size**  
  .IX Item "size"
  Set maximal size in number of frames. Default is 0.
* **start**  
  .IX Item "start"
  Set first frame of loop. Default is 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Loop single first frame infinitely:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          loop=loop=-1:size=1:start=0
  .Ve
* ·  
  Loop single first frame 10 times:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          loop=loop=10:size=1:start=0
  .Ve
* ·  
  Loop 10 first frames 5 times:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          loop=loop=5:size=10:start=0
  .Ve

<a name="lut1d"></a>

### lut1d

.IX Subsection "lut1d"
Apply a 1D \s-1LUT\s0 to an input video.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **file**  
  .IX Item "file"
  Set the 1D \s-1LUT\s0 file name.
  .Sp
  Currently supported formats:
    * **cube**  
      .IX Item "cube"
      Iridas
* **interp**  
  .IX Item "interp"
  Select interpolation mode.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **nearest**  
      .IX Item "nearest"
      Use values from the nearest defined point.
    * **linear**  
      .IX Item "linear"
      Interpolate values using the linear interpolation.
    * **cubic**  
      .IX Item "cubic"
      Interpolate values using the cubic interpolation.

<a name="lut3d"></a>

### lut3d

.IX Subsection "lut3d"
Apply a 3D \s-1LUT\s0 to an input video.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **file**  
  .IX Item "file"
  Set the 3D \s-1LUT\s0 file name.
  .Sp
  Currently supported formats:
    * **3dl**  
      .IX Item "3dl"
      AfterEffects
    * **cube**  
      .IX Item "cube"
      Iridas
    * **dat**  
      .IX Item "dat"
      DaVinci
    * **m3d**  
      .IX Item "m3d"
      Pandora
* **interp**  
  .IX Item "interp"
  Select interpolation mode.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **nearest**  
      .IX Item "nearest"
      Use values from the nearest defined point.
    * **trilinear**  
      .IX Item "trilinear"
      Interpolate values using the 8 points defining a cube.
    * **tetrahedral**  
      .IX Item "tetrahedral"
      Interpolate values using a tetrahedron.

This filter also supports the **framesync** options.

<a name="lumakey"></a>

### lumakey

.IX Subsection "lumakey"
Turn certain luma values into transparency.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **threshold**  
  .IX Item "threshold"
  Set the luma which will be used as base for transparency.
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **tolerance**  
  .IX Item "tolerance"
  Set the range of luma values to be keyed out.
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **softness**  
  .IX Item "softness"
  Set the range of softness. Default value is \f(CW0.
  Use this to control gradual transition from zero to full transparency.

<a name="lut-lutrgb-lutyuv"></a>

### lut, lutrgb, lutyuv

.IX Subsection "lut, lutrgb, lutyuv"
Compute a look-up table for binding each pixel component input value
to an output value, and apply it to the input video.

_lutyuv_ applies a lookup table to a \s-1YUV\s0 input video, _lutrgb_
to an \s-1RGB\s0 input video.

These filters accept the following parameters:

* **c0**  
  .IX Item "c0"
  set first pixel component expression
* **c1**  
  .IX Item "c1"
  set second pixel component expression
* **c2**  
  .IX Item "c2"
  set third pixel component expression
* **c3**  
  .IX Item "c3"
  set fourth pixel component expression, corresponds to the alpha component
* **r**  
  .IX Item "r"
  set red component expression
* **g**  
  .IX Item "g"
  set green component expression
* **b**  
  .IX Item "b"
  set blue component expression
* **a**  
  .IX Item "a"
  alpha component expression
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  set Y/luminance component expression
* **u**  
  .IX Item "u"
  set U/Cb component expression
* **v**  
  .IX Item "v"
  set V/Cr component expression

Each of them specifies the expression to use for computing the lookup table for
the corresponding pixel component values.

The exact component associated to each of the _c*_ options depends on the
format in input.

The _lut_ filter requires either \s-1YUV\s0 or \s-1RGB\s0 pixel formats in input,
_lutrgb_ requires \s-1RGB\s0 pixel formats in input, and _lutyuv_ requires \s-1YUV.\s0

The expressions can contain the following constants and functions:

* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  The input width and height.
* **val**  
  .IX Item "val"
  The input value for the pixel component.
* **clipval**  
  .IX Item "clipval"
  The input value, clipped to the _minval_-_maxval_ range.
* **maxval**  
  .IX Item "maxval"
  The maximum value for the pixel component.
* **minval**  
  .IX Item "minval"
  The minimum value for the pixel component.
* **negval**  
  .IX Item "negval"
  The negated value for the pixel component value, clipped to the
  _minval_-_maxval_ range; it corresponds to the expression
  maxval-clipval+minval\*(R".
* **clip(val)**  
  .IX Item "clip(val)"
  The computed value in _val_, clipped to the
  _minval_-_maxval_ range.
* **gammaval(gamma)**  
  .IX Item "gammaval(gamma)"
  The computed gamma correction value of the pixel component value,
  clipped to the _minval_-_maxval_ range. It corresponds to the
  expression
  "pow((clipval-minval)/(maxval-minval)\e,_gamma_)*(maxval-minval)+minval"

All expressions default to val\*(R".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Negate input video:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          lutrgb="r=maxval+minval-val:g=maxval+minval-val:b=maxval+minval-val"
          lutyuv="y=maxval+minval-val:u=maxval+minval-val:v=maxval+minval-val"
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The above is the same as:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          lutrgb="r=negval:g=negval:b=negval"
          lutyuv="y=negval:u=negval:v=negval"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Negate luminance:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutyuv=y=negval
  .Ve
* ·  
  Remove chroma components, turning the video into a graytone image:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutyuv="u=128:v=128"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply a luma burning effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutyuv="y=2*val"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Remove green and blue components:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutrgb="g=0:b=0"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set a constant alpha channel value on input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=rgba,lutrgb=a="maxval-minval/2"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Correct luminance gamma by a factor of 0.5:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutyuv=y=gammaval(0.5)
  .Ve
* ·  
  Discard least significant bits of luma:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutyuv=y=bitand(val, 128+64+32)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Technicolor like effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lutyuv=u=(val-maxval/2)*2+maxval/2\*(Aq:v=\*(Aq(val-maxval/2)*2+maxval/2\*(Aq
  .Ve

<a name="lut2-tlut2"></a>

### lut2, tlut2

.IX Subsection "lut2, tlut2"
The \f(CW`lut2\*(C' filter takes two input streams and outputs one
stream.

The \f(CW`tlut2\*(C' (time lut2) filter takes two consecutive frames
from one single stream.

This filter accepts the following parameters:

* **c0**  
  .IX Item "c0"
  set first pixel component expression
* **c1**  
  .IX Item "c1"
  set second pixel component expression
* **c2**  
  .IX Item "c2"
  set third pixel component expression
* **c3**  
  .IX Item "c3"
  set fourth pixel component expression, corresponds to the alpha component

Each of them specifies the expression to use for computing the lookup table for
the corresponding pixel component values.

The exact component associated to each of the _c*_ options depends on the
format in inputs.

The expressions can contain the following constants:

* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  The input width and height.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
  The first input value for the pixel component.
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  The second input value for the pixel component.
* **bdx**  
  .IX Item "bdx"
  The first input video bit depth.
* **bdy**  
  .IX Item "bdy"
  The second input video bit depth.

All expressions default to x\*(R".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Highlight differences between two \s-1RGB\s0 video streams:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lut2=ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Highlight differences between two \s-1YUV\s0 video streams:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lut2=ifnot(x-y,0,pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,pow(2,bdx-1),pow(2,bdx)-1):ifnot(x-y,pow(2,bdx-1),pow(2,bdx)-1)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Show max difference between two video streams:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          lut2=if(lt(x,y),0,if(gt(x,y),pow(2,bdx)-1,pow(2,bdx-1))):if(lt(x,y),0,if(gt(x,y),pow(2,bdx)-1,pow(2,bdx-1))):if(lt(x,y),0,if(gt(x,y),pow(2,bdx)-1,pow(2,bdx-1)))\*(Aq
  .Ve

<a name="maskedclamp"></a>

### maskedclamp

.IX Subsection "maskedclamp"
Clamp the first input stream with the second input and third input stream.

Returns the value of first stream to be between second input
stream - \f(CW`undershoot\*(C' and third input stream + \f(CW\*(C\`overshoot\*(C'.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **undershoot**  
  .IX Item "undershoot"
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **overshoot**  
  .IX Item "overshoot"
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be
  copied from first stream.
  By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.

<a name="maskedmerge"></a>

### maskedmerge

.IX Subsection "maskedmerge"
Merge the first input stream with the second input stream using per pixel
weights in the third input stream.

A value of 0 in the third stream pixel component means that pixel component
from first stream is returned unchanged, while maximum value (eg. 255 for
8-bit videos) means that pixel component from second stream is returned
unchanged. Intermediate values define the amount of merging between both
input stream's pixel components.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes will be processed as bitmap, unprocessed planes will be
  copied from first stream.
  By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.

<a name="mcdeint"></a>

### mcdeint

.IX Subsection "mcdeint"
Apply motion-compensation deinterlacing.

It needs one field per frame as input and must thus be used together
with yadif=1/3 or equivalent.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set the deinterlacing mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts one of the following values:
    * **fast**  
      .IX Item "fast"
    * **medium**  
      .IX Item "medium"
    * **slow**  
      .IX Item "slow"
      use iterative motion estimation
    * **extra\_slow**  
      .IX Item "extra_slow"
      like **slow**, but use multiple reference frames.
      .Sp
      Default value is **fast**.
* **parity**  
  .IX Item "parity"
  Set the picture field parity assumed for the input video. It must be
  one of the following values:
    * **0, tff**  
      .IX Item "0, tff"
      assume top field first
    * **1, bff**  
      .IX Item "1, bff"
      assume bottom field first
      .Sp
      Default value is **bff**.
* **qp**  
  .IX Item "qp"
  Set per-block quantization parameter (\s-1QP\s0) used by the internal
  encoder.
  .Sp
  Higher values should result in a smoother motion vector field but less
  optimal individual vectors. Default value is 1.

<a name="mergeplanes"></a>

### mergeplanes

.IX Subsection "mergeplanes"
Merge color channel components from several video streams.

The filter accepts up to 4 input streams, and merge selected input
planes to the output video.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **mapping**  
  .IX Item "mapping"
  Set input to output plane mapping. Default is \f(CW0.
  .Sp
  The mappings is specified as a bitmap. It should be specified as a
  hexadecimal number in the form 0xAa[Bb[Cc[Dd]]]. 'Aa' describes the
  mapping for the first plane of the output stream. 'A' sets the number of
  the input stream to use (from 0 to 3), and 'a' the plane number of the
  corresponding input to use (from 0 to 3). The rest of the mappings is
  similar, 'Bb' describes the mapping for the output stream second
  plane, 'Cc' describes the mapping for the output stream third plane and
  'Dd' describes the mapping for the output stream fourth plane.
* **format**  
  .IX Item "format"
  Set output pixel format. Default is \f(CW`yuva444p\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Merge three gray video streams of same width and height into single video stream:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          [a0][a1][a2]mergeplanes=0x001020:yuv444p
  .Ve
* ·  
  Merge 1st yuv444p stream and 2nd gray video stream into yuva444p video stream:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          [a0][a1]mergeplanes=0x00010210:yuva444p
  .Ve
* ·  
  Swap Y and A plane in yuva444p stream:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=yuva444p,mergeplanes=0x03010200:yuva444p
  .Ve
* ·  
  Swap U and V plane in yuv420p stream:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=yuv420p,mergeplanes=0x000201:yuv420p
  .Ve
* ·  
  Cast a rgb24 clip to yuv444p:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          format=rgb24,mergeplanes=0x000102:yuv444p
  .Ve

<a name="mestimate"></a>

### mestimate

.IX Subsection "mestimate"
Estimate and export motion vectors using block matching algorithms.
Motion vectors are stored in frame side data to be used by other filters.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **method**  
  .IX Item "method"
  Specify the motion estimation method. Accepts one of the following values:
    * **esa**  
      .IX Item "esa"
      Exhaustive search algorithm.
    * **tss**  
      .IX Item "tss"
      Three step search algorithm.
    * **tdls**  
      .IX Item "tdls"
      Two dimensional logarithmic search algorithm.
    * **ntss**  
      .IX Item "ntss"
      New three step search algorithm.
    * **fss**  
      .IX Item "fss"
      Four step search algorithm.
    * **ds**  
      .IX Item "ds"
      Diamond search algorithm.
    * **hexbs**  
      .IX Item "hexbs"
      Hexagon-based search algorithm.
    * **epzs**  
      .IX Item "epzs"
      Enhanced predictive zonal search algorithm.
    * **umh**  
      .IX Item "umh"
      Uneven multi-hexagon search algorithm.
      .Sp
      Default value is **esa**.
* **mb\_size**  
  .IX Item "mb_size"
  Macroblock size. Default \f(CW16.
* **search\_param**  
  .IX Item "search_param"
  Search parameter. Default \f(CW7.

<a name="midequalizer"></a>

### midequalizer

.IX Subsection "midequalizer"
Apply Midway Image Equalization effect using two video streams.

Midway Image Equalization adjusts a pair of images to have the same
histogram, while maintaining their dynamics as much as possible. It's
useful for e.g. matching exposures from a pair of stereo cameras.

This filter has two inputs and one output, which must be of same pixel format, but
may be of different sizes. The output of filter is first input adjusted with
midway histogram of both inputs.

This filter accepts the following option:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes to process. Default is \f(CW15, which is all available planes.

<a name="minterpolate"></a>

### minterpolate

.IX Subsection "minterpolate"
Convert the video to specified frame rate using motion interpolation.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **fps**  
  .IX Item "fps"
  Specify the output frame rate. This can be rational e.g. \f(CW`60000/1001\*(C'. Frames are dropped if _fps_ is lower than source fps. Default \f(CW60.
* **mi\_mode**  
  .IX Item "mi_mode"
  Motion interpolation mode. Following values are accepted:
    * **dup**  
      .IX Item "dup"
      Duplicate previous or next frame for interpolating new ones.
    * **blend**  
      .IX Item "blend"
      Blend source frames. Interpolated frame is mean of previous and next frames.
    * **mci**  
      .IX Item "mci"
      Motion compensated interpolation. Following options are effective when this mode is selected:
        * **mc\_mode**  
          .IX Item "mc_mode"
          Motion compensation mode. Following values are accepted:
            * **obmc**  
              .IX Item "obmc"
              Overlapped block motion compensation.
            * **aobmc**  
              .IX Item "aobmc"
              Adaptive overlapped block motion compensation. Window weighting coefficients are controlled adaptively according to the reliabilities of the neighboring motion vectors to reduce oversmoothing.
              .Sp
              Default mode is **obmc**.
        * **me\_mode**  
          .IX Item "me_mode"
          Motion estimation mode. Following values are accepted:
            * **bidir**  
              .IX Item "bidir"
              Bidirectional motion estimation. Motion vectors are estimated for each source frame in both forward and backward directions.
            * **bilat**  
              .IX Item "bilat"
              Bilateral motion estimation. Motion vectors are estimated directly for interpolated frame.
              .Sp
              Default mode is **bilat**.
        * **me**  
          .IX Item "me"
          The algorithm to be used for motion estimation. Following values are accepted:
            * **esa**  
              .IX Item "esa"
              Exhaustive search algorithm.
            * **tss**  
              .IX Item "tss"
              Three step search algorithm.
            * **tdls**  
              .IX Item "tdls"
              Two dimensional logarithmic search algorithm.
            * **ntss**  
              .IX Item "ntss"
              New three step search algorithm.
            * **fss**  
              .IX Item "fss"
              Four step search algorithm.
            * **ds**  
              .IX Item "ds"
              Diamond search algorithm.
            * **hexbs**  
              .IX Item "hexbs"
              Hexagon-based search algorithm.
            * **epzs**  
              .IX Item "epzs"
              Enhanced predictive zonal search algorithm.
            * **umh**  
              .IX Item "umh"
              Uneven multi-hexagon search algorithm.
              .Sp
              Default algorithm is **epzs**.
        * **mb\_size**  
          .IX Item "mb_size"
          Macroblock size. Default \f(CW16.
        * **search\_param**  
          .IX Item "search_param"
          Motion estimation search parameter. Default \f(CW32.
        * **vsbmc**  
          .IX Item "vsbmc"
          Enable variable-size block motion compensation. Motion estimation is applied with smaller block sizes at object boundaries in order to make the them less blur. Default is \f(CW0 (disabled).
* **scd**  
  .IX Item "scd"
  Scene change detection method. Scene change leads motion vectors to be in random direction. Scene change detection replace interpolated frames by duplicate ones. May not be needed for other modes. Following values are accepted:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Disable scene change detection.
    * **fdiff**  
      .IX Item "fdiff"
      Frame difference. Corresponding pixel values are compared and if it satisfies _scd\_threshold_ scene change is detected.
      .Sp
      Default method is **fdiff**.
* **scd\_threshold**  
  .IX Item "scd_threshold"
  Scene change detection threshold. Default is \f(CW5.0.

<a name="mix"></a>

### mix

.IX Subsection "mix"
Mix several video input streams into one video stream.

A description of the accepted options follows.

* **nb\_inputs**  
  .IX Item "nb_inputs"
  The number of inputs. If unspecified, it defaults to 2.
* **weights**  
  .IX Item "weights"
  Specify weight of each input video stream as sequence.
  Each weight is separated by space. If number of weights
  is smaller than number of _frames_ last specified
  weight will be used for all remaining unset weights.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Specify scale, if it is set it will be multiplied with sum
  of each weight multiplied with pixel values to give final destination
  pixel value. By default _scale_ is auto scaled to sum of weights.
* **duration**  
  .IX Item "duration"
  Specify how end of stream is determined.
    * **longest**  
      .IX Item "longest"
      The duration of the longest input. (default)
    * **shortest**  
      .IX Item "shortest"
      The duration of the shortest input.
    * **first**  
      .IX Item "first"
      The duration of the first input.

<a name="mpdecimate"></a>

### mpdecimate

.IX Subsection "mpdecimate"
Drop frames that do not differ greatly from the previous frame in
order to reduce frame rate.

The main use of this filter is for very-low-bitrate encoding
(e.g. streaming over dialup modem), but it could in theory be used for
fixing movies that were inverse-telecined incorrectly.

A description of the accepted options follows.

* **max**  
  .IX Item "max"
  Set the maximum number of consecutive frames which can be dropped (if
  positive), or the minimum interval between dropped frames (if
  negative). If the value is 0, the frame is dropped disregarding the
  number of previous sequentially dropped frames.
  .Sp
  Default value is 0.
* **hi**  
  .IX Item "hi"
* **lo**  
  .IX Item "lo"
* **frac**  
  .IX Item "frac"
  Set the dropping threshold values.
  .Sp
  Values for **hi** and **lo** are for 8x8 pixel blocks and
  represent actual pixel value differences, so a threshold of 64
  corresponds to 1 unit of difference for each pixel, or the same spread
  out differently over the block.
  .Sp
  A frame is a candidate for dropping if no 8x8 blocks differ by more
  than a threshold of **hi**, and if no more than **frac** blocks (1
  meaning the whole image) differ by more than a threshold of **lo**.
  .Sp
  Default value for **hi** is 64*12, default value for **lo** is
  64*5, and default value for **frac** is 0.33.

<a name="negate"></a>

### negate

.IX Subsection "negate"
Negate (invert) the input video.

It accepts the following option:

* **negate\_alpha**  
  .IX Item "negate_alpha"
  With value 1, it negates the alpha component, if present. Default value is 0.

<a name="nlmeans"></a>

### nlmeans

.IX Subsection "nlmeans"
Denoise frames using Non-Local Means algorithm.

Each pixel is adjusted by looking for other pixels with similar contexts. This
context similarity is defined by comparing their surrounding patches of size
**p**x**p**. Patches are searched in an area of **r**x**r**
around the pixel.

Note that the research area defines centers for patches, which means some
patches will be made of pixels outside that research area.

The filter accepts the following options.

* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
  Set denoising strength.
* **p**  
  .IX Item "p"
  Set patch size.
* **pc**  
  .IX Item "pc"
  Same as **p** but for chroma planes.
  .Sp
  The default value is _0_ and means automatic.
* **r**  
  .IX Item "r"
  Set research size.
* **rc**  
  .IX Item "rc"
  Same as **r** but for chroma planes.
  .Sp
  The default value is _0_ and means automatic.

<a name="nnedi"></a>

### nnedi

.IX Subsection "nnedi"
Deinterlace video using neural network edge directed interpolation.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **weights**  
  .IX Item "weights"
  Mandatory option, without binary file filter can not work.
  Currently file can be found here:
  https://github.com/dubhater/vapoursynth-nnedi3/blob/master/src/nnedi3_weights.bin
* **deint**  
  .IX Item "deint"
  Set which frames to deinterlace, by default it is \f(CW`all\*(C'.
  Can be \f(CW`all\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`interlaced\*(C'.
* **field**  
  .IX Item "field"
  Set mode of operation.
  .Sp
  Can be one of the following:
    * **af**  
      .IX Item "af"
      Use frame flags, both fields.
    * **a**  
      .IX Item "a"
      Use frame flags, single field.
    * **t**  
      .IX Item "t"
      Use top field only.
    * **b**  
      .IX Item "b"
      Use bottom field only.
    * **tf**  
      .IX Item "tf"
      Use both fields, top first.
    * **bf**  
      .IX Item "bf"
      Use both fields, bottom first.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes to process, by default filter process all frames.
* **nsize**  
  .IX Item "nsize"
  Set size of local neighborhood around each pixel, used by the predictor neural
  network.
  .Sp
  Can be one of the following:
    * **s8x6**  
      .IX Item "s8x6"
    * **s16x6**  
      .IX Item "s16x6"
    * **s32x6**  
      .IX Item "s32x6"
    * **s48x6**  
      .IX Item "s48x6"
    * **s8x4**  
      .IX Item "s8x4"
    * **s16x4**  
      .IX Item "s16x4"
    * **s32x4**  
      .IX Item "s32x4"
* **nns**  
  .IX Item "nns"
  Set the number of neurons in predictor neural network.
  Can be one of the following:
    * **n16**  
      .IX Item "n16"
    * **n32**  
      .IX Item "n32"
    * **n64**  
      .IX Item "n64"
    * **n128**  
      .IX Item "n128"
    * **n256**  
      .IX Item "n256"
* **qual**  
  .IX Item "qual"
  Controls the number of different neural network predictions that are blended
  together to compute the final output value. Can be \f(CW`fast\*(C', default or
  \f(CW`slow\*(C'.
* **etype**  
  .IX Item "etype"
  Set which set of weights to use in the predictor.
  Can be one of the following:
    * **a**  
      .IX Item "a"
      weights trained to minimize absolute error
    * **s**  
      .IX Item "s"
      weights trained to minimize squared error
* **pscrn**  
  .IX Item "pscrn"
  Controls whether or not the prescreener neural network is used to decide
  which pixels should be processed by the predictor neural network and which
  can be handled by simple cubic interpolation.
  The prescreener is trained to know whether cubic interpolation will be
  sufficient for a pixel or whether it should be predicted by the predictor nn.
  The computational complexity of the prescreener nn is much less than that of
  the predictor nn. Since most pixels can be handled by cubic interpolation,
  using the prescreener generally results in much faster processing.
  The prescreener is pretty accurate, so the difference between using it and not
  using it is almost always unnoticeable.
  .Sp
  Can be one of the following:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
    * **original**  
      .IX Item "original"
    * **new**  
      .IX Item "new"
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`new\*(C'.
* **fapprox**  
  .IX Item "fapprox"
  Set various debugging flags.

<a name="noformat"></a>

### noformat

.IX Subsection "noformat"
Force libavfilter not to use any of the specified pixel formats for the
input to the next filter.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **pix\_fmts**  
  .IX Item "pix_fmts"
  A '|'-separated list of pixel format names, such as
  pix_fmts=yuv420p|monow|rgb24".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Force libavfilter to use a format different from _yuv420p_ for the
  input to the vflip filter:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          noformat=pix_fmts=yuv420p,vflip
  .Ve
* ·  
  Convert the input video to any of the formats not contained in the list:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          noformat=yuv420p|yuv444p|yuv410p
  .Ve

<a name="noise"></a>

### noise

.IX Subsection "noise"
Add noise on video input frame.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **all\_seed**  
  .IX Item "all_seed"
* **c0\_seed**  
  .IX Item "c0_seed"
* **c1\_seed**  
  .IX Item "c1_seed"
* **c2\_seed**  
  .IX Item "c2_seed"
* **c3\_seed**  
  .IX Item "c3_seed"
  Set noise seed for specific pixel component or all pixel components in case
  of _all\_seed_. Default value is \f(CW123457.
* **all_strength, alls**  
  .IX Item "all_strength, alls"
* **c0_strength, c0s**  
  .IX Item "c0_strength, c0s"
* **c1_strength, c1s**  
  .IX Item "c1_strength, c1s"
* **c2_strength, c2s**  
  .IX Item "c2_strength, c2s"
* **c3_strength, c3s**  
  .IX Item "c3_strength, c3s"
  Set noise strength for specific pixel component or all pixel components in case
