  \f(CW`(main_w / main_h) * main\_sar\*(C'.
* _main\_hsub_  
  .IX Item "main_hsub"
* _main\_vsub_  
  .IX Item "main_vsub"
  The main input video's horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values.
  For example for the pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_
  is 1.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Scale a subtitle stream (b) to match the main video (a) in size before overlaying
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          scale2ref[b][a];[a][b]overlay\*(Aq
  .Ve

<a name="selectivecolor"></a>

### selectivecolor

.IX Subsection "selectivecolor"
Adjust cyan, magenta, yellow and black (\s-1CMYK\s0) to certain ranges of colors (such
as reds\*(R", \*(L"yellows\*(R", \*(L"greens\*(R", \*(L"cyans\*(R", ...). The adjustment range is defined
by the purity\*(R" of the color (that is, how saturated it already is).

This filter is similar to the Adobe Photoshop Selective Color tool.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **correction\_method**  
  .IX Item "correction_method"
  Select color correction method.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **absolute**  
      .IX Item "absolute"
      Specified adjustments are applied as-is\*(R" (added/subtracted to original pixel
      component value).
    * **relative**  
      .IX Item "relative"
      Specified adjustments are relative to the original component value.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`absolute\*(C'.
* **reds**  
  .IX Item "reds"
  Adjustments for red pixels (pixels where the red component is the maximum)
* **yellows**  
  .IX Item "yellows"
  Adjustments for yellow pixels (pixels where the blue component is the minimum)
* **greens**  
  .IX Item "greens"
  Adjustments for green pixels (pixels where the green component is the maximum)
* **cyans**  
  .IX Item "cyans"
  Adjustments for cyan pixels (pixels where the red component is the minimum)
* **blues**  
  .IX Item "blues"
  Adjustments for blue pixels (pixels where the blue component is the maximum)
* **magentas**  
  .IX Item "magentas"
  Adjustments for magenta pixels (pixels where the green component is the minimum)
* **whites**  
  .IX Item "whites"
  Adjustments for white pixels (pixels where all components are greater than 128)
* **neutrals**  
  .IX Item "neutrals"
  Adjustments for all pixels except pure black and pure white
* **blacks**  
  .IX Item "blacks"
  Adjustments for black pixels (pixels where all components are lesser than 128)
* **psfile**  
  .IX Item "psfile"
  Specify a Photoshop selective color file (\f(CW`.asv\*(C') to import the settings from.

All the adjustment settings (**reds**, **yellows**, ...) accept up to
4 space separated floating point adjustment values in the [-1,1] range,
respectively to adjust the amount of cyan, magenta, yellow and black for the
pixels of its range.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Increase cyan by 50% and reduce yellow by 33% in every green areas, and
  increase magenta by 27% in blue areas:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          selectivecolor=greens=.5 0 -.33 0:blues=0 .27
  .Ve
* ·  
  Use a Photoshop selective color preset:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          selectivecolor=psfile=MySelectiveColorPresets/Misty.asv
  .Ve

<a name="separatefields"></a>

### separatefields

.IX Subsection "separatefields"
The \f(CW`separatefields\*(C' takes a frame-based video input and splits
each frame into its components fields, producing a new half height clip
with twice the frame rate and twice the frame count.

This filter use field-dominance information in frame to decide which
of each pair of fields to place first in the output.
If it gets it wrong use **setfield** filter before \f(CW`separatefields\*(C' filter.

<a name="setdar-setsar"></a>

### setdar, setsar

.IX Subsection "setdar, setsar"
The \f(CW`setdar\*(C' filter sets the Display Aspect Ratio for the filter
output video.

This is done by changing the specified Sample (aka Pixel) Aspect
Ratio, according to the following equation:

.Vb 1
        &lt;DAR&gt; = &lt;HORIZONTAL_RESOLUTION&gt; / &lt;VERTICAL_RESOLUTION&gt; * &lt;SAR&gt;
.Ve

Keep in mind that the \f(CW`setdar\*(C' filter does not modify the pixel
dimensions of the video frame. Also, the display aspect ratio set by
this filter may be changed by later filters in the filterchain,
e.g. in case of scaling or if another setdar\*(R" or a \*(L"setsar\*(R" filter is
applied.

The \f(CW`setsar\*(C' filter sets the Sample (aka Pixel) Aspect Ratio for
the filter output video.

Note that as a consequence of the application of this filter, the
output display aspect ratio will change according to the equation
above.

Keep in mind that the sample aspect ratio set by the \f(CW`setsar\*(C'
filter may be changed by later filters in the filterchain, e.g. if
another setsar\*(R" or a \*(L"setdar\*(R" filter is applied.

It accepts the following parameters:
.ie n .IP "**r, ratio, dar (\f(CB""setdar"" only), sar (\f(CB""setsar"" only)**" 4
.el .IP "**r, ratio, dar (\f(CBsetdar only), sar (\f(CBsetsar only)**" 4
.IX Item "r, ratio, dar (setdar only), sar (setsar only)"
Set the aspect ratio used by the filter.
.Sp
The parameter can be a floating point number string, an expression, or
a string of the form _num_:_den_, where _num_ and
_den_ are the numerator and denominator of the aspect ratio. If
the parameter is not specified, it is assumed the value 0\*(R".
In case the form "_num_:_den_" is used, the \f(CW`:\*(C' character
should be escaped.

* **max**  
  .IX Item "max"
  Set the maximum integer value to use for expressing numerator and
  denominator when reducing the expressed aspect ratio to a rational.
  Default value is \f(CW100.

The parameter _sar_ is an expression containing
the following constants:

* **E, \s-1PI, PHI\s0**  
  .IX Item "E, PI, PHI"
  These are approximated values for the mathematical constants e
  (Euler's number), pi (Greek pi), and phi (the golden ratio).
* **w, h**  
  .IX Item "w, h"
  The input width and height.
* **a**  
  .IX Item "a"
  These are the same as _w_ / _h_.
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  The input sample aspect ratio.
* **dar**  
  .IX Item "dar"
  The input display aspect ratio. It is the same as
  (_w_ / _h_) * _sar_.
* **hsub, vsub**  
  .IX Item "hsub, vsub"
  Horizontal and vertical chroma subsample values. For example, for the
  pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_ is 1.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  To change the display aspect ratio to 16:9, specify one of the following:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          setdar=dar=1.77777
          setdar=dar=16/9
  .Ve
* ·  
  To change the sample aspect ratio to 10:11, specify:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setsar=sar=10/11
  .Ve
* ·  
  To set a display aspect ratio of 16:9, and specify a maximum integer value of
  1000 in the aspect ratio reduction, use the command:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setdar=ratio=16/9:max=1000
  .Ve

<a name="setfield"></a>

### setfield

.IX Subsection "setfield"
Force field for the output video frame.

The \f(CW`setfield\*(C' filter marks the interlace type field for the
output frames. It does not change the input frame, but only sets the
corresponding property, which affects how the frame is treated by
following filters (e.g. \f(CW`fieldorder\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`yadif\*(C').

The filter accepts the following options:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same field property.
    * **bff**  
      .IX Item "bff"
      Mark the frame as bottom-field-first.
    * **tff**  
      .IX Item "tff"
      Mark the frame as top-field-first.
    * **prog**  
      .IX Item "prog"
      Mark the frame as progressive.

<a name="setparams"></a>

### setparams

.IX Subsection "setparams"
Force frame parameter for the output video frame.

The \f(CW`setparams\*(C' filter marks interlace and color range for the
output frames. It does not change the input frame, but only sets the
corresponding property, which affects how the frame is treated by
filters/encoders.

* **field\_mode**  
  .IX Item "field_mode"
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same field property (default).
    * **bff**  
      .IX Item "bff"
      Mark the frame as bottom-field-first.
    * **tff**  
      .IX Item "tff"
      Mark the frame as top-field-first.
    * **prog**  
      .IX Item "prog"
      Mark the frame as progressive.
* **range**  
  .IX Item "range"
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same color range property (default).
    * **unspecified, unknown**  
      .IX Item "unspecified, unknown"
      Mark the frame as unspecified color range.
    * **limited, tv, mpeg**  
      .IX Item "limited, tv, mpeg"
      Mark the frame as limited range.
    * **full, pc, jpeg**  
      .IX Item "full, pc, jpeg"
      Mark the frame as full range.
* **color\_primaries**  
  .IX Item "color_primaries"
  Set the color primaries.
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same color primaries property (default).
    * **bt709**  
      .IX Item "bt709"
    * **unknown**  
      .IX Item "unknown"
    * **bt470m**  
      .IX Item "bt470m"
    * **bt470bg**  
      .IX Item "bt470bg"
    * **smpte170m**  
      .IX Item "smpte170m"
    * **smpte240m**  
      .IX Item "smpte240m"
    * **film**  
      .IX Item "film"
    * **bt2020**  
      .IX Item "bt2020"
    * **smpte428**  
      .IX Item "smpte428"
    * **smpte431**  
      .IX Item "smpte431"
    * **smpte432**  
      .IX Item "smpte432"
    * **jedec-p22**  
      .IX Item "jedec-p22"
* **color\_trc**  
  .IX Item "color_trc"
  Set the color transfert.
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same color trc property (default).
    * **bt709**  
      .IX Item "bt709"
    * **unknown**  
      .IX Item "unknown"
    * **bt470m**  
      .IX Item "bt470m"
    * **bt470bg**  
      .IX Item "bt470bg"
    * **smpte170m**  
      .IX Item "smpte170m"
    * **smpte240m**  
      .IX Item "smpte240m"
    * **linear**  
      .IX Item "linear"
    * **log100**  
      .IX Item "log100"
    * **log316**  
      .IX Item "log316"
    * **iec61966-2-4**  
      .IX Item "iec61966-2-4"
    * **bt1361e**  
      .IX Item "bt1361e"
    * **iec61966-2-1**  
      .IX Item "iec61966-2-1"
    * **bt2020-10**  
      .IX Item "bt2020-10"
    * **bt2020-12**  
      .IX Item "bt2020-12"
    * **smpte2084**  
      .IX Item "smpte2084"
    * **smpte428**  
      .IX Item "smpte428"
    * **arib-std-b67**  
      .IX Item "arib-std-b67"
* **colorspace**  
  .IX Item "colorspace"
  Set the colorspace.
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same colorspace property (default).
    * **gbr**  
      .IX Item "gbr"
    * **bt709**  
      .IX Item "bt709"
    * **unknown**  
      .IX Item "unknown"
    * **fcc**  
      .IX Item "fcc"
    * **bt470bg**  
      .IX Item "bt470bg"
    * **smpte170m**  
      .IX Item "smpte170m"
    * **smpte240m**  
      .IX Item "smpte240m"
    * **ycgco**  
      .IX Item "ycgco"
    * **bt2020nc**  
      .IX Item "bt2020nc"
    * **bt2020c**  
      .IX Item "bt2020c"
    * **smpte2085**  
      .IX Item "smpte2085"
    * **chroma-derived-nc**  
      .IX Item "chroma-derived-nc"
    * **chroma-derived-c**  
      .IX Item "chroma-derived-c"
    * **ictcp**  
      .IX Item "ictcp"

<a name="showinfo"></a>

### showinfo

.IX Subsection "showinfo"
Show a line containing various information for each input video frame.
The input video is not modified.

The shown line contains a sequence of key/value pairs of the form
_key_:_value_.

The following values are shown in the output:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  The (sequential) number of the input frame, starting from 0.
* **pts**  
  .IX Item "pts"
  The Presentation TimeStamp of the input frame, expressed as a number of
  time base units. The time base unit depends on the filter input pad.
* **pts\_time**  
  .IX Item "pts_time"
  The Presentation TimeStamp of the input frame, expressed as a number of
  seconds.
* **pos**  
  .IX Item "pos"
  The position of the frame in the input stream, or -1 if this information is
  unavailable and/or meaningless (for example in case of synthetic video).
* **fmt**  
  .IX Item "fmt"
  The pixel format name.
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  The sample aspect ratio of the input frame, expressed in the form
  _num_/_den_.
* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
  The size of the input frame. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
* **i**  
  .IX Item "i"
  The type of interlaced mode (P\*(R" for \*(L"progressive\*(R", \*(L"T\*(R" for top field first, \*(L"B\*(R"
  for bottom field first).
* **iskey**  
  .IX Item "iskey"
  This is 1 if the frame is a key frame, 0 otherwise.
* **type**  
  .IX Item "type"
  The picture type of the input frame (I\*(R" for an I-frame, \*(L"P\*(R" for a
  P-frame, B\*(R" for a B-frame, or \*(L"?\*(R" for an unknown type).
  Also refer to the documentation of the \f(CW`AVPictureType\*(C' enum and of
  the \f(CW`av\_get\_picture\_type\_char\*(C' function defined in
  _libavutil/avutil.h_.
* **checksum**  
  .IX Item "checksum"
  The Adler-32 checksum (printed in hexadecimal) of all the planes of the input frame.
* **plane\_checksum**  
  .IX Item "plane_checksum"
  The Adler-32 checksum (printed in hexadecimal) of each plane of the input frame,
  expressed in the form "[_c0_ _c1_ _c2_ _c3_]".

<a name="showpalette"></a>

### showpalette

.IX Subsection "showpalette"
Displays the 256 colors palette of each frame. This filter is only relevant for
_pal8_ pixel format frames.

It accepts the following option:

* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
  Set the size of the box used to represent one palette color entry. Default is
  \f(CW30 (for a \f(CW`30x30\*(C' pixel box).

<a name="shuffleframes"></a>

### shuffleframes

.IX Subsection "shuffleframes"
Reorder and/or duplicate and/or drop video frames.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **mapping**  
  .IX Item "mapping"
  Set the destination indexes of input frames.
  This is space or '|' separated list of indexes that maps input frames to output
  frames. Number of indexes also sets maximal value that each index may have.
  '-1' index have special meaning and that is to drop frame.

The first frame has the index 0. The default is to keep the input unchanged.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Swap second and third frame of every three frames of the input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf "shuffleframes=0 2 1" OUTPUT
  .Ve
* ·  
  Swap 10th and 1st frame of every ten frames of the input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf "shuffleframes=9 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8 0" OUTPUT
  .Ve

<a name="shuffleplanes"></a>

### shuffleplanes

.IX Subsection "shuffleplanes"
Reorder and/or duplicate video planes.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **map0**  
  .IX Item "map0"
  The index of the input plane to be used as the first output plane.
* **map1**  
  .IX Item "map1"
  The index of the input plane to be used as the second output plane.
* **map2**  
  .IX Item "map2"
  The index of the input plane to be used as the third output plane.
* **map3**  
  .IX Item "map3"
  The index of the input plane to be used as the fourth output plane.

The first plane has the index 0. The default is to keep the input unchanged.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Swap the second and third planes of the input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf shuffleplanes=0:2:1:3 OUTPUT
  .Ve

<a name="signalstats"></a>

### signalstats

.IX Subsection "signalstats"
Evaluate various visual metrics that assist in determining issues associated
with the digitization of analog video media.

By default the filter will log these metadata values:

* **\s-1YMIN\s0**  
  .IX Item "YMIN"
  Display the minimal Y value contained within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1YLOW\s0**  
  .IX Item "YLOW"
  Display the Y value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1YAVG\s0**  
  .IX Item "YAVG"
  Display the average Y value within the input frame. Expressed in range of
  [0-255].
* **\s-1YHIGH\s0**  
  .IX Item "YHIGH"
  Display the Y value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1YMAX\s0**  
  .IX Item "YMAX"
  Display the maximum Y value contained within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1UMIN\s0**  
  .IX Item "UMIN"
  Display the minimal U value contained within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1ULOW\s0**  
  .IX Item "ULOW"
  Display the U value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1UAVG\s0**  
  .IX Item "UAVG"
  Display the average U value within the input frame. Expressed in range of
  [0-255].
* **\s-1UHIGH\s0**  
  .IX Item "UHIGH"
  Display the U value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1UMAX\s0**  
  .IX Item "UMAX"
  Display the maximum U value contained within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1VMIN\s0**  
  .IX Item "VMIN"
  Display the minimal V value contained within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1VLOW\s0**  
  .IX Item "VLOW"
  Display the V value at the 10% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1VAVG\s0**  
  .IX Item "VAVG"
  Display the average V value within the input frame. Expressed in range of
  [0-255].
* **\s-1VHIGH\s0**  
  .IX Item "VHIGH"
  Display the V value at the 90% percentile within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1VMAX\s0**  
  .IX Item "VMAX"
  Display the maximum V value contained within the input frame. Expressed in
  range of [0-255].
* **\s-1SATMIN\s0**  
  .IX Item "SATMIN"
  Display the minimal saturation value contained within the input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].
* **\s-1SATLOW\s0**  
  .IX Item "SATLOW"
  Display the saturation value at the 10% percentile within the input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].
* **\s-1SATAVG\s0**  
  .IX Item "SATAVG"
  Display the average saturation value within the input frame. Expressed in range
  of [0-~181.02].
* **\s-1SATHIGH\s0**  
  .IX Item "SATHIGH"
  Display the saturation value at the 90% percentile within the input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].
* **\s-1SATMAX\s0**  
  .IX Item "SATMAX"
  Display the maximum saturation value contained within the input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-~181.02].
* **\s-1HUEMED\s0**  
  .IX Item "HUEMED"
  Display the median value for hue within the input frame. Expressed in range of
  [0-360].
* **\s-1HUEAVG\s0**  
  .IX Item "HUEAVG"
  Display the average value for hue within the input frame. Expressed in range of
  [0-360].
* **\s-1YDIF\s0**  
  .IX Item "YDIF"
  Display the average of sample value difference between all values of the Y
  plane in the current frame and corresponding values of the previous input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-255].
* **\s-1UDIF\s0**  
  .IX Item "UDIF"
  Display the average of sample value difference between all values of the U
  plane in the current frame and corresponding values of the previous input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-255].
* **\s-1VDIF\s0**  
  .IX Item "VDIF"
  Display the average of sample value difference between all values of the V
  plane in the current frame and corresponding values of the previous input frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-255].
* **\s-1YBITDEPTH\s0**  
  .IX Item "YBITDEPTH"
  Display bit depth of Y plane in current frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-16].
* **\s-1UBITDEPTH\s0**  
  .IX Item "UBITDEPTH"
  Display bit depth of U plane in current frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-16].
* **\s-1VBITDEPTH\s0**  
  .IX Item "VBITDEPTH"
  Display bit depth of V plane in current frame.
  Expressed in range of [0-16].

The filter accepts the following options:

* **stat**  
  .IX Item "stat"
* **out**  
  .IX Item "out"
  **stat** specify an additional form of image analysis.
  **out** output video with the specified type of pixel highlighted.
  .Sp
  Both options accept the following values:
    * **tout**  
      .IX Item "tout"
      Identify _temporal outliers_ pixels. A _temporal outlier_ is a pixel
      unlike the neighboring pixels of the same field. Examples of temporal outliers
      include the results of video dropouts, head clogs, or tape tracking issues.
    * **vrep**  
      .IX Item "vrep"
      Identify _vertical line repetition_. Vertical line repetition includes
      similar rows of pixels within a frame. In born-digital video vertical line
      repetition is common, but this pattern is uncommon in video digitized from an
      analog source. When it occurs in video that results from the digitization of an
      analog source it can indicate concealment from a dropout compensator.
    * **brng**  
      .IX Item "brng"
      Identify pixels that fall outside of legal broadcast range.
* **color, c**  
  .IX Item "color, c"
  Set the highlight color for the **out** option. The default color is
  yellow.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Output data of various video metrics:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffprobe -f lavfi movie=example.mov,signalstats="stat=tout+vrep+brng" -show_frames
  .Ve
* ·  
  Output specific data about the minimum and maximum values of the Y plane per frame:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffprobe -f lavfi movie=example.mov,signalstats -show_entries frame_tags=lavfi.signalstats.YMAX,lavfi.signalstats.YMIN
  .Ve
* ·  
  Playback video while highlighting pixels that are outside of broadcast range in red.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay example.mov -vf signalstats="out=brng:color=red"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Playback video with signalstats metadata drawn over the frame.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay example.mov -vf signalstats=stat=brng+vrep+tout,drawtext=fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:textfile=signalstat_drawtext.txt
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The contents of signalstat_drawtext.txt used in the command are:
  .Sp
  .Vb 5
          time %{pts:hms}
          Y (%{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.YMIN}-%{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.YMAX})
          U (%{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.UMIN}-%{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.UMAX})
          V (%{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.VMIN}-%{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.VMAX})
          saturation maximum: %{metadata:lavfi.signalstats.SATMAX}
  .Ve

<a name="signature"></a>

### signature

.IX Subsection "signature"
Calculates the \s-1MPEG-7\s0 Video Signature. The filter can handle more than one
input. In this case the matching between the inputs can be calculated additionally.
The filter always passes through the first input. The signature of each stream can
be written into a file.

It accepts the following options:

* **detectmode**  
  .IX Item "detectmode"
  Enable or disable the matching process.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **off**  
      .IX Item "off"
      Disable the calculation of a matching (default).
    * **full**  
      .IX Item "full"
      Calculate the matching for the whole video and output whether the whole video
      matches or only parts.
    * **fast**  
      .IX Item "fast"
      Calculate only until a matching is found or the video ends. Should be faster in
      some cases.
* **nb\_inputs**  
  .IX Item "nb_inputs"
  Set the number of inputs. The option value must be a non negative integer.
  Default value is 1.
* **filename**  
  .IX Item "filename"
  Set the path to which the output is written. If there is more than one input,
  the path must be a prototype, i.e. must contain \f(CW%d or \f(CW%0nd (where n is a positive
  integer), that will be replaced with the input number. If no filename is
  specified, no output will be written. This is the default.
* **format**  
  .IX Item "format"
  Choose the output format.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **binary**  
      .IX Item "binary"
      Use the specified binary representation (default).
    * **xml**  
      .IX Item "xml"
      Use the specified xml representation.
* **th\_d**  
  .IX Item "th_d"
  Set threshold to detect one word as similar. The option value must be an integer
  greater than zero. The default value is 9000.
* **th\_dc**  
  .IX Item "th_dc"
  Set threshold to detect all words as similar. The option value must be an integer
  greater than zero. The default value is 60000.
* **th\_xh**  
  .IX Item "th_xh"
  Set threshold to detect frames as similar. The option value must be an integer
  greater than zero. The default value is 116.
* **th\_di**  
  .IX Item "th_di"
  Set the minimum length of a sequence in frames to recognize it as matching
  sequence. The option value must be a non negative integer value.
  The default value is 0.
* **th\_it**  
  .IX Item "th_it"
  Set the minimum relation, that matching frames to all frames must have.
  The option value must be a double value between 0 and 1. The default value is 0.5.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  To calculate the signature of an input video and store it in signature.bin:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i input.mkv -vf signature=filename=signature.bin -map 0:v -f null -
  .Ve
* ·  
  To detect whether two videos match and store the signatures in \s-1XML\s0 format in
  signature0.xml and signature1.xml:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i input1.mkv -i input2.mkv -filter_complex "[0:v][1:v] signature=nb_inputs=2:detectmode=full:format=xml:filename=signature%d.xml" -map :v -f null -
  .Ve

<a name="smartblur"></a>

### smartblur

.IX Subsection "smartblur"
Blur the input video without impacting the outlines.

It accepts the following options:

* **luma_radius, lr**  
  .IX Item "luma_radius, lr"
  Set the luma radius. The option value must be a float number in
  the range [0.1,5.0] that specifies the variance of the gaussian filter
  used to blur the image (slower if larger). Default value is 1.0.
* **luma_strength, ls**  
  .IX Item "luma_strength, ls"
  Set the luma strength. The option value must be a float number
  in the range [-1.0,1.0] that configures the blurring. A value included
  in [0.0,1.0] will blur the image whereas a value included in
  [-1.0,0.0] will sharpen the image. Default value is 1.0.
* **luma_threshold, lt**  
  .IX Item "luma_threshold, lt"
  Set the luma threshold used as a coefficient to determine
  whether a pixel should be blurred or not. The option value must be an
  integer in the range [-30,30]. A value of 0 will filter all the image,
  a value included in [0,30] will filter flat areas and a value included
  in [-30,0] will filter edges. Default value is 0.
* **chroma_radius, cr**  
  .IX Item "chroma_radius, cr"
  Set the chroma radius. The option value must be a float number in
  the range [0.1,5.0] that specifies the variance of the gaussian filter
  used to blur the image (slower if larger). Default value is **luma\_radius**.
* **chroma_strength, cs**  
  .IX Item "chroma_strength, cs"
  Set the chroma strength. The option value must be a float number
  in the range [-1.0,1.0] that configures the blurring. A value included
  in [0.0,1.0] will blur the image whereas a value included in
  [-1.0,0.0] will sharpen the image. Default value is **luma\_strength**.
* **chroma_threshold, ct**  
  .IX Item "chroma_threshold, ct"
  Set the chroma threshold used as a coefficient to determine
  whether a pixel should be blurred or not. The option value must be an
  integer in the range [-30,30]. A value of 0 will filter all the image,
  a value included in [0,30] will filter flat areas and a value included
  in [-30,0] will filter edges. Default value is **luma\_threshold**.

If a chroma option is not explicitly set, the corresponding luma value
is set.

<a name="ssim"></a>

### ssim

.IX Subsection "ssim"
Obtain the \s-1SSIM\s0 (Structural SImilarity Metric) between two input videos.

This filter takes in input two input videos, the first input is
considered the main\*(R" source and is passed unchanged to the
output. The second input is used as a reference\*(R" video for computing
the \s-1SSIM.\s0

Both video inputs must have the same resolution and pixel format for
this filter to work correctly. Also it assumes that both inputs
have the same number of frames, which are compared one by one.

The filter stores the calculated \s-1SSIM\s0 of each frame.

The description of the accepted parameters follows.

* **stats_file, f**  
  .IX Item "stats_file, f"
  If specified the filter will use the named file to save the \s-1SSIM\s0 of
  each individual frame. When filename equals -\*(R" the data is sent to
  standard output.

The file printed if _stats\_file_ is selected, contains a sequence of
key/value pairs of the form _key_:_value_ for each compared
couple of frames.

A description of each shown parameter follows:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  sequential number of the input frame, starting from 1
* **Y, U, V, R, G, B**  
  .IX Item "Y, U, V, R, G, B"
  \s-1SSIM\s0 of the compared frames for the component specified by the suffix.
* **All**  
  .IX Item "All"
  \s-1SSIM\s0 of the compared frames for the whole frame.
* **dB**  
  .IX Item "dB"
  Same as above but in dB representation.

This filter also supports the **framesync** options.

For example:

.Vb 2
        movie=ref_movie.mpg, setpts=PTS-STARTPTS [main];
        [main][ref] ssim="stats_file=stats.log" [out]
.Ve

On this example the input file being processed is compared with the
reference file _ref\_movie.mpg_. The \s-1SSIM\s0 of each individual frame
is stored in _stats.log_.

Another example with both psnr and ssim at same time:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i main.mpg -i ref.mpg -lavfi  "ssim;[0:v][1:v]psnr" -f null -
.Ve

<a name="stereo3d"></a>

### stereo3d

.IX Subsection "stereo3d"
Convert between different stereoscopic image formats.

The filters accept the following options:

* **in**  
  .IX Item "in"
  Set stereoscopic image format of input.
  .Sp
  Available values for input image formats are:
    * **sbsl**  
      .IX Item "sbsl"
      side by side parallel (left eye left, right eye right)
    * **sbsr**  
      .IX Item "sbsr"
      side by side crosseye (right eye left, left eye right)
    * **sbs2l**  
      .IX Item "sbs2l"
      side by side parallel with half width resolution
      (left eye left, right eye right)
    * **sbs2r**  
      .IX Item "sbs2r"
      side by side crosseye with half width resolution
      (right eye left, left eye right)
    * **abl**  
      .IX Item "abl"
      above-below (left eye above, right eye below)
    * **abr**  
      .IX Item "abr"
      above-below (right eye above, left eye below)
    * **ab2l**  
      .IX Item "ab2l"
      above-below with half height resolution
      (left eye above, right eye below)
    * **ab2r**  
      .IX Item "ab2r"
      above-below with half height resolution
      (right eye above, left eye below)
    * **al**  
      .IX Item "al"
      alternating frames (left eye first, right eye second)
    * **ar**  
      .IX Item "ar"
      alternating frames (right eye first, left eye second)
    * **irl**  
      .IX Item "irl"
      interleaved rows (left eye has top row, right eye starts on next row)
    * **irr**  
      .IX Item "irr"
      interleaved rows (right eye has top row, left eye starts on next row)
    * **icl**  
      .IX Item "icl"
      interleaved columns, left eye first
    * **icr**  
      .IX Item "icr"
      interleaved columns, right eye first
      .Sp
      Default value is **sbsl**.
* **out**  
  .IX Item "out"
  Set stereoscopic image format of output.
    * **sbsl**  
      .IX Item "sbsl"
      side by side parallel (left eye left, right eye right)
    * **sbsr**  
      .IX Item "sbsr"
      side by side crosseye (right eye left, left eye right)
    * **sbs2l**  
      .IX Item "sbs2l"
      side by side parallel with half width resolution
      (left eye left, right eye right)
    * **sbs2r**  
      .IX Item "sbs2r"
      side by side crosseye with half width resolution
      (right eye left, left eye right)
    * **abl**  
      .IX Item "abl"
      above-below (left eye above, right eye below)
    * **abr**  
      .IX Item "abr"
      above-below (right eye above, left eye below)
    * **ab2l**  
      .IX Item "ab2l"
      above-below with half height resolution
      (left eye above, right eye below)
    * **ab2r**  
      .IX Item "ab2r"
      above-below with half height resolution
      (right eye above, left eye below)
    * **al**  
      .IX Item "al"
      alternating frames (left eye first, right eye second)
    * **ar**  
      .IX Item "ar"
      alternating frames (right eye first, left eye second)
    * **irl**  
      .IX Item "irl"
      interleaved rows (left eye has top row, right eye starts on next row)
    * **irr**  
      .IX Item "irr"
      interleaved rows (right eye has top row, left eye starts on next row)
    * **arbg**  
      .IX Item "arbg"
      anaglyph red/blue gray
      (red filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)
    * **argg**  
      .IX Item "argg"
      anaglyph red/green gray
      (red filter on left eye, green filter on right eye)
    * **arcg**  
      .IX Item "arcg"
      anaglyph red/cyan gray
      (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)
    * **arch**  
      .IX Item "arch"
      anaglyph red/cyan half colored
      (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)
    * **arcc**  
      .IX Item "arcc"
      anaglyph red/cyan color
      (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)
    * **arcd**  
      .IX Item "arcd"
      anaglyph red/cyan color optimized with the least squares projection of dubois
      (red filter on left eye, cyan filter on right eye)
    * **agmg**  
      .IX Item "agmg"
      anaglyph green/magenta gray
      (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)
    * **agmh**  
      .IX Item "agmh"
      anaglyph green/magenta half colored
      (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)
    * **agmc**  
      .IX Item "agmc"
      anaglyph green/magenta colored
      (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)
    * **agmd**  
      .IX Item "agmd"
      anaglyph green/magenta color optimized with the least squares projection of dubois
      (green filter on left eye, magenta filter on right eye)
    * **aybg**  
      .IX Item "aybg"
      anaglyph yellow/blue gray
      (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)
    * **aybh**  
      .IX Item "aybh"
      anaglyph yellow/blue half colored
      (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)
    * **aybc**  
      .IX Item "aybc"
      anaglyph yellow/blue colored
      (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)
    * **aybd**  
      .IX Item "aybd"
      anaglyph yellow/blue color optimized with the least squares projection of dubois
      (yellow filter on left eye, blue filter on right eye)
    * **ml**  
      .IX Item "ml"
      mono output (left eye only)
    * **mr**  
      .IX Item "mr"
      mono output (right eye only)
    * **chl**  
      .IX Item "chl"
      checkerboard, left eye first
    * **chr**  
      .IX Item "chr"
      checkerboard, right eye first
    * **icl**  
      .IX Item "icl"
      interleaved columns, left eye first
    * **icr**  
      .IX Item "icr"
      interleaved columns, right eye first
    * **hdmi**  
      .IX Item "hdmi"
      \s-1HDMI\s0 frame pack
      .Sp
      Default value is **arcd**.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Convert input video from side by side parallel to anaglyph yellow/blue dubois:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          stereo3d=sbsl:aybd
  .Ve
* ·  
  Convert input video from above below (left eye above, right eye below) to side by side crosseye.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          stereo3d=abl:sbsr
  .Ve

<a name="streamselect-astreamselect"></a>

### streamselect, astreamselect

.IX Subsection "streamselect, astreamselect"
Select video or audio streams.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **inputs**  
  .IX Item "inputs"
  Set number of inputs. Default is 2.
* **map**  
  .IX Item "map"
  Set input indexes to remap to outputs.

_Commands_
.IX Subsection "Commands"

The \f(CW`streamselect\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`astreamselect\*(C' filter supports the following
commands:

* **map**  
  .IX Item "map"
  Set input indexes to remap to outputs.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Select first 5 seconds 1st stream and rest of time 2nd stream:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          sendcmd=5.0 streamselect map 1\*(Aq,streamselect=inputs=2:map=0
  .Ve
* ·  
  Same as above, but for audio:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          asendcmd=5.0 astreamselect map 1\*(Aq,astreamselect=inputs=2:map=0
  .Ve

<a name="sobel"></a>

### sobel

.IX Subsection "sobel"
Apply sobel operator to input video stream.

The filter accepts the following option:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied.
  By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Set value which will be multiplied with filtered result.
* **delta**  
  .IX Item "delta"
  Set value which will be added to filtered result.

<a name="spp"></a>

### spp

.IX Subsection "spp"
Apply a simple postprocessing filter that compresses and decompresses the image
at several (or - in the case of **quality** level \f(CW6 - all) shifts
and average the results.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **quality**  
  .IX Item "quality"
  Set quality. This option defines the number of levels for averaging. It accepts
  an integer in the range 0-6. If set to \f(CW0, the filter will have no
  effect. A value of \f(CW6 means the higher quality. For each increment of
  that value the speed drops by a factor of approximately 2.  Default value is
  \f(CW3.
* **qp**  
  .IX Item "qp"
  Force a constant quantization parameter. If not set, the filter will use the \s-1QP\s0
  from the video stream (if available).
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set thresholding mode. Available modes are:
    * **hard**  
      .IX Item "hard"
      Set hard thresholding (default).
    * **soft**  
      .IX Item "soft"
      Set soft thresholding (better de-ringing effect, but likely blurrier).
* **use\_bframe\_qp**  
  .IX Item "use_bframe_qp"
  Enable the use of the \s-1QP\s0 from the B-Frames if set to \f(CW1. Using this
  option may cause flicker since the B-Frames have often larger \s-1QP.\s0 Default is
  \f(CW0 (not enabled).

<a name="sr"></a>

### sr

.IX Subsection "sr"
Scale the input by applying one of the super-resolution methods based on
convolutional neural networks. Supported models:

* ·  
  Super-Resolution Convolutional Neural Network model (\s-1SRCNN\s0).
  See &lt;**https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.00092**&gt;.
* ·  
  Efficient Sub-Pixel Convolutional Neural Network model (\s-1ESPCN\s0).
  See &lt;**https://arxiv.org/abs/1609.05158**&gt;.

Training scripts as well as scripts for model generation are provided in
the repository at &lt;**https://github.com/HighVoltageRocknRoll/sr.git**&gt;.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **dnn\_backend**  
  .IX Item "dnn_backend"
  Specify which \s-1DNN\s0 backend to use for model loading and execution. This option accepts
  the following values:
    * **native**  
      .IX Item "native"
      Native implementation of \s-1DNN\s0 loading and execution.
    * **tensorflow**  
      .IX Item "tensorflow"
      TensorFlow backend. To enable this backend you
      need to install the TensorFlow for C library (see
      &lt;**https://www.tensorflow.org/install/install\_c**&gt;) and configure FFmpeg with
      \f(CW`--enable-libtensorflow\*(C'
      .Sp
      Default value is **native**.
* **model**  
  .IX Item "model"
  Set path to model file specifying network architecture and its parameters.
  Note that different backends use different file formats. TensorFlow backend
  can load files for both formats, while native backend can load files for only
  its format.
* **scale\_factor**  
  .IX Item "scale_factor"
  Set scale factor for \s-1SRCNN\s0 model. Allowed values are \f(CW2, \f(CW3 and \f(CW4.
  Default value is \f(CW2. Scale factor is necessary for \s-1SRCNN\s0 model, because it accepts
  input upscaled using bicubic upscaling with proper scale factor.

<a name="subtitles"></a>

### subtitles

.IX Subsection "subtitles"
Draw subtitles on top of input video using the libass library.

To enable compilation of this filter you need to configure FFmpeg with
\f(CW`--enable-libass\*(C'. This filter also requires a build with libavcodec and
libavformat to convert the passed subtitles file to \s-1ASS\s0 (Advanced Substation
Alpha) subtitles format.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **filename, f**  
  .IX Item "filename, f"
  Set the filename of the subtitle file to read. It must be specified.
* **original\_size**  
  .IX Item "original_size"
  Specify the size of the original video, the video for which the \s-1ASS\s0 file
  was composed. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Due to a misdesign in \s-1ASS\s0 aspect ratio arithmetic, this is necessary to
  correctly scale the fonts if the aspect ratio has been changed.
* **fontsdir**  
  .IX Item "fontsdir"
  Set a directory path containing fonts that can be used by the filter.
  These fonts will be used in addition to whatever the font provider uses.
* **alpha**  
  .IX Item "alpha"
  Process alpha channel, by default alpha channel is untouched.
* **charenc**  
  .IX Item "charenc"
  Set subtitles input character encoding. \f(CW`subtitles\*(C' filter only. Only
  useful if not \s-1UTF-8.\s0
* **stream_index, si**  
  .IX Item "stream_index, si"
  Set subtitles stream index. \f(CW`subtitles\*(C' filter only.
* **force\_style**  
  .IX Item "force_style"
  Override default style or script info parameters of the subtitles. It accepts a
  string containing \s-1ASS\s0 style format \f(CW`KEY=VALUE\*(C' couples separated by \*(L",\*(R".

If the first key is not specified, it is assumed that the first value
specifies the **filename**.

For example, to render the file _sub.srt_ on top of the input
video, use the command:

.Vb 1
        subtitles=sub.srt
.Ve

which is equivalent to:

.Vb 1
        subtitles=filename=sub.srt
.Ve

To render the default subtitles stream from file _video.mkv_, use:

.Vb 1
        subtitles=video.mkv
.Ve

To render the second subtitles stream from that file, use:

.Vb 1
        subtitles=video.mkv:si=1
.Ve

To make the subtitles stream from _sub.srt_ appear in 80% transparent blue
\f(CW`DejaVu Serif\*(C', use:

.Vb 1
        subtitles=sub.srt:force_style=FontName=DejaVu Serif,PrimaryColour=&HCCFF0000\*(Aq
.Ve

<a name="super2xsai"></a>

### super2xsai

.IX Subsection "super2xsai"
Scale the input by 2x and smooth using the Super2xSaI (Scale and
Interpolate) pixel art scaling algorithm.

Useful for enlarging pixel art images without reducing sharpness.

<a name="swaprect"></a>

### swaprect

.IX Subsection "swaprect"
Swap two rectangular objects in video.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
  Set object width.
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  Set object height.
* **x1**  
  .IX Item "x1"
  Set 1st rect x coordinate.
* **y1**  
  .IX Item "y1"
  Set 1st rect y coordinate.
* **x2**  
  .IX Item "x2"
  Set 2nd rect x coordinate.
* **y2**  
  .IX Item "y2"
  Set 2nd rect y coordinate.
  .Sp
  All expressions are evaluated once for each frame.

The all options are expressions containing the following constants:

* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  The input width and height.
* **a**  
  .IX Item "a"
  same as _w_ / _h_
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  input sample aspect ratio
* **dar**  
  .IX Item "dar"
  input display aspect ratio, it is the same as (_w_ / _h_) * _sar_
* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  The number of the input frame, starting from 0.
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  The timestamp expressed in seconds. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if the input timestamp is unknown.
* **pos**  
  .IX Item "pos"
  the position in the file of the input frame, \s-1NAN\s0 if unknown

<a name="swapuv"></a>

### swapuv

.IX Subsection "swapuv"
Swap U & V plane.

<a name="telecine"></a>

### telecine

.IX Subsection "telecine"
Apply telecine process to the video.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **first\_field**  
  .IX Item "first_field"
    * **top, t**  
      .IX Item "top, t"
      top field first
    * **bottom, b**  
      .IX Item "bottom, b"
      bottom field first
      The default value is \f(CW`top\*(C'.
* **pattern**  
  .IX Item "pattern"
  A string of numbers representing the pulldown pattern you wish to apply.
  The default value is \f(CW23.

.Vb 1
        Some typical patterns:
        
        NTSC output (30i):
        27.5p: 32222
        24p: 23 (classic)
        24p: 2332 (preferred)
        20p: 33
        18p: 334
        16p: 3444
        
        PAL output (25i):
        27.5p: 12222
        24p: 222222222223 ("Euro pulldown")
        16.67p: 33
        16p: 33333334
.Ve

<a name="threshold"></a>

### threshold

.IX Subsection "threshold"
Apply threshold effect to video stream.

This filter needs four video streams to perform thresholding.
First stream is stream we are filtering.
Second stream is holding threshold values, third stream is holding min values,
and last, fourth stream is holding max values.

The filter accepts the following option:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied.
  By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.

For example if first stream pixel's component value is less then threshold value
of pixel component from 2nd threshold stream, third stream value will picked,
otherwise fourth stream pixel component value will be picked.

Using color source filter one can perform various types of thresholding:

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Binary threshold, using gray color as threshold:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -f lavfi -i color=black -f lavfi -i color=white -lavfi threshold output.avi
  .Ve
* ·  
  Inverted binary threshold, using gray color as threshold:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -f lavfi -i color=white -f lavfi -i color=black -lavfi threshold output.avi
  .Ve
* ·  
  Truncate binary threshold, using gray color as threshold:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -lavfi threshold output.avi
  .Ve
* ·  
  Threshold to zero, using gray color as threshold:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -f lavfi -i color=white -i 320x240.avi -lavfi threshold output.avi
  .Ve
* ·  
  Inverted threshold to zero, using gray color as threshold:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=gray -i 320x240.avi -f lavfi -i color=white -lavfi threshold output.avi
  .Ve

<a name="thumbnail"></a>

### thumbnail

.IX Subsection "thumbnail"
Select the most representative frame in a given sequence of consecutive frames.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  Set the frames batch size to analyze; in a set of _n_ frames, the filter
  will pick one of them, and then handle the next batch of _n_ frames until
  the end. Default is \f(CW100.

Since the filter keeps track of the whole frames sequence, a bigger _n_
value will result in a higher memory usage, so a high value is not recommended.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Extract one picture each 50 frames:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          thumbnail=50
  .Ve
* ·  
  Complete example of a thumbnail creation with **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i in.avi -vf thumbnail,scale=300:200 -frames:v 1 out.png
  .Ve

<a name="tile"></a>

### tile

.IX Subsection "tile"
Tile several successive frames together.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **layout**  
  .IX Item "layout"
  Set the grid size (i.e. the number of lines and columns). For the syntax of
  this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
* **nb\_frames**  
  .IX Item "nb_frames"
  Set the maximum number of frames to render in the given area. It must be less
  than or equal to _w_x_h_. The default value is \f(CW0, meaning all
  the area will be used.
* **margin**  
  .IX Item "margin"
  Set the outer border margin in pixels.
* **padding**  
  .IX Item "padding"
  Set the inner border thickness (i.e. the number of pixels between frames). For
  more advanced padding options (such as having different values for the edges),
  refer to the pad video filter.
* **color**  
  .IX Item "color"
  Specify the color of the unused area. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  The default value of _color_ is black\*(R".
* **overlap**  
  .IX Item "overlap"
  Set the number of frames to overlap when tiling several successive frames together.
  The value must be between \f(CW0 and _nb_frames - 1_.
* **init\_padding**  
  .IX Item "init_padding"
  Set the number of frames to initially be empty before displaying first output frame.
  This controls how soon will one get first output frame.
  The value must be between \f(CW0 and _nb_frames - 1_.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Produce 8x8 \s-1PNG\s0 tiles of all keyframes (**-skip_frame nokey**) in a movie:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -skip_frame nokey -i file.avi -vf scale=128:72,tile=8x8\*(Aq -an -vsync 0 keyframes%03d.png
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The **-vsync 0** is necessary to prevent **ffmpeg** from
  duplicating each output frame to accommodate the originally detected frame
  rate.
* ·  
  Display \f(CW5 pictures in an area of \f(CW`3x2\*(C' frames,
  with \f(CW7 pixels between them, and \f(CW2 pixels of initial margin, using
  mixed flat and named options:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          tile=3x2:nb_frames=5:padding=7:margin=2
  .Ve

<a name="tinterlace"></a>

### tinterlace

.IX Subsection "tinterlace"
Perform various types of temporal field interlacing.

Frames are counted starting from 1, so the first input frame is
considered odd.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Specify the mode of the interlacing. This option can also be specified
  as a value alone. See below for a list of values for this option.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **merge, 0**  
      .IX Item "merge, 0"
      Move odd frames into the upper field, even into the lower field,
      generating a double height frame at half frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              
              Output:
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
      .Ve
    * **drop_even, 1**  
      .IX Item "drop_even, 1"
      Only output odd frames, even frames are dropped, generating a frame with
      unchanged height at half frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              
              Output:
              11111                           33333
              11111                           33333
              11111                           33333
              11111                           33333
      .Ve
    * **drop_odd, 2**  
      .IX Item "drop_odd, 2"
      Only output even frames, odd frames are dropped, generating a frame with
      unchanged height at half frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              
              Output:
                              22222                           44444
                              22222                           44444
                              22222                           44444
                              22222                           44444
      .Ve
    * **pad, 3**  
      .IX Item "pad, 3"
      Expand each frame to full height, but pad alternate lines with black,
      generating a frame with double height at the same input frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              
              Output:
              11111           .....           33333           .....
              .....           22222           .....           44444
              11111           .....           33333           .....
              .....           22222           .....           44444
              11111           .....           33333           .....
              .....           22222           .....           44444
              11111           .....           33333           .....
              .....           22222           .....           44444
      .Ve
    * **interleave_top, 4**  
      .IX Item "interleave_top, 4"
      Interleave the upper field from odd frames with the lower field from
      even frames, generating a frame with unchanged height at half frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
              11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
              11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
              11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
              
              Output:
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
      .Ve
    * **interleave_bottom, 5**  
      .IX Item "interleave_bottom, 5"
      Interleave the lower field from odd frames with the upper field from
      even frames, generating a frame with unchanged height at half frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
              11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
              11111           22222&lt;-         33333           44444&lt;-
              11111&lt;-         22222           33333&lt;-         44444
              
              Output:
              22222                           44444
              11111                           33333
              22222                           44444
              11111                           33333
      .Ve
    * **interlacex2, 6**  
      .IX Item "interlacex2, 6"
      Double frame rate with unchanged height. Frames are inserted each
      containing the second temporal field from the previous input frame and
      the first temporal field from the next input frame. This mode relies on
      the top_field_first flag. Useful for interlaced video displays with no
      field synchronisation.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222           33333           44444
               11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
               11111           22222           33333           44444
              
              Output:
              11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444   44444
               11111   11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444
              11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444   44444
               11111   11111   22222   22222   33333   33333   44444
      .Ve
    * **mergex2, 7**  
      .IX Item "mergex2, 7"
      Move odd frames into the upper field, even into the lower field,
      generating a double height frame at same frame rate.
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
               ------&gt; time
              Input:
              Frame 1         Frame 2         Frame 3         Frame 4
              
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              11111           22222           33333           44444
              
              Output:
              11111           33333           33333           55555
              22222           22222           44444           44444
              11111           33333           33333           55555
              22222           22222           44444           44444
              11111           33333           33333           55555
              22222           22222           44444           44444
              11111           33333           33333           55555
              22222           22222           44444           44444
      .Ve
      .Sp
      Numeric values are deprecated but are accepted for backward
      compatibility reasons.
      .Sp
      Default mode is \f(CW`merge\*(C'.
* **flags**  
  .IX Item "flags"
  Specify flags influencing the filter process.
  .Sp
  Available value for _flags_ is:
    * **low_pass_filter, vlfp**  
      .IX Item "low_pass_filter, vlfp"
      Enable linear vertical low-pass filtering in the filter.
      Vertical low-pass filtering is required when creating an interlaced
      destination from a progressive source which contains high-frequency
      vertical detail. Filtering will reduce interlace 'twitter' and Moire
      patterning.
    * **complex_filter, cvlfp**  
      .IX Item "complex_filter, cvlfp"
      Enable complex vertical low-pass filtering.
      This will slightly less reduce interlace 'twitter' and Moire
      patterning but better retain detail and subjective sharpness impression.
      .Sp
      Vertical low-pass filtering can only be enabled for **mode**
      _interleave\_top_ and _interleave\_bottom_.

<a name="tmix"></a>

### tmix

.IX Subsection "tmix"
Mix successive video frames.

A description of the accepted options follows.

* **frames**  
  .IX Item "frames"
  The number of successive frames to mix. If unspecified, it defaults to 3.
* **weights**  
  .IX Item "weights"
  Specify weight of each input video frame.
  Each weight is separated by space. If number of weights is smaller than
  number of _frames_ last specified weight will be used for all remaining
  unset weights.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Specify scale, if it is set it will be multiplied with sum
  of each weight multiplied with pixel values to give final destination
  pixel value. By default _scale_ is auto scaled to sum of weights.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Average 7 successive frames:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          tmix=frames=7:weights="1 1 1 1 1 1 1"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply simple temporal convolution:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          tmix=frames=3:weights="-1 3 -1"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Similar as above but only showing temporal differences:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          tmix=frames=3:weights="-1 2 -1":scale=1
  .Ve

<a name="tonemap"></a>

### tonemap

.IX Subsection "tonemap"
Tone map colors from different dynamic ranges.

This filter expects data in single precision floating point, as it needs to
operate on (and can output) out-of-range values. Another filter, such as
**zscale**, is needed to convert the resulting frame to a usable format.

The tonemapping algorithms implemented only work on linear light, so input
data should be linearized beforehand (and possibly correctly tagged).

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf zscale=transfer=linear,tonemap=clip,zscale=transfer=bt709,format=yuv420p OUTPUT
.Ve

_Options_
.IX Subsection "Options"

The filter accepts the following options.

* **tonemap**  
  .IX Item "tonemap"
  Set the tone map algorithm to use.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _none_  
      .IX Item "none"
      Do not apply any tone map, only desaturate overbright pixels.
    * _clip_  
      .IX Item "clip"
      Hard-clip any out-of-range values. Use it for perfect color accuracy for
      in-range values, while distorting out-of-range values.
    * _linear_  
      .IX Item "linear"
      Stretch the entire reference gamut to a linear multiple of the display.
    * _gamma_  
      .IX Item "gamma"
      Fit a logarithmic transfer between the tone curves.
    * _reinhard_  
      .IX Item "reinhard"
      Preserve overall image brightness with a simple curve, using nonlinear
      contrast, which results in flattening details and degrading color accuracy.
    * _hable_  
      .IX Item "hable"
      Preserve both dark and bright details better than _reinhard_, at the cost
      of slightly darkening everything. Use it when detail preservation is more
      important than color and brightness accuracy.
    * _mobius_  
      .IX Item "mobius"
      Smoothly map out-of-range values, while retaining contrast and colors for
      in-range material as much as possible. Use it when color accuracy is more
      important than detail preservation.
      .Sp
      Default is none.
* **param**  
  .IX Item "param"
  Tune the tone mapping algorithm.
  .Sp
  This affects the following algorithms:
    * _none_  
      .IX Item "none"
      Ignored.
    * _linear_  
      .IX Item "linear"
      Specifies the scale factor to use while stretching.
      Default to 1.0.
    * _gamma_  
      .IX Item "gamma"
      Specifies the exponent of the function.
      Default to 1.8.
    * _clip_  
      .IX Item "clip"
      Specify an extra linear coefficient to multiply into the signal before clipping.
      Default to 1.0.
    * _reinhard_  
      .IX Item "reinhard"
      Specify the local contrast coefficient at the display peak.
      Default to 0.5, which means that in-gamut values will be about half as bright
      as when clipping.
    * _hable_  
      .IX Item "hable"
      Ignored.
    * _mobius_  
      .IX Item "mobius"
      Specify the transition point from linear to mobius transform. Every value
      below this point is guaranteed to be mapped 1:1. The higher the value, the
      more accurate the result will be, at the cost of losing bright details.
      Default to 0.3, which due to the steep initial slope still preserves in-range
      colors fairly accurately.
* **desat**  
  .IX Item "desat"
  Apply desaturation for highlights that exceed this level of brightness. The
  higher the parameter, the more color information will be preserved. This
  setting helps prevent unnaturally blown-out colors for super-highlights, by
  (smoothly) turning into white instead. This makes images feel more natural,
  at the cost of reducing information about out-of-range colors.
  .Sp
  The default of 2.0 is somewhat conservative and will mostly just apply to
  skies or directly sunlit surfaces. A setting of 0.0 disables this option.
  .Sp
  This option works only if the input frame has a supported color tag.
* **peak**  
  .IX Item "peak"
  Override signal/nominal/reference peak with this value. Useful when the
  embedded peak information in display metadata is not reliable or when tone
  mapping from a lower range to a higher range.

<a name="transpose"></a>

### transpose

.IX Subsection "transpose"
Transpose rows with columns in the input video and optionally flip it.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **dir**  
  .IX Item "dir"
  Specify the transposition direction.
  .Sp
  Can assume the following values:
    * **0, 4, cclock\_flip**  
      .IX Item "0, 4, cclock_flip"
      Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise and vertically flip (default), that is:
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
              L.R     L.l
              . . -&gt;  . .
              l.r     R.r
      .Ve
    * **1, 5, clock**  
      .IX Item "1, 5, clock"
      Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise, that is:
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
              L.R     l.L
              . . -&gt;  . .
              l.r     r.R
      .Ve
    * **2, 6, cclock**  
      .IX Item "2, 6, cclock"
      Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise, that is:
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
              L.R     R.r
              . . -&gt;  . .
              l.r     L.l
      .Ve
    * **3, 7, clock\_flip**  
      .IX Item "3, 7, clock_flip"
      Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise and vertically flip, that is:
      .Sp
      .Vb 3
              L.R     r.R
              . . -&gt;  . .
              l.r     l.L
      .Ve
      .Sp
      For values between 4-7, the transposition is only done if the input
      video geometry is portrait and not landscape. These values are
      deprecated, the \f(CW`passthrough\*(C' option should be used instead.
      .Sp
      Numerical values are deprecated, and should be dropped in favor of
      symbolic constants.
* **passthrough**  
  .IX Item "passthrough"
  Do not apply the transposition if the input geometry matches the one
  specified by the specified value. It accepts the following values:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Always apply transposition.
    * **portrait**  
      .IX Item "portrait"
      Preserve portrait geometry (when _height_ &gt;= _width_).
    * **landscape**  
      .IX Item "landscape"
      Preserve landscape geometry (when _width_ &gt;= _height_).
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`none\*(C'.

For example to rotate by 90 degrees clockwise and preserve portrait
layout:

.Vb 1
        transpose=dir=1:passthrough=portrait
.Ve

The command above can also be specified as:

.Vb 1
        transpose=1:portrait
.Ve

<a name="transpose_npp"></a>

### transpose_npp

.IX Subsection "transpose_npp"
Transpose rows with columns in the input video and optionally flip it.
For more in depth examples see the **transpose** video filter, which shares mostly the same options.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **dir**  
  .IX Item "dir"
  Specify the transposition direction.
  .Sp
  Can assume the following values:
    * **cclock\_flip**  
      .IX Item "cclock_flip"
      Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise and vertically flip. (default)
    * **clock**  
      .IX Item "clock"
      Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise.
    * **cclock**  
      .IX Item "cclock"
      Rotate by 90 degrees counterclockwise.
    * **clock\_flip**  
      .IX Item "clock_flip"
      Rotate by 90 degrees clockwise and vertically flip.
* **passthrough**  
  .IX Item "passthrough"
  Do not apply the transposition if the input geometry matches the one
  specified by the specified value. It accepts the following values:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Always apply transposition. (default)
    * **portrait**  
      .IX Item "portrait"
      Preserve portrait geometry (when _height_ &gt;= _width_).
    * **landscape**  
      .IX Item "landscape"
      Preserve landscape geometry (when _width_ &gt;= _height_).

<a name="trim"></a>

### trim

.IX Subsection "trim"
Trim the input so that the output contains one continuous subpart of the input.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **start**  
  .IX Item "start"
  Specify the time of the start of the kept section, i.e. the frame with the
  timestamp _start_ will be the first frame in the output.
* **end**  
  .IX Item "end"
  Specify the time of the first frame that will be dropped, i.e. the frame
  immediately preceding the one with the timestamp _end_ will be the last
  frame in the output.
* **start\_pts**  
  .IX Item "start_pts"
  This is the same as _start_, except this option sets the start timestamp
  in timebase units instead of seconds.
* **end\_pts**  
  .IX Item "end_pts"
  This is the same as _end_, except this option sets the end timestamp
  in timebase units instead of seconds.
* **duration**  
  .IX Item "duration"
  The maximum duration of the output in seconds.
* **start\_frame**  
  .IX Item "start_frame"
  The number of the first frame that should be passed to the output.
* **end\_frame**  
  .IX Item "end_frame"
  The number of the first frame that should be dropped.

**start**, **end**, and **duration** are expressed as time
duration specifications; see
**the Time duration section in the ffmpeg-utils\|(1) manual**
for the accepted syntax.

Note that the first two sets of the start/end options and the **duration**
option look at the frame timestamp, while the _frame variants simply count the
frames that pass through the filter. Also note that this filter does not modify
the timestamps. If you wish for the output timestamps to start at zero, insert a
setpts filter after the trim filter.

If multiple start or end options are set, this filter tries to be greedy and
keep all the frames that match at least one of the specified constraints. To keep
only the part that matches all the constraints at once, chain multiple trim
filters.

The defaults are such that all the input is kept. So it is possible to set e.g.
just the end values to keep everything before the specified time.

Examples:

* ·  
  Drop everything except the second minute of input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf trim=60:120
  .Ve
* ·  
  Keep only the first second:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -vf trim=duration=1
  .Ve

<a name="unpremultiply"></a>

### unpremultiply

.IX Subsection "unpremultiply"
Apply alpha unpremultiply effect to input video stream using first plane
of second stream as alpha.

Both streams must have same dimensions and same pixel format.

The filter accepts the following option:

* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  Set which planes will be processed, unprocessed planes will be copied.
  By default value 0xf, all planes will be processed.
  .Sp
  If the format has 1 or 2 components, then luma is bit 0.
  If the format has 3 or 4 components:
  for \s-1RGB\s0 formats bit 0 is green, bit 1 is blue and bit 2 is red;
  for \s-1YUV\s0 formats bit 0 is luma, bit 1 is chroma-U and bit 2 is chroma-V.
  If present, the alpha channel is always the last bit.
* **inplace**  
  .IX Item "inplace"
  Do not require 2nd input for processing, instead use alpha plane from input stream.

<a name="unsharp"></a>

### unsharp

.IX Subsection "unsharp"
Sharpen or blur the input video.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **luma_msize_x, lx**  
  .IX Item "luma_msize_x, lx"
  Set the luma matrix horizontal size. It must be an odd integer between
  3 and 23. The default value is 5.
* **luma_msize_y, ly**  
  .IX Item "luma_msize_y, ly"
  Set the luma matrix vertical size. It must be an odd integer between 3
  and 23. The default value is 5.
* **luma_amount, la**  
  .IX Item "luma_amount, la"
  Set the luma effect strength. It must be a floating point number, reasonable
  values lay between -1.5 and 1.5.
  .Sp
  Negative values will blur the input video, while positive values will
  sharpen it, a value of zero will disable the effect.
  .Sp
  Default value is 1.0.
* **chroma_msize_x, cx**  
  .IX Item "chroma_msize_x, cx"
  Set the chroma matrix horizontal size. It must be an odd integer
  between 3 and 23. The default value is 5.
* **chroma_msize_y, cy**  
  .IX Item "chroma_msize_y, cy"
  Set the chroma matrix vertical size. It must be an odd integer
  between 3 and 23. The default value is 5.
* **chroma_amount, ca**  
  .IX Item "chroma_amount, ca"
  Set the chroma effect strength. It must be a floating point number, reasonable
  values lay between -1.5 and 1.5.
  .Sp
  Negative values will blur the input video, while positive values will
  sharpen it, a value of zero will disable the effect.
  .Sp
  Default value is 0.0.

All parameters are optional and default to the equivalent of the
string '5:5:1.0:5:5:0.0'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Apply strong luma sharpen effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          unsharp=luma_msize_x=7:luma_msize_y=7:luma_amount=2.5
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply a strong blur of both luma and chroma parameters:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          unsharp=7:7:-2:7:7:-2
  .Ve

<a name="uspp"></a>

### uspp

.IX Subsection "uspp"
Apply ultra slow/simple postprocessing filter that compresses and decompresses
the image at several (or - in the case of **quality** level \f(CW8 - all)
shifts and average the results.

The way this differs from the behavior of spp is that uspp actually encodes &
decodes each case with libavcodec Snow, whereas spp uses a simplified intra only 8x8
\s-1DCT\s0 similar to \s-1MJPEG.\s0

The filter accepts the following options:

* **quality**  
  .IX Item "quality"
  Set quality. This option defines the number of levels for averaging. It accepts
  an integer in the range 0-8. If set to \f(CW0, the filter will have no
  effect. A value of \f(CW8 means the higher quality. For each increment of
  that value the speed drops by a factor of approximately 2.  Default value is
  \f(CW3.
* **qp**  
  .IX Item "qp"
  Force a constant quantization parameter. If not set, the filter will use the \s-1QP\s0
  from the video stream (if available).

<a name="vaguedenoiser"></a>

### vaguedenoiser

.IX Subsection "vaguedenoiser"
Apply a wavelet based denoiser.

It transforms each frame from the video input into the wavelet domain,
using Cohen-Daubechies-Feauveau 9/7. Then it applies some filtering to
the obtained coefficients. It does an inverse wavelet transform after.
Due to wavelet properties, it should give a nice smoothed result, and
reduced noise, without blurring picture features.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **threshold**  
  .IX Item "threshold"
  The filtering strength. The higher, the more filtered the video will be.
  Hard thresholding can use a higher threshold than soft thresholding
  before the video looks overfiltered. Default value is 2.
* **method**  
  .IX Item "method"
  The filtering method the filter will use.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **hard**  
      .IX Item "hard"
      All values under the threshold will be zeroed.
    * **soft**  
      .IX Item "soft"
      All values under the threshold will be zeroed. All values above will be
      reduced by the threshold.
    * **garrote**  
      .IX Item "garrote"
      Scales or nullifies coefficients - intermediary between (more) soft and
      (less) hard thresholding.
      .Sp
      Default is garrote.
* **nsteps**  
  .IX Item "nsteps"
  Number of times, the wavelet will decompose the picture. Picture can't
  be decomposed beyond a particular point (typically, 8 for a 640x480
  frame - as 2^9 = 512 &gt; 480). Valid values are integers between 1 and 32. Default value is 6.
* **percent**  
  .IX Item "percent"
  Partial of full denoising (limited coefficients shrinking), from 0 to 100. Default value is 85.
* **planes**  
  .IX Item "planes"
  A list of the planes to process. By default all planes are processed.

<a name="vectorscope"></a>

### vectorscope

.IX Subsection "vectorscope"
Display 2 color component values in the two dimensional graph (which is called
a vectorscope).

This filter accepts the following options:

* **mode, m**  
  .IX Item "mode, m"
  Set vectorscope mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **gray**  
      .IX Item "gray"
      Gray values are displayed on graph, higher brightness means more pixels have
      same component color value on location in graph. This is the default mode.
    * **color**  
      .IX Item "color"
      Gray values are displayed on graph. Surrounding pixels values which are not
      present in video frame are drawn in gradient of 2 color components which are
      set by option \f(CW`x\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`y\*(C'. The 3rd color component is static.
    * **color2**  
      .IX Item "color2"
      Actual color components values present in video frame are displayed on graph.
    * **color3**  
      .IX Item "color3"
      Similar as color2 but higher frequency of same values \f(CW`x\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`y\*(C'
      on graph increases value of another color component, which is luminance by
      default values of \f(CW`x\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`y\*(C'.
    * **color4**  
      .IX Item "color4"
      Actual colors present in video frame are displayed on graph. If two different
      colors map to same position on graph then color with higher value of component
      not present in graph is picked.
    * **color5**  
      .IX Item "color5"
      Gray values are displayed on graph. Similar to \f(CW`color\*(C' but with 3rd color
      component picked from radial gradient.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
  Set which color component will be represented on X-axis. Default is \f(CW1.
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  Set which color component will be represented on Y-axis. Default is \f(CW2.
* **intensity, i**  
  .IX Item "intensity, i"
  Set intensity, used by modes: gray, color, color3 and color5 for increasing brightness
  of color component which represents frequency of (X, Y) location in graph.
* **envelope, e**  
  .IX Item "envelope, e"
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      No envelope, this is default.
    * **instant**  
      .IX Item "instant"
      Instant envelope, even darkest single pixel will be clearly highlighted.
    * **peak**  
      .IX Item "peak"
      Hold maximum and minimum values presented in graph over time. This way you
      can still spot out of range values without constantly looking at vectorscope.
    * **peak+instant**  
      .IX Item "peak+instant"
      Peak and instant envelope combined together.
* **graticule, g**  
  .IX Item "graticule, g"
  Set what kind of graticule to draw.
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
    * **green**  
      .IX Item "green"
    * **color**  
      .IX Item "color"
* **opacity, o**  
  .IX Item "opacity, o"
  Set graticule opacity.
* **flags, f**  
  .IX Item "flags, f"
  Set graticule flags.
    * **white**  
      .IX Item "white"
      Draw graticule for white point.
    * **black**  
      .IX Item "black"
      Draw graticule for black point.
    * **name**  
      .IX Item "name"
      Draw color points short names.
* **bgopacity, b**  
  .IX Item "bgopacity, b"
  Set background opacity.
* **lthreshold, l**  
  .IX Item "lthreshold, l"
  Set low threshold for color component not represented on X or Y axis.
  Values lower than this value will be ignored. Default is 0.
  Note this value is multiplied with actual max possible value one pixel component
