  can have. So for 8-bit input and low threshold value of 0.1 actual threshold
  is 0.1 * 255 = 25.
* **hthreshold, h**  
  .IX Item "hthreshold, h"
  Set high threshold for color component not represented on X or Y axis.
  Values higher than this value will be ignored. Default is 1.
  Note this value is multiplied with actual max possible value one pixel component
  can have. So for 8-bit input and high threshold value of 0.9 actual threshold
  is 0.9 * 255 = 230.
* **colorspace, c**  
  .IX Item "colorspace, c"
  Set what kind of colorspace to use when drawing graticule.
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
    * **601**  
      .IX Item "601"
    * **709**  
      .IX Item "709"
      .Sp
      Default is auto.

<a name="vidstabdetect"></a>

### vidstabdetect

.IX Subsection "vidstabdetect"
Analyze video stabilization/deshaking. Perform pass 1 of 2, see
**vidstabtransform** for pass 2.

This filter generates a file with relative translation and rotation
transform information about subsequent frames, which is then used by
the **vidstabtransform** filter.

To enable compilation of this filter you need to configure FFmpeg with
\f(CW`--enable-libvidstab\*(C'.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **result**  
  .IX Item "result"
  Set the path to the file used to write the transforms information.
  Default value is _transforms.trf_.
* **shakiness**  
  .IX Item "shakiness"
  Set how shaky the video is and how quick the camera is. It accepts an
  integer in the range 1-10, a value of 1 means little shakiness, a
  value of 10 means strong shakiness. Default value is 5.
* **accuracy**  
  .IX Item "accuracy"
  Set the accuracy of the detection process. It must be a value in the
  range 1-15. A value of 1 means low accuracy, a value of 15 means high
  accuracy. Default value is 15.
* **stepsize**  
  .IX Item "stepsize"
  Set stepsize of the search process. The region around minimum is
  scanned with 1 pixel resolution. Default value is 6.
* **mincontrast**  
  .IX Item "mincontrast"
  Set minimum contrast. Below this value a local measurement field is
  discarded. Must be a floating point value in the range 0-1. Default
  value is 0.3.
* **tripod**  
  .IX Item "tripod"
  Set reference frame number for tripod mode.
  .Sp
  If enabled, the motion of the frames is compared to a reference frame
  in the filtered stream, identified by the specified number. The idea
  is to compensate all movements in a more-or-less static scene and keep
  the camera view absolutely still.
  .Sp
  If set to 0, it is disabled. The frames are counted starting from 1.
* **show**  
  .IX Item "show"
  Show fields and transforms in the resulting frames. It accepts an
  integer in the range 0-2. Default value is 0, which disables any
  visualization.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Use default values:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vidstabdetect
  .Ve
* ·  
  Analyze strongly shaky movie and put the results in file
  _mytransforms.trf_:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vidstabdetect=shakiness=10:accuracy=15:result="mytransforms.trf"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Visualize the result of internal transformations in the resulting
  video:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vidstabdetect=show=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Analyze a video with medium shakiness using **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i input -vf vidstabdetect=shakiness=5:show=1 dummy.avi
  .Ve

<a name="vidstabtransform"></a>

### vidstabtransform

.IX Subsection "vidstabtransform"
Video stabilization/deshaking: pass 2 of 2,
see **vidstabdetect** for pass 1.

Read a file with transform information for each frame and
apply/compensate them. Together with the **vidstabdetect**
filter this can be used to deshake videos. See also
&lt;**http://public.hronopik.de/vid.stab**&gt;. It is important to also use
the **unsharp** filter, see below.

To enable compilation of this filter you need to configure FFmpeg with
\f(CW`--enable-libvidstab\*(C'.

_Options_
.IX Subsection "Options"

* **input**  
  .IX Item "input"
  Set path to the file used to read the transforms. Default value is
  _transforms.trf_.
* **smoothing**  
  .IX Item "smoothing"
  Set the number of frames (value*2 + 1) used for lowpass filtering the
  camera movements. Default value is 10.
  .Sp
  For example a number of 10 means that 21 frames are used (10 in the
  past and 10 in the future) to smoothen the motion in the video. A
  larger value leads to a smoother video, but limits the acceleration of
  the camera (pan/tilt movements). 0 is a special case where a static
  camera is simulated.
* **optalgo**  
  .IX Item "optalgo"
  Set the camera path optimization algorithm.
  .Sp
  Accepted values are:
    * **gauss**  
      .IX Item "gauss"
      gaussian kernel low-pass filter on camera motion (default)
    * **avg**  
      .IX Item "avg"
      averaging on transformations
* **maxshift**  
  .IX Item "maxshift"
  Set maximal number of pixels to translate frames. Default value is -1,
  meaning no limit.
* **maxangle**  
  .IX Item "maxangle"
  Set maximal angle in radians (degree*PI/180) to rotate frames. Default
  value is -1, meaning no limit.
* **crop**  
  .IX Item "crop"
  Specify how to deal with borders that may be visible due to movement
  compensation.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **keep**  
      .IX Item "keep"
      keep image information from previous frame (default)
    * **black**  
      .IX Item "black"
      fill the border black
* **invert**  
  .IX Item "invert"
  Invert transforms if set to 1. Default value is 0.
* **relative**  
  .IX Item "relative"
  Consider transforms as relative to previous frame if set to 1,
  absolute if set to 0. Default value is 0.
* **zoom**  
  .IX Item "zoom"
  Set percentage to zoom. A positive value will result in a zoom-in
  effect, a negative value in a zoom-out effect. Default value is 0 (no
  zoom).
* **optzoom**  
  .IX Item "optzoom"
  Set optimal zooming to avoid borders.
  .Sp
  Accepted values are:
    * **0**  
      .IX Item "0"
      disabled
    * **1**  
      .IX Item "1"
      optimal static zoom value is determined (only very strong movements
      will lead to visible borders) (default)
    * **2**  
      .IX Item "2"
      optimal adaptive zoom value is determined (no borders will be
      visible), see **zoomspeed**
      .Sp
      Note that the value given at zoom is added to the one calculated here.
* **zoomspeed**  
  .IX Item "zoomspeed"
  Set percent to zoom maximally each frame (enabled when
  **optzoom** is set to 2). Range is from 0 to 5, default value is
  0.25.
* **interpol**  
  .IX Item "interpol"
  Specify type of interpolation.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **no**  
      .IX Item "no"
      no interpolation
    * **linear**  
      .IX Item "linear"
      linear only horizontal
    * **bilinear**  
      .IX Item "bilinear"
      linear in both directions (default)
    * **bicubic**  
      .IX Item "bicubic"
      cubic in both directions (slow)
* **tripod**  
  .IX Item "tripod"
  Enable virtual tripod mode if set to 1, which is equivalent to
  \f(CW`relative=0:smoothing=0\*(C'. Default value is 0.
  .Sp
  Use also \f(CW`tripod\*(C' option of **vidstabdetect**.
* **debug**  
  .IX Item "debug"
  Increase log verbosity if set to 1. Also the detected global motions
  are written to the temporary file _global\_motions.trf_. Default
  value is 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Use **ffmpeg** for a typical stabilization with default values:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i inp.mpeg -vf vidstabtransform,unsharp=5:5:0.8:3:3:0.4 inp_stabilized.mpeg
  .Ve
  .Sp
  Note the use of the **unsharp** filter which is always recommended.
* ·  
  Zoom in a bit more and load transform data from a given file:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vidstabtransform=zoom=5:input="mytransforms.trf"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Smoothen the video even more:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vidstabtransform=smoothing=30
  .Ve

<a name="vflip"></a>

### vflip

.IX Subsection "vflip"
Flip the input video vertically.

For example, to vertically flip a video with **ffmpeg**:

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i in.avi -vf "vflip" out.avi
.Ve

<a name="vfrdet"></a>

### vfrdet

.IX Subsection "vfrdet"
Detect variable frame rate video.

This filter tries to detect if the input is variable or constant frame rate.

At end it will output number of frames detected as having variable delta pts,
and ones with constant delta pts.
If there was frames with variable delta, than it will also show min and max delta
encountered.

<a name="vibrance"></a>

### vibrance

.IX Subsection "vibrance"
Boost or alter saturation.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **intensity**  
  .IX Item "intensity"
  Set strength of boost if positive value or strength of alter if negative value.
  Default is 0. Allowed range is from -2 to 2.
* **rbal**  
  .IX Item "rbal"
  Set the red balance. Default is 1. Allowed range is from -10 to 10.
* **gbal**  
  .IX Item "gbal"
  Set the green balance. Default is 1. Allowed range is from -10 to 10.
* **bbal**  
  .IX Item "bbal"
  Set the blue balance. Default is 1. Allowed range is from -10 to 10.
* **rlum**  
  .IX Item "rlum"
  Set the red luma coefficient.
* **glum**  
  .IX Item "glum"
  Set the green luma coefficient.
* **blum**  
  .IX Item "blum"
  Set the blue luma coefficient.

<a name="vignette"></a>

### vignette

.IX Subsection "vignette"
Make or reverse a natural vignetting effect.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **angle, a**  
  .IX Item "angle, a"
  Set lens angle expression as a number of radians.
  .Sp
  The value is clipped in the \f(CW`[0,PI/2]\*(C' range.
  .Sp
  Default value: \f(CW"PI/5"
* **x0**  
  .IX Item "x0"
* **y0**  
  .IX Item "y0"
  Set center coordinates expressions. Respectively \f(CW"w/2" and \f(CW"h/2"
  by default.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set forward/backward mode.
  .Sp
  Available modes are:
    * **forward**  
      .IX Item "forward"
      The larger the distance from the central point, the darker the image becomes.
    * **backward**  
      .IX Item "backward"
      The larger the distance from the central point, the brighter the image becomes.
      This can be used to reverse a vignette effect, though there is no automatic
      detection to extract the lens **angle** and other settings (yet). It can
      also be used to create a burning effect.
      .Sp
      Default value is **forward**.
* **eval**  
  .IX Item "eval"
  Set evaluation mode for the expressions (**angle**, **x0**, **y0**).
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **init**  
      .IX Item "init"
      Evaluate expressions only once during the filter initialization.
    * **frame**  
      .IX Item "frame"
      Evaluate expressions for each incoming frame. This is way slower than the
      **init** mode since it requires all the scalers to be re-computed, but it
      allows advanced dynamic expressions.
      .Sp
      Default value is **init**.
* **dither**  
  .IX Item "dither"
  Set dithering to reduce the circular banding effects. Default is \f(CW1
  (enabled).
* **aspect**  
  .IX Item "aspect"
  Set vignette aspect. This setting allows one to adjust the shape of the vignette.
  Setting this value to the \s-1SAR\s0 of the input will make a rectangular vignetting
  following the dimensions of the video.
  .Sp
  Default is \f(CW`1/1\*(C'.

_Expressions_
.IX Subsection "Expressions"

The **alpha**, **x0** and **y0** expressions can contain the
following parameters.

* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  input width and height
* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  the number of input frame, starting from 0
* **pts**  
  .IX Item "pts"
  the \s-1PTS\s0 (Presentation TimeStamp) time of the filtered video frame, expressed in
  _\s-1TB\s0_ units, \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined
* **r**  
  .IX Item "r"
  frame rate of the input video, \s-1NAN\s0 if the input frame rate is unknown
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  the \s-1PTS\s0 (Presentation TimeStamp) of the filtered video frame,
  expressed in seconds, \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined
* **tb**  
  .IX Item "tb"
  time base of the input video

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Apply simple strong vignetting effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vignette=PI/4
  .Ve
* ·  
  Make a flickering vignetting:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          vignette=PI/4+random(1)*PI/50\*(Aq:eval=frame
  .Ve

<a name="vmafmotion"></a>

### vmafmotion

.IX Subsection "vmafmotion"
Obtain the average vmaf motion score of a video.
It is one of the component filters of \s-1VMAF.\s0

The obtained average motion score is printed through the logging system.

In the below example the input file _ref.mpg_ is being processed and score
is computed.

.Vb 1
        ffmpeg -i ref.mpg -lavfi vmafmotion -f null -
.Ve

<a name="vstack"></a>

### vstack

.IX Subsection "vstack"
Stack input videos vertically.

All streams must be of same pixel format and of same width.

Note that this filter is faster than using **overlay** and **pad** filter
to create same output.

The filter accept the following option:

* **inputs**  
  .IX Item "inputs"
  Set number of input streams. Default is 2.
* **shortest**  
  .IX Item "shortest"
  If set to 1, force the output to terminate when the shortest input
  terminates. Default value is 0.

<a name="w3fdif"></a>

### w3fdif

.IX Subsection "w3fdif"
Deinterlace the input video (w3fdif\*(R" stands for \*(L"Weston 3 Field
Deinterlacing Filter).

Based on the process described by Martin Weston for \s-1BBC R&D,\s0 and
implemented based on the de-interlace algorithm written by Jim
Easterbrook for \s-1BBC R&D,\s0 the Weston 3 field deinterlacing filter
uses filter coefficients calculated by \s-1BBC R&D.\s0

There are two sets of filter coefficients, so called simple\*(R":
and complex\*(R". Which set of filter coefficients is used can
be set by passing an optional parameter:

* **filter**  
  .IX Item "filter"
  Set the interlacing filter coefficients. Accepts one of the following values:
    * **simple**  
      .IX Item "simple"
      Simple filter coefficient set.
    * **complex**  
      .IX Item "complex"
      More-complex filter coefficient set.
      .Sp
      Default value is **complex**.
* **deint**  
  .IX Item "deint"
  Specify which frames to deinterlace. Accept one of the following values:
    * **all**  
      .IX Item "all"
      Deinterlace all frames,
    * **interlaced**  
      .IX Item "interlaced"
      Only deinterlace frames marked as interlaced.
      .Sp
      Default value is **all**.

<a name="waveform"></a>

### waveform

.IX Subsection "waveform"
Video waveform monitor.

The waveform monitor plots color component intensity. By default luminance
only. Each column of the waveform corresponds to a column of pixels in the
source video.

It accepts the following options:

* **mode, m**  
  .IX Item "mode, m"
  Can be either \f(CW`row\*(C', or \f(CW\*(C\`column\*(C'. Default is \f(CW\*(C\`column\*(C'.
  In row mode, the graph on the left side represents color component value 0 and
  the right side represents value = 255. In column mode, the top side represents
  color component value = 0 and bottom side represents value = 255.
* **intensity, i**  
  .IX Item "intensity, i"
  Set intensity. Smaller values are useful to find out how many values of the same
  luminance are distributed across input rows/columns.
  Default value is \f(CW0.04. Allowed range is [0, 1].
* **mirror, r**  
  .IX Item "mirror, r"
  Set mirroring mode. \f(CW0 means unmirrored, \f(CW1 means mirrored.
  In mirrored mode, higher values will be represented on the left
  side for \f(CW`row\*(C' mode and at the top for \f(CW\*(C\`column\*(C' mode. Default is
  \f(CW1 (mirrored).
* **display, d**  
  .IX Item "display, d"
  Set display mode.
  It accepts the following values:
    * **overlay**  
      .IX Item "overlay"
      Presents information identical to that in the \f(CW`parade\*(C', except
      that the graphs representing color components are superimposed directly
      over one another.
      .Sp
      This display mode makes it easier to spot relative differences or similarities
      in overlapping areas of the color components that are supposed to be identical,
      such as neutral whites, grays, or blacks.
    * **stack**  
      .IX Item "stack"
      Display separate graph for the color components side by side in
      \f(CW`row\*(C' mode or one below the other in \f(CW\*(C\`column\*(C' mode.
    * **parade**  
      .IX Item "parade"
      Display separate graph for the color components side by side in
      \f(CW`column\*(C' mode or one below the other in \f(CW\*(C\`row\*(C' mode.
      .Sp
      Using this display mode makes it easy to spot color casts in the highlights
      and shadows of an image, by comparing the contours of the top and the bottom
      graphs of each waveform. Since whites, grays, and blacks are characterized
      by exactly equal amounts of red, green, and blue, neutral areas of the picture
      should display three waveforms of roughly equal width/height. If not, the
      correction is easy to perform by making level adjustments the three waveforms.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`stack\*(C'.
* **components, c**  
  .IX Item "components, c"
  Set which color components to display. Default is 1, which means only luminance
  or red color component if input is in \s-1RGB\s0 colorspace. If is set for example to
  7 it will display all 3 (if) available color components.
* **envelope, e**  
  .IX Item "envelope, e"
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      No envelope, this is default.
    * **instant**  
      .IX Item "instant"
      Instant envelope, minimum and maximum values presented in graph will be easily
      visible even with small \f(CW`step\*(C' value.
    * **peak**  
      .IX Item "peak"
      Hold minimum and maximum values presented in graph across time. This way you
      can still spot out of range values without constantly looking at waveforms.
    * **peak+instant**  
      .IX Item "peak+instant"
      Peak and instant envelope combined together.
* **filter, f**  
  .IX Item "filter, f"
    * **lowpass**  
      .IX Item "lowpass"
      No filtering, this is default.
    * **flat**  
      .IX Item "flat"
      Luma and chroma combined together.
    * **aflat**  
      .IX Item "aflat"
      Similar as above, but shows difference between blue and red chroma.
    * **xflat**  
      .IX Item "xflat"
      Similar as above, but use different colors.
    * **chroma**  
      .IX Item "chroma"
      Displays only chroma.
    * **color**  
      .IX Item "color"
      Displays actual color value on waveform.
    * **acolor**  
      .IX Item "acolor"
      Similar as above, but with luma showing frequency of chroma values.
* **graticule, g**  
  .IX Item "graticule, g"
  Set which graticule to display.
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Do not display graticule.
    * **green**  
      .IX Item "green"
      Display green graticule showing legal broadcast ranges.
    * **orange**  
      .IX Item "orange"
      Display orange graticule showing legal broadcast ranges.
* **opacity, o**  
  .IX Item "opacity, o"
  Set graticule opacity.
* **flags, fl**  
  .IX Item "flags, fl"
  Set graticule flags.
    * **numbers**  
      .IX Item "numbers"
      Draw numbers above lines. By default enabled.
    * **dots**  
      .IX Item "dots"
      Draw dots instead of lines.
* **scale, s**  
  .IX Item "scale, s"
  Set scale used for displaying graticule.
    * **digital**  
      .IX Item "digital"
    * **millivolts**  
      .IX Item "millivolts"
    * **ire**  
      .IX Item "ire"
      .Sp
      Default is digital.
* **bgopacity, b**  
  .IX Item "bgopacity, b"
  Set background opacity.

<a name="weave-doubleweave"></a>

### weave, doubleweave

.IX Subsection "weave, doubleweave"
The \f(CW`weave\*(C' takes a field-based video input and join
each two sequential fields into single frame, producing a new double
height clip with half the frame rate and half the frame count.

The \f(CW`doubleweave\*(C' works same as \f(CW\*(C\`weave\*(C' but without
halving frame rate and frame count.

It accepts the following option:

* **first\_field**  
  .IX Item "first_field"
  Set first field. Available values are:
    * **top, t**  
      .IX Item "top, t"
      Set the frame as top-field-first.
    * **bottom, b**  
      .IX Item "bottom, b"
      Set the frame as bottom-field-first.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Interlace video using **select** and **separatefields** filter:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          separatefields,select=eq(mod(n,4),0)+eq(mod(n,4),3),weave
  .Ve

<a name="xbr"></a>

### xbr

.IX Subsection "xbr"
Apply the xBR high-quality magnification filter which is designed for pixel
art. It follows a set of edge-detection rules, see
&lt;**https://forums.libretro.com/t/xbr-algorithm-tutorial/123**&gt;.

It accepts the following option:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  Set the scaling dimension: \f(CW2 for \f(CW`2xBR\*(C', \f(CW3 for
  \f(CW`3xBR\*(C' and \f(CW4 for \f(CW\*(C\`4xBR\*(C'.
  Default is \f(CW3.

<a name="xstack"></a>

### xstack

.IX Subsection "xstack"
Stack video inputs into custom layout.

All streams must be of same pixel format.

The filter accept the following option:

* **inputs**  
  .IX Item "inputs"
  Set number of input streams. Default is 2.
* **layout**  
  .IX Item "layout"
  Specify layout of inputs.
  This option requires the desired layout configuration to be explicitly set by the user.
  This sets position of each video input in output. Each input
  is separated by '|'.
  The first number represents the column, and the second number represents the row.
  Numbers start at 0 and are separated by '_'. Optionally one can use wX and hX,
  where X is video input from which to take width or height.
  Multiple values can be used when separated by '+'. In such
  case values are summed together.
* **shortest**  
  .IX Item "shortest"
  If set to 1, force the output to terminate when the shortest input
  terminates. Default value is 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Display 4 inputs into 2x2 grid,
  note that if inputs are of different sizes unused gaps might appear,
  as not all of output video is used.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          xstack=inputs=4:layout=0_0|0_h0|w0_0|w0_h0
  .Ve
* ·  
  Display 4 inputs into 1x4 grid,
  note that if inputs are of different sizes unused gaps might appear,
  as not all of output video is used.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          xstack=inputs=4:layout=0_0|0_h0|0_h0+h1|0_h0+h1+h2
  .Ve
* ·  
  Display 9 inputs into 3x3 grid,
  note that if inputs are of different sizes unused gaps might appear,
  as not all of output video is used.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          xstack=inputs=9:layout=w3_0|w3_h0+h2|w3_h0|0_h4|0_0|w3+w1_0|0_h1+h2|w3+w1_h0|w3+w1_h1+h2
  .Ve

<a name="yadif"></a>

### yadif

.IX Subsection "yadif"
Deinterlace the input video (yadif\*(R" means \*(L"yet another deinterlacing
filter).

It accepts the following parameters:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  The interlacing mode to adopt. It accepts one of the following values:
    * **0, send\_frame**  
      .IX Item "0, send_frame"
      Output one frame for each frame.
    * **1, send\_field**  
      .IX Item "1, send_field"
      Output one frame for each field.
    * **2, send\_frame\_nospatial**  
      .IX Item "2, send_frame_nospatial"
      Like \f(CW`send\_frame\*(C', but it skips the spatial interlacing check.
    * **3, send\_field\_nospatial**  
      .IX Item "3, send_field_nospatial"
      Like \f(CW`send\_field\*(C', but it skips the spatial interlacing check.
      .Sp
      The default value is \f(CW`send\_frame\*(C'.
* **parity**  
  .IX Item "parity"
  The picture field parity assumed for the input interlaced video. It accepts one
  of the following values:
    * **0, tff**  
      .IX Item "0, tff"
      Assume the top field is first.
    * **1, bff**  
      .IX Item "1, bff"
      Assume the bottom field is first.
    * **-1, auto**  
      .IX Item "-1, auto"
      Enable automatic detection of field parity.
      .Sp
      The default value is \f(CW`auto\*(C'.
      If the interlacing is unknown or the decoder does not export this information,
      top field first will be assumed.
* **deint**  
  .IX Item "deint"
  Specify which frames to deinterlace. Accept one of the following
  values:
    * **0, all**  
      .IX Item "0, all"
      Deinterlace all frames.
    * **1, interlaced**  
      .IX Item "1, interlaced"
      Only deinterlace frames marked as interlaced.
      .Sp
      The default value is \f(CW`all\*(C'.

<a name="yadif_cuda"></a>

### yadif_cuda

.IX Subsection "yadif_cuda"
Deinterlace the input video using the **yadif** algorithm, but implemented
in \s-1CUDA\s0 so that it can work as part of a \s-1GPU\s0 accelerated pipeline with nvdec
and/or nvenc.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  The interlacing mode to adopt. It accepts one of the following values:
    * **0, send\_frame**  
      .IX Item "0, send_frame"
      Output one frame for each frame.
    * **1, send\_field**  
      .IX Item "1, send_field"
      Output one frame for each field.
    * **2, send\_frame\_nospatial**  
      .IX Item "2, send_frame_nospatial"
      Like \f(CW`send\_frame\*(C', but it skips the spatial interlacing check.
    * **3, send\_field\_nospatial**  
      .IX Item "3, send_field_nospatial"
      Like \f(CW`send\_field\*(C', but it skips the spatial interlacing check.
      .Sp
      The default value is \f(CW`send\_frame\*(C'.
* **parity**  
  .IX Item "parity"
  The picture field parity assumed for the input interlaced video. It accepts one
  of the following values:
    * **0, tff**  
      .IX Item "0, tff"
      Assume the top field is first.
    * **1, bff**  
      .IX Item "1, bff"
      Assume the bottom field is first.
    * **-1, auto**  
      .IX Item "-1, auto"
      Enable automatic detection of field parity.
      .Sp
      The default value is \f(CW`auto\*(C'.
      If the interlacing is unknown or the decoder does not export this information,
      top field first will be assumed.
* **deint**  
  .IX Item "deint"
  Specify which frames to deinterlace. Accept one of the following
  values:
    * **0, all**  
      .IX Item "0, all"
      Deinterlace all frames.
    * **1, interlaced**  
      .IX Item "1, interlaced"
      Only deinterlace frames marked as interlaced.
      .Sp
      The default value is \f(CW`all\*(C'.

<a name="zoompan"></a>

### zoompan

.IX Subsection "zoompan"
Apply Zoom & Pan effect.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **zoom, z**  
  .IX Item "zoom, z"
  Set the zoom expression. Default is 1.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  Set the x and y expression. Default is 0.
* **d**  
  .IX Item "d"
  Set the duration expression in number of frames.
  This sets for how many number of frames effect will last for
  single input image.
* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
  Set the output image size, default is 'hd720'.
* **fps**  
  .IX Item "fps"
  Set the output frame rate, default is '25'.

Each expression can contain the following constants:

* **in_w, iw**  
  .IX Item "in_w, iw"
  Input width.
* **in_h, ih**  
  .IX Item "in_h, ih"
  Input height.
* **out_w, ow**  
  .IX Item "out_w, ow"
  Output width.
* **out_h, oh**  
  .IX Item "out_h, oh"
  Output height.
* **in**  
  .IX Item "in"
  Input frame count.
* **on**  
  .IX Item "on"
  Output frame count.
* **x**  
  .IX Item "x"
* **y**  
  .IX Item "y"
  Last calculated 'x' and 'y' position from 'x' and 'y' expression
  for current input frame.
* **px**  
  .IX Item "px"
* **py**  
  .IX Item "py"
  'x' and 'y' of last output frame of previous input frame or 0 when there was
  not yet such frame (first input frame).
* **zoom**  
  .IX Item "zoom"
  Last calculated zoom from 'z' expression for current input frame.
* **pzoom**  
  .IX Item "pzoom"
  Last calculated zoom of last output frame of previous input frame.
* **duration**  
  .IX Item "duration"
  Number of output frames for current input frame. Calculated from 'd' expression
  for each input frame.
* **pduration**  
  .IX Item "pduration"
  number of output frames created for previous input frame
* **a**  
  .IX Item "a"
  Rational number: input width / input height
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  sample aspect ratio
* **dar**  
  .IX Item "dar"
  display aspect ratio

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Zoom-in up to 1.5 and pan at same time to some spot near center of picture:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          zoompan=z=min(zoom+0.0015,1.5)\*(Aq:d=700:x=\*(Aqif(gte(zoom,1.5),x,x+1/a)\*(Aq:y=\*(Aqif(gte(zoom,1.5),y,y+1)\*(Aq:s=640x360
  .Ve
* ·  
  Zoom-in up to 1.5 and pan always at center of picture:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          zoompan=z=min(zoom+0.0015,1.5)\*(Aq:d=700:x=\*(Aqiw/2-(iw/zoom/2)\*(Aq:y=\*(Aqih/2-(ih/zoom/2)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Same as above but without pausing:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          zoompan=z=min(max(zoom,pzoom)+0.0015,1.5)\*(Aq:d=1:x=\*(Aqiw/2-(iw/zoom/2)\*(Aq:y=\*(Aqih/2-(ih/zoom/2)\*(Aq
  .Ve

<a name="zscale"></a>

### zscale

.IX Subsection "zscale"
Scale (resize) the input video, using the z.lib library:
&lt;**https://github.com/sekrit-twc/zimg**&gt;. To enable compilation of this
filter, you need to configure FFmpeg with \f(CW`--enable-libzimg\*(C'.

The zscale filter forces the output display aspect ratio to be the same
as the input, by changing the output sample aspect ratio.

If the input image format is different from the format requested by
the next filter, the zscale filter will convert the input to the
requested format.

_Options_
.IX Subsection "Options"

The filter accepts the following options.

* **width, w**  
  .IX Item "width, w"
* **height, h**  
  .IX Item "height, h"
  Set the output video dimension expression. Default value is the input
  dimension.
  .Sp
  If the _width_ or _w_ value is 0, the input width is used for
  the output. If the _height_ or _h_ value is 0, the input height
  is used for the output.
  .Sp
  If one and only one of the values is -n with n &gt;= 1, the zscale filter
  will use a value that maintains the aspect ratio of the input image,
  calculated from the other specified dimension. After that it will,
  however, make sure that the calculated dimension is divisible by n and
  adjust the value if necessary.
  .Sp
  If both values are -n with n &gt;= 1, the behavior will be identical to
  both values being set to 0 as previously detailed.
  .Sp
  See below for the list of accepted constants for use in the dimension
  expression.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set the video size. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
* **dither, d**  
  .IX Item "dither, d"
  Set the dither type.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _none_  
      .IX Item "none"
    * _ordered_  
      .IX Item "ordered"
    * _random_  
      .IX Item "random"
    * _error\_diffusion_  
      .IX Item "error_diffusion"
      .Sp
      Default is none.
* **filter, f**  
  .IX Item "filter, f"
  Set the resize filter type.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _point_  
      .IX Item "point"
    * _bilinear_  
      .IX Item "bilinear"
    * _bicubic_  
      .IX Item "bicubic"
    * _spline16_  
      .IX Item "spline16"
    * _spline36_  
      .IX Item "spline36"
    * _lanczos_  
      .IX Item "lanczos"
      .Sp
      Default is bilinear.
* **range, r**  
  .IX Item "range, r"
  Set the color range.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _limited_  
      .IX Item "limited"
    * _full_  
      .IX Item "full"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **primaries, p**  
  .IX Item "primaries, p"
  Set the color primaries.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _709_  
      .IX Item "709"
    * _unspecified_  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
    * _170m_  
      .IX Item "170m"
    * _240m_  
      .IX Item "240m"
    * _2020_  
      .IX Item "2020"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **transfer, t**  
  .IX Item "transfer, t"
  Set the transfer characteristics.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _709_  
      .IX Item "709"
    * _unspecified_  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
    * _601_  
      .IX Item "601"
    * _linear_  
      .IX Item "linear"
    * _2020\_10_  
      .IX Item "2020_10"
    * _2020\_12_  
      .IX Item "2020_12"
    * _smpte2084_  
      .IX Item "smpte2084"
    * _iec61966-2-1_  
      .IX Item "iec61966-2-1"
    * _arib-std-b67_  
      .IX Item "arib-std-b67"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **matrix, m**  
  .IX Item "matrix, m"
  Set the colorspace matrix.
  .Sp
  Possible value are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _709_  
      .IX Item "709"
    * _unspecified_  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
    * _470bg_  
      .IX Item "470bg"
    * _170m_  
      .IX Item "170m"
    * _2020\_ncl_  
      .IX Item "2020_ncl"
    * _2020\_cl_  
      .IX Item "2020_cl"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **rangein, rin**  
  .IX Item "rangein, rin"
  Set the input color range.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _limited_  
      .IX Item "limited"
    * _full_  
      .IX Item "full"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **primariesin, pin**  
  .IX Item "primariesin, pin"
  Set the input color primaries.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _709_  
      .IX Item "709"
    * _unspecified_  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
    * _170m_  
      .IX Item "170m"
    * _240m_  
      .IX Item "240m"
    * _2020_  
      .IX Item "2020"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **transferin, tin**  
  .IX Item "transferin, tin"
  Set the input transfer characteristics.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _709_  
      .IX Item "709"
    * _unspecified_  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
    * _601_  
      .IX Item "601"
    * _linear_  
      .IX Item "linear"
    * _2020\_10_  
      .IX Item "2020_10"
    * _2020\_12_  
      .IX Item "2020_12"
      .Sp
      Default is same as input.
* **matrixin, min**  
  .IX Item "matrixin, min"
  Set the input colorspace matrix.
  .Sp
  Possible value are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _709_  
      .IX Item "709"
    * _unspecified_  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
    * _470bg_  
      .IX Item "470bg"
    * _170m_  
      .IX Item "170m"
    * _2020\_ncl_  
      .IX Item "2020_ncl"
    * _2020\_cl_  
      .IX Item "2020_cl"
* **chromal, c**  
  .IX Item "chromal, c"
  Set the output chroma location.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _left_  
      .IX Item "left"
    * _center_  
      .IX Item "center"
    * _topleft_  
      .IX Item "topleft"
    * _top_  
      .IX Item "top"
    * _bottomleft_  
      .IX Item "bottomleft"
    * _bottom_  
      .IX Item "bottom"
* **chromalin, cin**  
  .IX Item "chromalin, cin"
  Set the input chroma location.
  .Sp
  Possible values are:
    * _input_  
      .IX Item "input"
    * _left_  
      .IX Item "left"
    * _center_  
      .IX Item "center"
    * _topleft_  
      .IX Item "topleft"
    * _top_  
      .IX Item "top"
    * _bottomleft_  
      .IX Item "bottomleft"
    * _bottom_  
      .IX Item "bottom"
* **npl**  
  .IX Item "npl"
  Set the nominal peak luminance.

The values of the **w** and **h** options are expressions
containing the following constants:

* _in\_w_  
  .IX Item "in_w"
* _in\_h_  
  .IX Item "in_h"
  The input width and height
* _iw_  
  .IX Item "iw"
* _ih_  
  .IX Item "ih"
  These are the same as _in\_w_ and _in\_h_.
* _out\_w_  
  .IX Item "out_w"
* _out\_h_  
  .IX Item "out_h"
  The output (scaled) width and height
* _ow_  
  .IX Item "ow"
* _oh_  
  .IX Item "oh"
  These are the same as _out\_w_ and _out\_h_
* _a_  
  .IX Item "a"
  The same as _iw_ / _ih_
* _sar_  
  .IX Item "sar"
  input sample aspect ratio
* _dar_  
  .IX Item "dar"
  The input display aspect ratio. Calculated from \f(CW`(iw / ih) * sar\*(C'.
* _hsub_  
  .IX Item "hsub"
* _vsub_  
  .IX Item "vsub"
  horizontal and vertical input chroma subsample values. For example for the
  pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_ is 1.
* _ohsub_  
  .IX Item "ohsub"
* _ovsub_  
  .IX Item "ovsub"
  horizontal and vertical output chroma subsample values. For example for the
  pixel format yuv422p\*(R" _hsub_ is 2 and _vsub_ is 1.

<a name="video-sources"></a>

# Video Sources

.IX Header "VIDEO SOURCES"
Below is a description of the currently available video sources.

<a name="buffer"></a>

### buffer

.IX Subsection "buffer"
Buffer video frames, and make them available to the filter chain.

This source is mainly intended for a programmatic use, in particular
through the interface defined in _libavfilter/vsrc\_buffer.h_.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **video\_size**  
  .IX Item "video_size"
  Specify the size (width and height) of the buffered video frames. For the
  syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
* **width**  
  .IX Item "width"
  The input video width.
* **height**  
  .IX Item "height"
  The input video height.
* **pix\_fmt**  
  .IX Item "pix_fmt"
  A string representing the pixel format of the buffered video frames.
  It may be a number corresponding to a pixel format, or a pixel format
  name.
* **time\_base**  
  .IX Item "time_base"
  Specify the timebase assumed by the timestamps of the buffered frames.
* **frame\_rate**  
  .IX Item "frame_rate"
  Specify the frame rate expected for the video stream.
* **pixel_aspect, sar**  
  .IX Item "pixel_aspect, sar"
  The sample (pixel) aspect ratio of the input video.
* **sws\_param**  
  .IX Item "sws_param"
  Specify the optional parameters to be used for the scale filter which
  is automatically inserted when an input change is detected in the
  input size or format.
* **hw\_frames\_ctx**  
  .IX Item "hw_frames_ctx"
  When using a hardware pixel format, this should be a reference to an
  AVHWFramesContext describing input frames.

For example:

.Vb 1
        buffer=width=320:height=240:pix_fmt=yuv410p:time_base=1/24:sar=1
.Ve

will instruct the source to accept video frames with size 320x240 and
with format yuv410p\*(R", assuming 1/24 as the timestamps timebase and
square pixels (1:1 sample aspect ratio).
Since the pixel format with name yuv410p\*(R" corresponds to the number 6
(check the enum AVPixelFormat definition in _libavutil/pixfmt.h_),
this example corresponds to:

.Vb 1
        buffer=size=320x240:pixfmt=6:time_base=1/24:pixel_aspect=1/1
.Ve

Alternatively, the options can be specified as a flat string, but this
syntax is deprecated:

_width_:_height_:_pix\_fmt_:_time\_base.num_:_time\_base.den_:_pixel\_aspect.num_:_pixel\_aspect.den_[:_sws\_param_]

<a name="cellauto"></a>

### cellauto

.IX Subsection "cellauto"
Create a pattern generated by an elementary cellular automaton.

The initial state of the cellular automaton can be defined through the
**filename** and **pattern** options. If such options are
not specified an initial state is created randomly.

At each new frame a new row in the video is filled with the result of
the cellular automaton next generation. The behavior when the whole
frame is filled is defined by the **scroll** option.

This source accepts the following options:

* **filename, f**  
  .IX Item "filename, f"
  Read the initial cellular automaton state, i.e. the starting row, from
  the specified file.
  In the file, each non-whitespace character is considered an alive
  cell, a newline will terminate the row, and further characters in the
  file will be ignored.
* **pattern, p**  
  .IX Item "pattern, p"
  Read the initial cellular automaton state, i.e. the starting row, from
  the specified string.
  .Sp
  Each non-whitespace character in the string is considered an alive
  cell, a newline will terminate the row, and further characters in the
  string will be ignored.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set the video rate, that is the number of frames generated per second.
  Default is 25.
* **random_fill_ratio, ratio**  
  .IX Item "random_fill_ratio, ratio"
  Set the random fill ratio for the initial cellular automaton row. It
  is a floating point number value ranging from 0 to 1, defaults to
  1/PHI.
  .Sp
  This option is ignored when a file or a pattern is specified.
* **random_seed, seed**  
  .IX Item "random_seed, seed"
  Set the seed for filling randomly the initial row, must be an integer
  included between 0 and \s-1UINT32_MAX.\s0 If not specified, or if explicitly
  set to -1, the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best
  effort basis.
* **rule**  
  .IX Item "rule"
  Set the cellular automaton rule, it is a number ranging from 0 to 255.
  Default value is 110.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set the size of the output video. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  .Sp
  If **filename** or **pattern** is specified, the size is set
  by default to the width of the specified initial state row, and the
  height is set to _width_ * \s-1PHI.\s0
  .Sp
  If **size** is set, it must contain the width of the specified
  pattern string, and the specified pattern will be centered in the
  larger row.
  .Sp
  If a filename or a pattern string is not specified, the size value
  defaults to 320x518\*(R" (used for a randomly generated initial state).
* **scroll**  
  .IX Item "scroll"
  If set to 1, scroll the output upward when all the rows in the output
  have been already filled. If set to 0, the new generated row will be
  written over the top row just after the bottom row is filled.
  Defaults to 1.
* **start_full, full**  
  .IX Item "start_full, full"
  If set to 1, completely fill the output with generated rows before
  outputting the first frame.
  This is the default behavior, for disabling set the value to 0.
* **stitch**  
  .IX Item "stitch"
  If set to 1, stitch the left and right row edges together.
  This is the default behavior, for disabling set the value to 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Read the initial state from _pattern_, and specify an output of
  size 200x400.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          cellauto=f=pattern:s=200x400
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate a random initial row with a width of 200 cells, with a fill
  ratio of 2/3:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          cellauto=ratio=2/3:s=200x200
  .Ve
* ·  
  Create a pattern generated by rule 18 starting by a single alive cell
  centered on an initial row with width 100:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          cellauto=p=@s=100x400:full=0:rule=18
  .Ve
* ·  
  Specify a more elaborated initial pattern:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          cellauto=p=@@ @ @@\*(Aq:s=100x400:full=0:rule=18
  .Ve

<a name="coreimagesrc"></a>

### coreimagesrc

.IX Subsection "coreimagesrc"
Video source generated on \s-1GPU\s0 using Apple's CoreImage \s-1API\s0 on \s-1OSX.\s0

This video source is a specialized version of the **coreimage** video filter.
Use a core image generator at the beginning of the applied filterchain to
generate the content.

The coreimagesrc video source accepts the following options:

* **list\_generators**  
  .IX Item "list_generators"
  List all available generators along with all their respective options as well as
  possible minimum and maximum values along with the default values.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          list_generators=true
  .Ve
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the size of the sourced video. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  The default value is \f(CW`320x240\*(C'.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Specify the frame rate of the sourced video, as the number of frames
  generated per second. It has to be a string in the format
  _frame\_rate\_num_/_frame\_rate\_den_, an integer number, a floating point
  number or a valid video frame rate abbreviation. The default value is
  25\*(R".
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  Set the sample aspect ratio of the sourced video.
* **duration, d**  
  .IX Item "duration, d"
  Set the duration of the sourced video. See
  **the Time duration section in the ffmpeg-utils\|(1) manual**
  for the accepted syntax.
  .Sp
  If not specified, or the expressed duration is negative, the video is
  supposed to be generated forever.

Additionally, all options of the **coreimage** video filter are accepted.
A complete filterchain can be used for further processing of the
generated input without CPU-HOST transfer. See **coreimage** documentation
and examples for details.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Use CIQRCodeGenerator to create a \s-1QR\s0 code for the FFmpeg homepage,
  given as complete and escaped command-line for Apple's standard bash shell:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -f lavfi -i coreimagesrc=s=100x100:filter=CIQRCodeGenerator@inputMessage=https\e\e\e\e\e://FFmpeg.org/@inputCorrectionLevel=H -frames:v 1 QRCode.png
  .Ve
  .Sp
  This example is equivalent to the QRCode example of **coreimage** without the
  need for a nullsrc video source.

<a name="mandelbrot"></a>

### mandelbrot

.IX Subsection "mandelbrot"
Generate a Mandelbrot set fractal, and progressively zoom towards the
point specified with _start\_x_ and _start\_y_.

This source accepts the following options:

* **end\_pts**  
  .IX Item "end_pts"
  Set the terminal pts value. Default value is 400.
* **end\_scale**  
  .IX Item "end_scale"
  Set the terminal scale value.
  Must be a floating point value. Default value is 0.3.
* **inner**  
  .IX Item "inner"
  Set the inner coloring mode, that is the algorithm used to draw the
  Mandelbrot fractal internal region.
  .Sp
  It shall assume one of the following values:
    * **black**  
      .IX Item "black"
      Set black mode.
    * **convergence**  
      .IX Item "convergence"
      Show time until convergence.
    * **mincol**  
      .IX Item "mincol"
      Set color based on point closest to the origin of the iterations.
    * **period**  
      .IX Item "period"
      Set period mode.
      .Sp
      Default value is _mincol_.
* **bailout**  
  .IX Item "bailout"
  Set the bailout value. Default value is 10.0.
* **maxiter**  
  .IX Item "maxiter"
  Set the maximum of iterations performed by the rendering
  algorithm. Default value is 7189.
* **outer**  
  .IX Item "outer"
  Set outer coloring mode.
  It shall assume one of following values:
    * **iteration\_count**  
      .IX Item "iteration_count"
      Set iteration cound mode.
    * **normalized\_iteration\_count**  
      .IX Item "normalized_iteration_count"
      set normalized iteration count mode.
      .Sp
      Default value is _normalized\_iteration\_count_.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set frame rate, expressed as number of frames per second. Default
  value is 25\*(R".
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set frame size. For the syntax of this option, check the Video
  size section in the ffmpeg-utils manual. Default value is \*(L"640x480\*(R".
* **start\_scale**  
  .IX Item "start_scale"
  Set the initial scale value. Default value is 3.0.
* **start\_x**  
  .IX Item "start_x"
  Set the initial x position. Must be a floating point value between
  -100 and 100. Default value is -0.743643887037158704752191506114774.
* **start\_y**  
  .IX Item "start_y"
  Set the initial y position. Must be a floating point value between
  -100 and 100. Default value is -0.131825904205311970493132056385139.

<a name="mptestsrc"></a>

### mptestsrc

.IX Subsection "mptestsrc"
Generate various test patterns, as generated by the MPlayer test filter.

The size of the generated video is fixed, and is 256x256.
This source is useful in particular for testing encoding features.

This source accepts the following options:

* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Specify the frame rate of the sourced video, as the number of frames
  generated per second. It has to be a string in the format
  _frame\_rate\_num_/_frame\_rate\_den_, an integer number, a floating point
  number or a valid video frame rate abbreviation. The default value is
  25\*(R".
* **duration, d**  
  .IX Item "duration, d"
  Set the duration of the sourced video. See
  **the Time duration section in the ffmpeg-utils\|(1) manual**
  for the accepted syntax.
  .Sp
  If not specified, or the expressed duration is negative, the video is
  supposed to be generated forever.
* **test, t**  
  .IX Item "test, t"
  Set the number or the name of the test to perform. Supported tests are:
    * **dc\_luma**  
      .IX Item "dc_luma"
    * **dc\_chroma**  
      .IX Item "dc_chroma"
    * **freq\_luma**  
      .IX Item "freq_luma"
    * **freq\_chroma**  
      .IX Item "freq_chroma"
    * **amp\_luma**  
      .IX Item "amp_luma"
    * **amp\_chroma**  
      .IX Item "amp_chroma"
    * **cbp**  
      .IX Item "cbp"
    * **mv**  
      .IX Item "mv"
    * **ring1**  
      .IX Item "ring1"
    * **ring2**  
      .IX Item "ring2"
    * **all**  
      .IX Item "all"
      .Sp
      Default value is all\*(R", which will cycle through the list of all tests.

Some examples:

.Vb 1
        mptestsrc=t=dc_luma
.Ve

will generate a dc_luma\*(R" test pattern.

<a name="frei0r_src"></a>

### frei0r_src

.IX Subsection "frei0r_src"
Provide a frei0r source.

To enable compilation of this filter you need to install the frei0r
header and configure FFmpeg with \f(CW`--enable-frei0r\*(C'.

This source accepts the following parameters:

* **size**  
  .IX Item "size"
  The size of the video to generate. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
* **framerate**  
  .IX Item "framerate"
  The framerate of the generated video. It may be a string of the form
  _num_/_den_ or a frame rate abbreviation.
* **filter\_name**  
  .IX Item "filter_name"
  The name to the frei0r source to load. For more information regarding frei0r and
  how to set the parameters, read the **frei0r** section in the video filters
  documentation.
* **filter\_params**  
  .IX Item "filter_params"
  A '|'-separated list of parameters to pass to the frei0r source.

For example, to generate a frei0r partik0l source with size 200x200
and frame rate 10 which is overlaid on the overlay filter main input:

.Vb 1
        frei0r_src=size=200x200:framerate=10:filter_name=partik0l:filter_params=1234 [overlay]; [in][overlay] overlay
.Ve

<a name="life"></a>

### life

.IX Subsection "life"
Generate a life pattern.

This source is based on a generalization of John Conway's life game.

The sourced input represents a life grid, each pixel represents a cell
which can be in one of two possible states, alive or dead. Every cell
interacts with its eight neighbours, which are the cells that are
horizontally, vertically, or diagonally adjacent.

At each interaction the grid evolves according to the adopted rule,
which specifies the number of neighbor alive cells which will make a
cell stay alive or born. The **rule** option allows one to specify
the rule to adopt.

This source accepts the following options:

* **filename, f**  
  .IX Item "filename, f"
  Set the file from which to read the initial grid state. In the file,
  each non-whitespace character is considered an alive cell, and newline
  is used to delimit the end of each row.
  .Sp
  If this option is not specified, the initial grid is generated
  randomly.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set the video rate, that is the number of frames generated per second.
  Default is 25.
* **random_fill_ratio, ratio**  
  .IX Item "random_fill_ratio, ratio"
  Set the random fill ratio for the initial random grid. It is a
  floating point number value ranging from 0 to 1, defaults to 1/PHI.
  It is ignored when a file is specified.
* **random_seed, seed**  
  .IX Item "random_seed, seed"
  Set the seed for filling the initial random grid, must be an integer
  included between 0 and \s-1UINT32_MAX.\s0 If not specified, or if explicitly
  set to -1, the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best
  effort basis.
* **rule**  
  .IX Item "rule"
  Set the life rule.
  .Sp
  A rule can be specified with a code of the kind "S_\s-1NS\s0_/B_\s-1NB\s0_",
  where _\s-1NS\s0_ and _\s-1NB\s0_ are sequences of numbers in the range 0-8,
  _\s-1NS\s0_ specifies the number of alive neighbor cells which make a
  live cell stay alive, and _\s-1NB\s0_ the number of alive neighbor cells
  which make a dead cell to become alive (i.e. to born\*(R").
  s\*(R" and \*(L"b\*(R" can be used in place of \*(L"S\*(R" and \*(L"B\*(R", respectively.
  .Sp
  Alternatively a rule can be specified by an 18-bits integer. The 9
  high order bits are used to encode the next cell state if it is alive
  for each number of neighbor alive cells, the low order bits specify
  the rule for borning\*(R" new cells. Higher order bits encode for an
  higher number of neighbor cells.
  For example the number 6153 = \f(CW`(12&lt;&lt;9)+9\*(C' specifies a stay alive
  rule of 12 and a born rule of 9, which corresponds to S23/B03\*(R".
  .Sp
  Default value is S23/B3\*(R", which is the original Conway's game of life
  rule, and will keep a cell alive if it has 2 or 3 neighbor alive
  cells, and will born a new cell if there are three alive cells around
  a dead cell.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set the size of the output video. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  .Sp
  If **filename** is specified, the size is set by default to the
  same size of the input file. If **size** is set, it must contain
  the size specified in the input file, and the initial grid defined in
  that file is centered in the larger resulting area.
  .Sp
  If a filename is not specified, the size value defaults to 320x240\*(R"
  (used for a randomly generated initial grid).
* **stitch**  
  .IX Item "stitch"
  If set to 1, stitch the left and right grid edges together, and the
  top and bottom edges also. Defaults to 1.
* **mold**  
  .IX Item "mold"
  Set cell mold speed. If set, a dead cell will go from **death\_color** to
  **mold\_color** with a step of **mold**. **mold** can have a
  value from 0 to 255.
* **life\_color**  
  .IX Item "life_color"
  Set the color of living (or new born) cells.
* **death\_color**  
  .IX Item "death_color"
  Set the color of dead cells. If **mold** is set, this is the first color
  used to represent a dead cell.
* **mold\_color**  
  .IX Item "mold_color"
  Set mold color, for definitely dead and moldy cells.
  .Sp
  For the syntax of these 3 color options, check the Color\*(R" section in the
  ffmpeg-utils manual.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Read a grid from _pattern_, and center it on a grid of size
  300x300 pixels:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          life=f=pattern:s=300x300
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate a random grid of size 200x200, with a fill ratio of 2/3:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          life=ratio=2/3:s=200x200
  .Ve
* ·  
  Specify a custom rule for evolving a randomly generated grid:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          life=rule=S14/B34
  .Ve
* ·  
  Full example with slow death effect (mold) using **ffplay**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay -f lavfi life=s=300x200:mold=10:r=60:ratio=0.1:death_color=#C83232:life_color=#00ff00,scale=1200:800:flags=16
  .Ve

<a name="allrgb-allyuv-color-haldclutsrc-nullsrc-pal75bars-pal100bars-rgbtestsrc-smptebars-smptehdbars-testsrc-testsrc2-yuvtestsrc"></a>

### allrgb, allyuv, color, haldclutsrc, nullsrc, pal75bars, pal100bars, rgbtestsrc, smptebars, smptehdbars, testsrc, testsrc2, yuvtestsrc

.IX Subsection "allrgb, allyuv, color, haldclutsrc, nullsrc, pal75bars, pal100bars, rgbtestsrc, smptebars, smptehdbars, testsrc, testsrc2, yuvtestsrc"
The \f(CW`allrgb\*(C' source returns frames of size 4096x4096 of all rgb colors.

The \f(CW`allyuv\*(C' source returns frames of size 4096x4096 of all yuv colors.

The \f(CW`color\*(C' source provides an uniformly colored input.

The \f(CW`haldclutsrc\*(C' source provides an identity Hald \s-1CLUT.\s0 See also
**haldclut** filter.

The \f(CW`nullsrc\*(C' source returns unprocessed video frames. It is
mainly useful to be employed in analysis / debugging tools, or as the
source for filters which ignore the input data.

The \f(CW`pal75bars\*(C' source generates a color bars pattern, based on
\s-1EBU PAL\s0 recommendations with 75% color levels.

The \f(CW`pal100bars\*(C' source generates a color bars pattern, based on
\s-1EBU PAL\s0 recommendations with 100% color levels.

The \f(CW`rgbtestsrc\*(C' source generates an \s-1RGB\s0 test pattern useful for
detecting \s-1RGB\s0 vs \s-1BGR\s0 issues. You should see a red, green and blue
stripe from top to bottom.

The \f(CW`smptebars\*(C' source generates a color bars pattern, based on
the \s-1SMPTE\s0 Engineering Guideline \s-1EG 1-1990.\s0

The \f(CW`smptehdbars\*(C' source generates a color bars pattern, based on
the \s-1SMPTE RP 219-2002.\s0

The \f(CW`testsrc\*(C' source generates a test video pattern, showing a
color pattern, a scrolling gradient and a timestamp. This is mainly
intended for testing purposes.

The \f(CW`testsrc2\*(C' source is similar to testsrc, but supports more
pixel formats instead of just \f(CW`rgb24\*(C'. This allows using it as an
input for other tests without requiring a format conversion.

The \f(CW`yuvtestsrc\*(C' source generates an \s-1YUV\s0 test pattern. You should
see a y, cb and cr stripe from top to bottom.

The sources accept the following parameters:

* **level**  
  .IX Item "level"
  Specify the level of the Hald \s-1CLUT,\s0 only available in the \f(CW`haldclutsrc\*(C'
  source. A level of \f(CW`N\*(C' generates a picture of \f(CW\*(C\`N*N*N\*(C' by \f(CW\*(C\`N*N*N\*(C'
  pixels to be used as identity matrix for 3D lookup tables. Each component is
  coded on a \f(CW`1/(N*N)\*(C' scale.
* **color, c**  
  .IX Item "color, c"
  Specify the color of the source, only available in the \f(CW`color\*(C'
  source. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Color\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the size of the sourced video. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  The default value is \f(CW`320x240\*(C'.
  .Sp
  This option is not available with the \f(CW`allrgb\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`allyuv\*(C', and
  \f(CW`haldclutsrc\*(C' filters.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Specify the frame rate of the sourced video, as the number of frames
  generated per second. It has to be a string in the format
  _frame\_rate\_num_/_frame\_rate\_den_, an integer number, a floating point
  number or a valid video frame rate abbreviation. The default value is
  25\*(R".
* **duration, d**  
  .IX Item "duration, d"
  Set the duration of the sourced video. See
  **the Time duration section in the ffmpeg-utils\|(1) manual**
  for the accepted syntax.
  .Sp
  If not specified, or the expressed duration is negative, the video is
  supposed to be generated forever.
* **sar**  
  .IX Item "sar"
  Set the sample aspect ratio of the sourced video.
* **alpha**  
  .IX Item "alpha"
  Specify the alpha (opacity) of the background, only available in the
  \f(CW`testsrc2\*(C' source. The value must be between 0 (fully transparent) and
  255 (fully opaque, the default).
* **decimals, n**  
  .IX Item "decimals, n"
  Set the number of decimals to show in the timestamp, only available in the
  \f(CW`testsrc\*(C' source.
  .Sp
  The displayed timestamp value will correspond to the original
  timestamp value multiplied by the power of 10 of the specified
  value. Default value is 0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Generate a video with a duration of 5.3 seconds, with size
  176x144 and a frame rate of 10 frames per second:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          testsrc=duration=5.3:size=qcif:rate=10
  .Ve
* ·  
  The following graph description will generate a red source
  with an opacity of 0.2, with size qcif\*(R" and a frame rate of 10
  frames per second:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          color=c=red@0.2:s=qcif:r=10
  .Ve
* ·  
  If the input content is to be ignored, \f(CW`nullsrc\*(C' can be used. The
  following command generates noise in the luminance plane by employing
  the \f(CW`geq\*(C' filter:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          nullsrc=s=256x256, geq=random(1)*255:128:128
  .Ve

_Commands_
.IX Subsection "Commands"

The \f(CW`color\*(C' source supports the following commands:

* **c, color**  
  .IX Item "c, color"
  Set the color of the created image. Accepts the same syntax of the
  corresponding **color** option.

<a name="openclsrc"></a>

### openclsrc

.IX Subsection "openclsrc"
Generate video using an OpenCL program.

* **source**  
  .IX Item "source"
  OpenCL program source file.
* **kernel**  
  .IX Item "kernel"
  Kernel name in program.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Size of frames to generate.  This must be set.
* **format**  
  .IX Item "format"
  Pixel format to use for the generated frames.  This must be set.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Number of frames generated every second.  Default value is '25'.

For details of how the program loading works, see the **program\_opencl**
filter.

Example programs:

* ·  
  Generate a colour ramp by setting pixel values from the position of the pixel
  in the output image.  (Note that this will work with all pixel formats, but
  the generated output will not be the same.)
  .Sp
  .Vb 4
          _\|_kernel void ramp(_\|_write_only image2d_t dst,
                             unsigned int index)
          {
              int2 loc = (int2)(get_global_id(0), get_global_id(1));
          
              float4 val;
              val.xy = val.zw = convert_float2(loc) / convert_float2(get_image_dim(dst));
          
              write_imagef(dst, loc, val);
          }
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate a Sierpinski carpet pattern, panning by a single pixel each frame.
  .Sp
  .Vb 4
          _\|_kernel void sierpinski_carpet(_\|_write_only image2d_t dst,
                                          unsigned int index)
          {
              int2 loc = (int2)(get_global_id(0), get_global_id(1));
          
              float4 value = 0.0f;
              int x = loc.x + index;
              int y = loc.y + index;
              while (x &gt; 0 || y &gt; 0) {
                  if (x % 3 == 1 && y % 3 == 1) {
                      value = 1.0f;
                      break;
                  }
                  x /= 3;
                  y /= 3;
              }
          
              write_imagef(dst, loc, value);
          }
  .Ve

<a name="video-sinks"></a>

# Video Sinks

.IX Header "VIDEO SINKS"
Below is a description of the currently available video sinks.

<a name="buffersink"></a>

### buffersink

.IX Subsection "buffersink"
Buffer video frames, and make them available to the end of the filter
graph.

This sink is mainly intended for programmatic use, in particular
through the interface defined in _libavfilter/buffersink.h_
or the options system.

It accepts a pointer to an AVBufferSinkContext structure, which
defines the incoming buffers' formats, to be passed as the opaque
parameter to \f(CW`avfilter\_init\_filter\*(C' for initialization.

<a name="nullsink"></a>

### nullsink

.IX Subsection "nullsink"
Null video sink: do absolutely nothing with the input video. It is
mainly useful as a template and for use in analysis / debugging
tools.

<a name="multimedia-filters"></a>

# Multimedia Filters

.IX Header "MULTIMEDIA FILTERS"
Below is a description of the currently available multimedia filters.

<a name="abitscope"></a>

### abitscope

.IX Subsection "abitscope"
Convert input audio to a video output, displaying the audio bit scope.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set frame rate, expressed as number of frames per second. Default
  value is 25\*(R".
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`1024x256\*(C'.
* **colors**  
  .IX Item "colors"
  Specify list of colors separated by space or by '|' which will be used to
  draw channels. Unrecognized or missing colors will be replaced
  by white color.

<a name="ahistogram"></a>

### ahistogram

.IX Subsection "ahistogram"
Convert input audio to a video output, displaying the volume histogram.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **dmode**  
  .IX Item "dmode"
  Specify how histogram is calculated.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **single**  
      .IX Item "single"
      Use single histogram for all channels.
    * **separate**  
      .IX Item "separate"
      Use separate histogram for each channel.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`single\*(C'.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set frame rate, expressed as number of frames per second. Default
  value is 25\*(R".
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`hd720\*(C'.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Set display scale.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      logarithmic
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      square root
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      cubic root
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      linear
    * **rlog**  
      .IX Item "rlog"
      reverse logarithmic
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`log\*(C'.
* **ascale**  
  .IX Item "ascale"
  Set amplitude scale.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      logarithmic
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      linear
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`log\*(C'.
* **acount**  
  .IX Item "acount"
  Set how much frames to accumulate in histogram.
  Defauls is 1. Setting this to -1 accumulates all frames.
* **rheight**  
  .IX Item "rheight"
  Set histogram ratio of window height.
* **slide**  
  .IX Item "slide"
  Set sonogram sliding.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **replace**  
      .IX Item "replace"
      replace old rows with new ones.
    * **scroll**  
      .IX Item "scroll"
      scroll from top to bottom.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`replace\*(C'.

<a name="aphasemeter"></a>

### aphasemeter

.IX Subsection "aphasemeter"
Measures phase of input audio, which is exported as metadata \f(CW`lavfi.aphasemeter.phase\*(C',
representing mean phase of current audio frame. A video output can also be produced and is
enabled by default. The audio is passed through as first output.

Audio will be rematrixed to stereo if it has a different channel layout. Phase value is in
range \f(CW`[-1, 1]\*(C' where \f(CW\*(C\`-1\*(C' means left and right channels are completely out of phase
and \f(CW1 means channels are in phase.

The filter accepts the following options, all related to its video output:

* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set the output frame rate. Default value is \f(CW25.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`800x400\*(C'.
* **rc**  
  .IX Item "rc"
* **gc**  
  .IX Item "gc"
* **bc**  
  .IX Item "bc"
  Specify the red, green, blue contrast. Default values are \f(CW2,
  \f(CW7 and \f(CW1.
  Allowed range is \f(CW`[0, 255]\*(C'.
* **mpc**  
  .IX Item "mpc"
  Set color which will be used for drawing median phase. If color is
  \f(CW`none\*(C' which is default, no median phase value will be drawn.
* **video**  
  .IX Item "video"
  Enable video output. Default is enabled.

<a name="avectorscope"></a>

### avectorscope

.IX Subsection "avectorscope"
Convert input audio to a video output, representing the audio vector
scope.

The filter is used to measure the difference between channels of stereo
audio stream. A monoaural signal, consisting of identical left and right
signal, results in straight vertical line. Any stereo separation is visible
as a deviation from this line, creating a Lissajous figure.
If the straight (or deviation from it) but horizontal line appears this
indicates that the left and right channels are out of phase.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **mode, m**  
  .IX Item "mode, m"
  Set the vectorscope mode.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **lissajous**  
      .IX Item "lissajous"
      Lissajous rotated by 45 degrees.
    * **lissajous\_xy**  
      .IX Item "lissajous_xy"
      Same as above but not rotated.
    * **polar**  
      .IX Item "polar"
      Shape resembling half of circle.
      .Sp
      Default value is **lissajous**.
* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Set the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`400x400\*(C'.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set the output frame rate. Default value is \f(CW25.
* **rc**  
  .IX Item "rc"
* **gc**  
  .IX Item "gc"
* **bc**  
  .IX Item "bc"
* **ac**  
  .IX Item "ac"
  Specify the red, green, blue and alpha contrast. Default values are \f(CW40,
  \f(CW160, \f(CW80 and \f(CW255.
  Allowed range is \f(CW`[0, 255]\*(C'.
* **rf**  
  .IX Item "rf"
* **gf**  
  .IX Item "gf"
* **bf**  
  .IX Item "bf"
* **af**  
  .IX Item "af"
  Specify the red, green, blue and alpha fade. Default values are \f(CW15,
  \f(CW10, \f(CW5 and \f(CW5.
  Allowed range is \f(CW`[0, 255]\*(C'.
* **zoom**  
  .IX Item "zoom"
  Set the zoom factor. Default value is \f(CW1. Allowed range is \f(CW`[0, 10]\*(C'.
  Values lower than _1_ will auto adjust zoom factor to maximal possible value.
* **draw**  
  .IX Item "draw"
  Set the vectorscope drawing mode.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **dot**  
      .IX Item "dot"
      Draw dot for each sample.
    * **line**  
      .IX Item "line"
      Draw line between previous and current sample.
      .Sp
      Default value is **dot**.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Specify amplitude scale of audio samples.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      Linear.
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      Square root.
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      Cubic root.
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      Logarithmic.
* **swap**  
  .IX Item "swap"
  Swap left channel axis with right channel axis.
* **mirror**  
  .IX Item "mirror"
  Mirror axis.
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      No mirror.
    * **x**  
      .IX Item "x"
      Mirror only x axis.
    * **y**  
      .IX Item "y"
      Mirror only y axis.
    * **xy**  
      .IX Item "xy"
      Mirror both axis.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Complete example using **ffplay**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          ffplay -f lavfi amovie=input.mp3, asplit [a][out1];
                       [a] avectorscope=zoom=1.3:rc=2:gc=200:bc=10:rf=1:gf=8:bf=7 [out0]
  .Ve

<a name="bench-abench"></a>

### bench, abench

.IX Subsection "bench, abench"
Benchmark part of a filtergraph.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **action**  
  .IX Item "action"
  Start or stop a timer.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **start**  
      .IX Item "start"
      Get the current time, set it as frame metadata (using the key
      \f(CW`lavfi.bench.start\_time\*(C'), and forward the frame to the next filter.
    * **stop**  
      .IX Item "stop"
      Get the current time and fetch the \f(CW`lavfi.bench.start\_time\*(C' metadata from
      the input frame metadata to get the time difference. Time difference, average,
      maximum and minimum time (respectively \f(CW`t\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`avg\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`max\*(C' and
      \f(CW`min\*(C') are then printed. The timestamps are expressed in seconds.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Benchmark **selectivecolor** filter:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          bench=start,selectivecolor=reds=-.2 .12 -.49,bench=stop
  .Ve

<a name="concat"></a>

### concat

.IX Subsection "concat"
Concatenate audio and video streams, joining them together one after the
other.

The filter works on segments of synchronized video and audio streams. All
segments must have the same number of streams of each type, and that will
also be the number of streams at output.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  Set the number of segments. Default is 2.
* **v**  
  .IX Item "v"
  Set the number of output video streams, that is also the number of video
  streams in each segment. Default is 1.
* **a**  
  .IX Item "a"
  Set the number of output audio streams, that is also the number of audio
  streams in each segment. Default is 0.
* **unsafe**  
  .IX Item "unsafe"
  Activate unsafe mode: do not fail if segments have a different format.

The filter has _v_+_a_ outputs: first _v_ video outputs, then
_a_ audio outputs.

There are _n_x(_v_+_a_) inputs: first the inputs for the first
segment, in the same order as the outputs, then the inputs for the second
segment, etc.

Related streams do not always have exactly the same duration, for various
reasons including codec frame size or sloppy authoring. For that reason,
related synchronized streams (e.g. a video and its audio track) should be
concatenated at once. The concat filter will use the duration of the longest
stream in each segment (except the last one), and if necessary pad shorter
audio streams with silence.

For this filter to work correctly, all segments must start at timestamp 0.

All corresponding streams must have the same parameters in all segments; the
filtering system will automatically select a common pixel format for video
streams, and a common sample format, sample rate and channel layout for
audio streams, but other settings, such as resolution, must be converted
explicitly by the user.

Different frame rates are acceptable but will result in variable frame rate
at output; be sure to configure the output file to handle it.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Concatenate an opening, an episode and an ending, all in bilingual version
  (video in stream 0, audio in streams 1 and 2):
  .Sp
  .Vb 4
          ffmpeg -i opening.mkv -i episode.mkv -i ending.mkv -filter_complex \e
            [0:0] [0:1] [0:2] [1:0] [1:1] [1:2] [2:0] [2:1] [2:2]
             concat=n=3:v=1:a=2 [v] [a1] [a2] \e
            -map [v]\*(Aq -map \*(Aq[a1]\*(Aq -map \*(Aq[a2]\*(Aq output.mkv
  .Ve
* ·  
  Concatenate two parts, handling audio and video separately, using the
  (a)movie sources, and adjusting the resolution:
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          movie=part1.mp4, scale=512:288 [v1] ; amovie=part1.mp4 [a1] ;
          movie=part2.mp4, scale=512:288 [v2] ; amovie=part2.mp4 [a2] ;
          [v1] [v2] concat [outv] ; [a1] [a2] concat=v=0:a=1 [outa]
  .Ve
  .Sp
  Note that a desync will happen at the stitch if the audio and video streams
  do not have exactly the same duration in the first file.

_Commands_
.IX Subsection "Commands"

This filter supports the following commands:

* **next**  
  .IX Item "next"
  Close the current segment and step to the next one

<a name="drawgraph-adrawgraph"></a>

### drawgraph, adrawgraph

.IX Subsection "drawgraph, adrawgraph"
Draw a graph using input video or audio metadata.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **m1**  
  .IX Item "m1"
  Set 1st frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.
* **fg1**  
  .IX Item "fg1"
  Set 1st foreground color expression.
* **m2**  
  .IX Item "m2"
  Set 2nd frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.
* **fg2**  
  .IX Item "fg2"
  Set 2nd foreground color expression.
* **m3**  
  .IX Item "m3"
  Set 3rd frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.
* **fg3**  
  .IX Item "fg3"
  Set 3rd foreground color expression.
* **m4**  
  .IX Item "m4"
  Set 4th frame metadata key from which metadata values will be used to draw a graph.
* **fg4**  
  .IX Item "fg4"
  Set 4th foreground color expression.
* **min**  
  .IX Item "min"
  Set minimal value of metadata value.
* **max**  
  .IX Item "max"
  Set maximal value of metadata value.
* **bg**  
  .IX Item "bg"
  Set graph background color. Default is white.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set graph mode.
  .Sp
  Available values for mode is:
    * **bar**  
      .IX Item "bar"
    * **dot**  
      .IX Item "dot"
    * **line**  
      .IX Item "line"
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`line\*(C'.
* **slide**  
  .IX Item "slide"
  Set slide mode.
  .Sp
  Available values for slide is:
    * **frame**  
      .IX Item "frame"
      Draw new frame when right border is reached.
    * **replace**  
      .IX Item "replace"
      Replace old columns with new ones.
    * **scroll**  
      .IX Item "scroll"
      Scroll from right to left.
    * **rscroll**  
      .IX Item "rscroll"
      Scroll from left to right.
    * **picture**  
      .IX Item "picture"
      Draw single picture.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`frame\*(C'.
* **size**  
  .IX Item "size"
  Set size of graph video. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  The default value is \f(CW`900x256\*(C'.
  .Sp
  The foreground color expressions can use the following variables:
    * **\s-1MIN\s0**  
      .IX Item "MIN"
      Minimal value of metadata value.
    * **\s-1MAX\s0**  
      .IX Item "MAX"
      Maximal value of metadata value.
    * **\s-1VAL\s0**  
      .IX Item "VAL"
      Current metadata key value.
      .Sp
      The color is defined as 0xAABBGGRR.

Example using metadata from **signalstats** filter:

.Vb 1
        signalstats,drawgraph=lavfi.signalstats.YAVG:min=0:max=255
.Ve

Example using metadata from **ebur128** filter:

.Vb 1
        ebur128=metadata=1,adrawgraph=lavfi.r128.M:min=-120:max=5
.Ve

<a name="ebur128"></a>

### ebur128

.IX Subsection "ebur128"
\s-1EBU R128\s0 scanner filter. This filter takes an audio stream as input and outputs
it unchanged. By default, it logs a message at a frequency of 10Hz with the
Momentary loudness (identified by \f(CW`M\*(C'), Short-term loudness (\f(CW\*(C\`S\*(C'),
Integrated loudness (\f(CW`I\*(C') and Loudness Range (\f(CW\*(C\`LRA\*(C').

The filter also has a video output (see the _video_ option) with a real
time graph to observe the loudness evolution. The graphic contains the logged
message mentioned above, so it is not printed anymore when this option is set,
unless the verbose logging is set. The main graphing area contains the
short-term loudness (3 seconds of analysis), and the gauge on the right is for
the momentary loudness (400 milliseconds), but can optionally be configured
to instead display short-term loudness (see _gauge_).

The green area marks a  +/- 1LU target range around the target loudness
(-23LUFS by default, unless modified through _target_).

More information about the Loudness Recommendation \s-1EBU R128\s0 on
&lt;**http://tech.ebu.ch/loudness**&gt;.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **video**  
  .IX Item "video"
  Activate the video output. The audio stream is passed unchanged whether this
  option is set or no. The video stream will be the first output stream if
  activated. Default is \f(CW0.
* **size**  
  .IX Item "size"
  Set the video size. This option is for video only. For the syntax of this
  option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default and minimum resolution is \f(CW`640x480\*(C'.
* **meter**  
  .IX Item "meter"
  Set the \s-1EBU\s0 scale meter. Default is \f(CW9. Common values are \f(CW9 and
  \f(CW18, respectively for \s-1EBU\s0 scale meter +9 and \s-1EBU\s0 scale meter +18. Any
  other integer value between this range is allowed.
* **metadata**  
  .IX Item "metadata"
