  Set metadata injection. If set to \f(CW1, the audio input will be segmented
  into 100ms output frames, each of them containing various loudness information
  in metadata.  All the metadata keys are prefixed with \f(CW`lavfi.r128.\*(C'.
  .Sp
  Default is \f(CW0.
* **framelog**  
  .IX Item "framelog"
  Force the frame logging level.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **info**  
      .IX Item "info"
      information logging level
    * **verbose**  
      .IX Item "verbose"
      verbose logging level
      .Sp
      By default, the logging level is set to _info_. If the **video** or
      the **metadata** options are set, it switches to _verbose_.
* **peak**  
  .IX Item "peak"
  Set peak mode(s).
  .Sp
  Available modes can be cumulated (the option is a \f(CW`flag\*(C' type). Possible
  values are:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Disable any peak mode (default).
    * **sample**  
      .IX Item "sample"
      Enable sample-peak mode.
      .Sp
      Simple peak mode looking for the higher sample value. It logs a message
      for sample-peak (identified by \f(CW`SPK\*(C').
    * **true**  
      .IX Item "true"
      Enable true-peak mode.
      .Sp
      If enabled, the peak lookup is done on an over-sampled version of the input
      stream for better peak accuracy. It logs a message for true-peak.
      (identified by \f(CW`TPK\*(C') and true-peak per frame (identified by \f(CW\*(C\`FTPK\*(C').
      This mode requires a build with \f(CW`libswresample\*(C'.
* **dualmono**  
  .IX Item "dualmono"
  Treat mono input files as dual mono\*(R". If a mono file is intended for playback
  on a stereo system, its \s-1EBU R128\s0 measurement will be perceptually incorrect.
  If set to \f(CW`true\*(C', this option will compensate for this effect.
  Multi-channel input files are not affected by this option.
* **panlaw**  
  .IX Item "panlaw"
  Set a specific pan law to be used for the measurement of dual mono files.
  This parameter is optional, and has a default value of -3.01dB.
* **target**  
  .IX Item "target"
  Set a specific target level (in \s-1LUFS\s0) used as relative zero in the visualization.
  This parameter is optional and has a default value of -23LUFS as specified
  by \s-1EBU R128.\s0 However, material published online may prefer a level of -16LUFS
  (e.g. for use with podcasts or video platforms).
* **gauge**  
  .IX Item "gauge"
  Set the value displayed by the gauge. Valid values are \f(CW`momentary\*(C' and s
  \f(CW`shortterm\*(C'. By default the momentary value will be used, but in certain
  scenarios it may be more useful to observe the short term value instead (e.g.
  live mixing).
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Sets the display scale for the loudness. Valid parameters are \f(CW`absolute\*(C'
  (in \s-1LUFS\s0) or \f(CW`relative\*(C' (\s-1LU\s0) relative to the target. This only affects the
  video output, not the summary or continuous log output.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Real-time graph using **ffplay**, with a \s-1EBU\s0 scale meter +18:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay -f lavfi -i "amovie=input.mp3,ebur128=video=1:meter=18 [out0][out1]"
  .Ve
* ·  
  Run an analysis with **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -nostats -i input.mp3 -filter_complex ebur128 -f null -
  .Ve

<a name="interleave-ainterleave"></a>

### interleave, ainterleave

.IX Subsection "interleave, ainterleave"
Temporally interleave frames from several inputs.

\f(CW`interleave\*(C' works with video inputs, \f(CW\*(C\`ainterleave\*(C' with audio.

These filters read frames from several inputs and send the oldest
queued frame to the output.

Input streams must have well defined, monotonically increasing frame
timestamp values.

In order to submit one frame to output, these filters need to enqueue
at least one frame for each input, so they cannot work in case one
input is not yet terminated and will not receive incoming frames.

For example consider the case when one input is a \f(CW`select\*(C' filter
which always drops input frames. The \f(CW`interleave\*(C' filter will keep
reading from that input, but it will never be able to send new frames
to output until the input sends an end-of-stream signal.

Also, depending on inputs synchronization, the filters will drop
frames in case one input receives more frames than the other ones, and
the queue is already filled.

These filters accept the following options:

* **nb_inputs, n**  
  .IX Item "nb_inputs, n"
  Set the number of different inputs, it is 2 by default.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Interleave frames belonging to different streams using **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i bambi.avi -i pr0n.mkv -filter_complex "[0:v][1:v] interleave" out.avi
  .Ve
* ·  
  Add flickering blur effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=if(gt(random(0), 0.2), 1, 2)\*(Aq:n=2 [tmp], boxblur=2:2, [tmp] interleave
  .Ve

<a name="metadata-ametadata"></a>

### metadata, ametadata

.IX Subsection "metadata, ametadata"
Manipulate frame metadata.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set mode of operation of the filter.
  .Sp
  Can be one of the following:
    * **select**  
      .IX Item "select"
      If both \f(CW`value\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`key\*(C' is set, select frames
      which have such metadata. If only \f(CW`key\*(C' is set, select
      every frame that has such key in metadata.
    * **add**  
      .IX Item "add"
      Add new metadata \f(CW`key\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`value\*(C'. If key is already available
      do nothing.
    * **modify**  
      .IX Item "modify"
      Modify value of already present key.
    * **delete**  
      .IX Item "delete"
      If \f(CW`value\*(C' is set, delete only keys that have such value.
      Otherwise, delete key. If \f(CW`key\*(C' is not set, delete all metadata values in
      the frame.
    * **print**  
      .IX Item "print"
      Print key and its value if metadata was found. If \f(CW`key\*(C' is not set print all
      metadata values available in frame.
* **key**  
  .IX Item "key"
  Set key used with all modes. Must be set for all modes except \f(CW`print\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`delete\*(C'.
* **value**  
  .IX Item "value"
  Set metadata value which will be used. This option is mandatory for
  \f(CW`modify\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`add\*(C' mode.
* **function**  
  .IX Item "function"
  Which function to use when comparing metadata value and \f(CW`value\*(C'.
  .Sp
  Can be one of following:
    * **same\_str**  
      .IX Item "same_str"
      Values are interpreted as strings, returns true if metadata value is same as \f(CW`value\*(C'.
    * **starts\_with**  
      .IX Item "starts_with"
      Values are interpreted as strings, returns true if metadata value starts with
      the \f(CW`value\*(C' option string.
    * **less**  
      .IX Item "less"
      Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if metadata value is less than \f(CW`value\*(C'.
    * **equal**  
      .IX Item "equal"
      Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if \f(CW`value\*(C' is equal with metadata value.
    * **greater**  
      .IX Item "greater"
      Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if metadata value is greater than \f(CW`value\*(C'.
    * **expr**  
      .IX Item "expr"
      Values are interpreted as floats, returns true if expression from option \f(CW`expr\*(C'
      evaluates to true.
* **expr**  
  .IX Item "expr"
  Set expression which is used when \f(CW`function\*(C' is set to \f(CW\*(C\`expr\*(C'.
  The expression is evaluated through the eval \s-1API\s0 and can contain the following
  constants:
    * **\s-1VALUE1\s0**  
      .IX Item "VALUE1"
      Float representation of \f(CW`value\*(C' from metadata key.
    * **\s-1VALUE2\s0**  
      .IX Item "VALUE2"
      Float representation of \f(CW`value\*(C' as supplied by user in \f(CW\*(C\`value\*(C' option.
* **file**  
  .IX Item "file"
  If specified in \f(CW`print\*(C' mode, output is written to the named file. Instead of
  plain filename any writable url can be specified. Filename \`\`-'' is a shorthand
  for standard output. If \f(CW`file\*(C' option is not set, output is written to the log
  with \s-1AV_LOG_INFO\s0 loglevel.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Print all metadata values for frames with key \f(CW`lavfi.signalstats.YDIF\*(C' with values
  between 0 and 1.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          signalstats,metadata=print:key=lavfi.signalstats.YDIF:value=0:function=expr:expr=between(VALUE1,0,1)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Print silencedetect output to file _metadata.txt_.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          silencedetect,ametadata=mode=print:file=metadata.txt
  .Ve
* ·  
  Direct all metadata to a pipe with file descriptor 4.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          metadata=mode=print:file=pipe\e:4\*(Aq
  .Ve

<a name="perms-aperms"></a>

### perms, aperms

.IX Subsection "perms, aperms"
Set read/write permissions for the output frames.

These filters are mainly aimed at developers to test direct path in the
following filter in the filtergraph.

The filters accept the following options:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Select the permissions mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **none**  
      .IX Item "none"
      Do nothing. This is the default.
    * **ro**  
      .IX Item "ro"
      Set all the output frames read-only.
    * **rw**  
      .IX Item "rw"
      Set all the output frames directly writable.
    * **toggle**  
      .IX Item "toggle"
      Make the frame read-only if writable, and writable if read-only.
    * **random**  
      .IX Item "random"
      Set each output frame read-only or writable randomly.
* **seed**  
  .IX Item "seed"
  Set the seed for the _random_ mode, must be an integer included between
  \f(CW0 and \f(CW`UINT32\_MAX\*(C'. If not specified, or if explicitly set to
  \f(CW`-1\*(C', the filter will try to use a good random seed on a best effort
  basis.

Note: in case of auto-inserted filter between the permission filter and the
following one, the permission might not be received as expected in that
following filter. Inserting a **format** or **aformat** filter before the
perms/aperms filter can avoid this problem.

<a name="realtime-arealtime"></a>

### realtime, arealtime

.IX Subsection "realtime, arealtime"
Slow down filtering to match real time approximately.

These filters will pause the filtering for a variable amount of time to
match the output rate with the input timestamps.
They are similar to the **re** option to \f(CW`ffmpeg\*(C'.

They accept the following options:

* **limit**  
  .IX Item "limit"
  Time limit for the pauses. Any pause longer than that will be considered
  a timestamp discontinuity and reset the timer. Default is 2 seconds.

<a name="select-aselect"></a>

### select, aselect

.IX Subsection "select, aselect"
Select frames to pass in output.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **expr, e**  
  .IX Item "expr, e"
  Set expression, which is evaluated for each input frame.
  .Sp
  If the expression is evaluated to zero, the frame is discarded.
  .Sp
  If the evaluation result is negative or NaN, the frame is sent to the
  first output; otherwise it is sent to the output with index
  \f(CW`ceil(val)-1\*(C', assuming that the input index starts from 0.
  .Sp
  For example a value of \f(CW1.2 corresponds to the output with index
  \f(CW`ceil(1.2)-1 = 2-1 = 1\*(C', that is the second output.
* **outputs, n**  
  .IX Item "outputs, n"
  Set the number of outputs. The output to which to send the selected
  frame is based on the result of the evaluation. Default value is 1.

The expression can contain the following constants:

* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  The (sequential) number of the filtered frame, starting from 0.
* **selected\_n**  
  .IX Item "selected_n"
  The (sequential) number of the selected frame, starting from 0.
* **prev\_selected\_n**  
  .IX Item "prev_selected_n"
  The sequential number of the last selected frame. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **\s-1TB\s0**  
  .IX Item "TB"
  The timebase of the input timestamps.
* **pts**  
  .IX Item "pts"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 (Presentation TimeStamp) of the filtered video frame,
  expressed in _\s-1TB\s0_ units. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 of the filtered video frame,
  expressed in seconds. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **prev\_pts**  
  .IX Item "prev_pts"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 of the previously filtered video frame. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **prev\_selected\_pts**  
  .IX Item "prev_selected_pts"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 of the last previously filtered video frame. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **prev\_selected\_t**  
  .IX Item "prev_selected_t"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 of the last previously selected video frame, expressed in seconds. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **start\_pts**  
  .IX Item "start_pts"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 of the first video frame in the video. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **start\_t**  
  .IX Item "start_t"
  The time of the first video frame in the video. It's \s-1NAN\s0 if undefined.
* **pict\_type** _(video only)_  
  .IX Item "pict_type (video only)"
  The type of the filtered frame. It can assume one of the following
  values:
    * **I**  
      .IX Item "I"
    * **P**  
      .IX Item "P"
    * **B**  
      .IX Item "B"
    * **S**  
      .IX Item "S"
    * **\s-1SI\s0**  
      .IX Item "SI"
    * **\s-1SP\s0**  
      .IX Item "SP"
    * **\s-1BI\s0**  
      .IX Item "BI"
* **interlace\_type** _(video only)_  
  .IX Item "interlace_type (video only)"
  The frame interlace type. It can assume one of the following values:
    * **\s-1PROGRESSIVE\s0**  
      .IX Item "PROGRESSIVE"
      The frame is progressive (not interlaced).
    * **\s-1TOPFIRST\s0**  
      .IX Item "TOPFIRST"
      The frame is top-field-first.
    * **\s-1BOTTOMFIRST\s0**  
      .IX Item "BOTTOMFIRST"
      The frame is bottom-field-first.
* **consumed\_sample\_n** _(audio only)_  
  .IX Item "consumed_sample_n (audio only)"
  the number of selected samples before the current frame
* **samples\_n** _(audio only)_  
  .IX Item "samples_n (audio only)"
  the number of samples in the current frame
* **sample\_rate** _(audio only)_  
  .IX Item "sample_rate (audio only)"
  the input sample rate
* **key**  
  .IX Item "key"
  This is 1 if the filtered frame is a key-frame, 0 otherwise.
* **pos**  
  .IX Item "pos"
  the position in the file of the filtered frame, -1 if the information
  is not available (e.g. for synthetic video)
* **scene** _(video only)_  
  .IX Item "scene (video only)"
  value between 0 and 1 to indicate a new scene; a low value reflects a low
  probability for the current frame to introduce a new scene, while a higher
  value means the current frame is more likely to be one (see the example below)
* **concatdec\_select**  
  .IX Item "concatdec_select"
  The concat demuxer can select only part of a concat input file by setting an
  inpoint and an outpoint, but the output packets may not be entirely contained
  in the selected interval. By using this variable, it is possible to skip frames
  generated by the concat demuxer which are not exactly contained in the selected
  interval.
  .Sp
  This works by comparing the frame pts against the _lavf.concat.start\_time_
  and the _lavf.concat.duration_ packet metadata values which are also
  present in the decoded frames.
  .Sp
  The _concatdec\_select_ variable is -1 if the frame pts is at least
  start_time and either the duration metadata is missing or the frame pts is less
  than start_time + duration, 0 otherwise, and NaN if the start_time metadata is
  missing.
  .Sp
  That basically means that an input frame is selected if its pts is within the
  interval set by the concat demuxer.

The default value of the select expression is 1\*(R".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Select all frames in input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select
  .Ve
  .Sp
  The example above is the same as:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Skip all frames:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=0
  .Ve
* ·  
  Select only I-frames:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=eq(pict_type\e,I)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Select one frame every 100:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=not(mod(n\e,100))\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Select only frames contained in the 10-20 time interval:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=between(t\e,10\e,20)
  .Ve
* ·  
  Select only I-frames contained in the 10-20 time interval:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=between(t\e,10\e,20)*eq(pict_type\e,I)
  .Ve
* ·  
  Select frames with a minimum distance of 10 seconds:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=isnan(prev_selected_t)+gte(t-prev_selected_t\e,10)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Use aselect to select only audio frames with samples number &gt; 100:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          aselect=gt(samples_n\e,100)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Create a mosaic of the first scenes:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i video.avi -vf select=gt(scene\e,0.4)\*(Aq,scale=160:120,tile -frames:v 1 preview.png
  .Ve
  .Sp
  Comparing _scene_ against a value between 0.3 and 0.5 is generally a sane
  choice.
* ·  
  Send even and odd frames to separate outputs, and compose them:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          select=n=2:e=mod(n, 2)+1\*(Aq [odd][even]; [odd] pad=h=2*ih [tmp]; [tmp][even] overlay=y=h
  .Ve
* ·  
  Select useful frames from an ffconcat file which is using inpoints and
  outpoints but where the source files are not intra frame only.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -copyts -vsync 0 -segment_time_metadata 1 -i input.ffconcat -vf select=concatdec_select -af aselect=concatdec_select output.avi
  .Ve

<a name="sendcmd-asendcmd"></a>

### sendcmd, asendcmd

.IX Subsection "sendcmd, asendcmd"
Send commands to filters in the filtergraph.

These filters read commands to be sent to other filters in the
filtergraph.

\f(CW`sendcmd\*(C' must be inserted between two video filters,
\f(CW`asendcmd\*(C' must be inserted between two audio filters, but apart
from that they act the same way.

The specification of commands can be provided in the filter arguments
with the _commands_ option, or in a file specified by the
_filename_ option.

These filters accept the following options:

* **commands, c**  
  .IX Item "commands, c"
  Set the commands to be read and sent to the other filters.
* **filename, f**  
  .IX Item "filename, f"
  Set the filename of the commands to be read and sent to the other
  filters.

_Commands syntax_
.IX Subsection "Commands syntax"

A commands description consists of a sequence of interval
specifications, comprising a list of commands to be executed when a
particular event related to that interval occurs. The occurring event
is typically the current frame time entering or leaving a given time
interval.

An interval is specified by the following syntax:

.Vb 1
        &lt;START&gt;[-&lt;END&gt;] &lt;COMMANDS&gt;;
.Ve

The time interval is specified by the _\s-1START\s0_ and _\s-1END\s0_ times.
_\s-1END\s0_ is optional and defaults to the maximum time.

The current frame time is considered within the specified interval if
it is included in the interval [_\s-1START\s0_, _\s-1END\s0_), that is when
the time is greater or equal to _\s-1START\s0_ and is lesser than
_\s-1END\s0_.

_\s-1COMMANDS\s0_ consists of a sequence of one or more command
specifications, separated by ,\*(R", relating to that interval.  The
syntax of a command specification is given by:

.Vb 1
        [&lt;FLAGS&gt;] &lt;TARGET&gt; &lt;COMMAND&gt; &lt;ARG&gt;
.Ve

_\s-1FLAGS\s0_ is optional and specifies the type of events relating to
the time interval which enable sending the specified command, and must
be a non-null sequence of identifier flags separated by +\*(R" or \*(L"|\*(R" and
enclosed between [\*(R" and \*(L"]\*(R".

The following flags are recognized:

* **enter**  
  .IX Item "enter"
  The command is sent when the current frame timestamp enters the
  specified interval. In other words, the command is sent when the
  previous frame timestamp was not in the given interval, and the
  current is.
* **leave**  
  .IX Item "leave"
  The command is sent when the current frame timestamp leaves the
  specified interval. In other words, the command is sent when the
  previous frame timestamp was in the given interval, and the
  current is not.

If _\s-1FLAGS\s0_ is not specified, a default value of \f(CW`[enter]\*(C' is
assumed.

_\s-1TARGET\s0_ specifies the target of the command, usually the name of
the filter class or a specific filter instance name.

_\s-1COMMAND\s0_ specifies the name of the command for the target filter.

_\s-1ARG\s0_ is optional and specifies the optional list of argument for
the given _\s-1COMMAND\s0_.

Between one interval specification and another, whitespaces, or
sequences of characters starting with \f(CW`#\*(C' until the end of line,
are ignored and can be used to annotate comments.

A simplified \s-1BNF\s0 description of the commands specification syntax
follows:

.Vb 6
        &lt;COMMAND_FLAG&gt;  ::= "enter" | "leave"
        &lt;COMMAND_FLAGS&gt; ::= &lt;COMMAND_FLAG&gt; [(+|"|")&lt;COMMAND_FLAG&gt;]
        &lt;COMMAND&gt;       ::= ["[" &lt;COMMAND_FLAGS&gt; "]"] &lt;TARGET&gt; &lt;COMMAND&gt; [&lt;ARG&gt;]
        &lt;COMMANDS&gt;      ::= &lt;COMMAND&gt; [,&lt;COMMANDS&gt;]
        &lt;INTERVAL&gt;      ::= &lt;START&gt;[-&lt;END&gt;] &lt;COMMANDS&gt;
        &lt;INTERVALS&gt;     ::= &lt;INTERVAL&gt;[;&lt;INTERVALS&gt;]
.Ve

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Specify audio tempo change at second 4:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          asendcmd=c=4.0 atempo tempo 1.5\*(Aq,atempo
  .Ve
* ·  
  Target a specific filter instance:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          asendcmd=c=4.0 atempo@my tempo 1.5\*(Aq,atempo@my
  .Ve
* ·  
  Specify a list of drawtext and hue commands in a file.
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          # show text in the interval 5-10
          5.0-10.0 [enter] drawtext reinit fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=hello world\*(Aq,
                   [leave] drawtext reinit fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=\*(Aq;
          
          # desaturate the image in the interval 15-20
          15.0-20.0 [enter] hue s 0,
                    [enter] drawtext reinit fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=nocolor\*(Aq,
                    [leave] hue s 1,
                    [leave] drawtext reinit fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=color\*(Aq;
          
          # apply an exponential saturation fade-out effect, starting from time 25
          25 [enter] hue s exp(25-t)
  .Ve
  .Sp
  A filtergraph allowing to read and process the above command list
  stored in a file _test.cmd_, can be specified with:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          sendcmd=f=test.cmd,drawtext=fontfile=FreeSerif.ttf:text=\*(Aq,hue
  .Ve

<a name="setpts-asetpts"></a>

### setpts, asetpts

.IX Subsection "setpts, asetpts"
Change the \s-1PTS\s0 (presentation timestamp) of the input frames.

\f(CW`setpts\*(C' works on video frames, \f(CW\*(C\`asetpts\*(C' on audio frames.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **expr**  
  .IX Item "expr"
  The expression which is evaluated for each frame to construct its timestamp.

The expression is evaluated through the eval \s-1API\s0 and can contain the following
constants:

* **\s-1FRAME_RATE, FR\s0**  
  .IX Item "FRAME_RATE, FR"
  frame rate, only defined for constant frame-rate video
* **\s-1PTS\s0**  
  .IX Item "PTS"
  The presentation timestamp in input
* **N**  
  .IX Item "N"
  The count of the input frame for video or the number of consumed samples,
  not including the current frame for audio, starting from 0.
* **\s-1NB\_CONSUMED\_SAMPLES\s0**  
  .IX Item "NB_CONSUMED_SAMPLES"
  The number of consumed samples, not including the current frame (only
  audio)
* **\s-1NB_SAMPLES, S\s0**  
  .IX Item "NB_SAMPLES, S"
  The number of samples in the current frame (only audio)
* **\s-1SAMPLE_RATE, SR\s0**  
  .IX Item "SAMPLE_RATE, SR"
  The audio sample rate.
* **\s-1STARTPTS\s0**  
  .IX Item "STARTPTS"
  The \s-1PTS\s0 of the first frame.
* **\s-1STARTT\s0**  
  .IX Item "STARTT"
  the time in seconds of the first frame
* **\s-1INTERLACED\s0**  
  .IX Item "INTERLACED"
  State whether the current frame is interlaced.
* **T**  
  .IX Item "T"
  the time in seconds of the current frame
* **\s-1POS\s0**  
  .IX Item "POS"
  original position in the file of the frame, or undefined if undefined
  for the current frame
* **\s-1PREV\_INPTS\s0**  
  .IX Item "PREV_INPTS"
  The previous input \s-1PTS.\s0
* **\s-1PREV\_INT\s0**  
  .IX Item "PREV_INT"
  previous input time in seconds
* **\s-1PREV\_OUTPTS\s0**  
  .IX Item "PREV_OUTPTS"
  The previous output \s-1PTS.\s0
* **\s-1PREV\_OUTT\s0**  
  .IX Item "PREV_OUTT"
  previous output time in seconds
* **\s-1RTCTIME\s0**  
  .IX Item "RTCTIME"
  The wallclock (\s-1RTC\s0) time in microseconds. This is deprecated, use **time**\|(0)
  instead.
* **\s-1RTCSTART\s0**  
  .IX Item "RTCSTART"
  The wallclock (\s-1RTC\s0) time at the start of the movie in microseconds.
* **\s-1TB\s0**  
  .IX Item "TB"
  The timebase of the input timestamps.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Start counting \s-1PTS\s0 from zero
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=PTS-STARTPTS
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply fast motion effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=0.5*PTS
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply slow motion effect:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=2.0*PTS
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set fixed rate of 25 frames per second:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=N/(25*TB)
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set fixed rate 25 fps with some jitter:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=1/(25*TB) * (N + 0.05 * sin(N*2*PI/25))\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Apply an offset of 10 seconds to the input \s-1PTS:\s0
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=PTS+10/TB
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate timestamps from a live source\*(R" and rebase onto the current timebase:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          setpts=(RTCTIME - RTCSTART) / (TB * 1000000)\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Generate timestamps by counting samples:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          asetpts=N/SR/TB
  .Ve

<a name="setrange"></a>

### setrange

.IX Subsection "setrange"
Force color range for the output video frame.

The \f(CW`setrange\*(C' filter marks the color range property for the
output frames. It does not change the input frame, but only sets the
corresponding property, which affects how the frame is treated by
following filters.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **range**  
  .IX Item "range"
  Available values are:
    * **auto**  
      .IX Item "auto"
      Keep the same color range property.
    * **unspecified, unknown**  
      .IX Item "unspecified, unknown"
      Set the color range as unspecified.
    * **limited, tv, mpeg**  
      .IX Item "limited, tv, mpeg"
      Set the color range as limited.
    * **full, pc, jpeg**  
      .IX Item "full, pc, jpeg"
      Set the color range as full.

<a name="settb-asettb"></a>

### settb, asettb

.IX Subsection "settb, asettb"
Set the timebase to use for the output frames timestamps.
It is mainly useful for testing timebase configuration.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **expr, tb**  
  .IX Item "expr, tb"
  The expression which is evaluated into the output timebase.

The value for **tb** is an arithmetic expression representing a
rational. The expression can contain the constants \s-1AVTB\*(R"\s0 (the default
timebase), intb\*(R" (the input timebase) and \*(L"sr\*(R" (the sample rate,
audio only). Default value is intb\*(R".

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Set the timebase to 1/25:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          settb=expr=1/25
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set the timebase to 1/10:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          settb=expr=0.1
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set the timebase to 1001/1000:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          settb=1+0.001
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set the timebase to 2*intb:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          settb=2*intb
  .Ve
* ·  
  Set the default timebase value:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          settb=AVTB
  .Ve

<a name="showcqt"></a>

### showcqt

.IX Subsection "showcqt"
Convert input audio to a video output representing frequency spectrum
logarithmically using Brown-Puckette constant Q transform algorithm with
direct frequency domain coefficient calculation (but the transform itself
is not really constant Q, instead the Q factor is actually variable/clamped),
with musical tone scale, from E0 to D#10.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. It must be even. For the syntax of this option,
  check the **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`1920x1080\*(C'.
* **fps, rate, r**  
  .IX Item "fps, rate, r"
  Set the output frame rate. Default value is \f(CW25.
* **bar\_h**  
  .IX Item "bar_h"
  Set the bargraph height. It must be even. Default value is \f(CW`-1\*(C' which
  computes the bargraph height automatically.
* **axis\_h**  
  .IX Item "axis_h"
  Set the axis height. It must be even. Default value is \f(CW`-1\*(C' which computes
  the axis height automatically.
* **sono\_h**  
  .IX Item "sono_h"
  Set the sonogram height. It must be even. Default value is \f(CW`-1\*(C' which
  computes the sonogram height automatically.
* **fullhd**  
  .IX Item "fullhd"
  Set the fullhd resolution. This option is deprecated, use _size_, _s_
  instead. Default value is \f(CW1.
* **sono_v, volume**  
  .IX Item "sono_v, volume"
  Specify the sonogram volume expression. It can contain variables:
    * **bar\_v**  
      .IX Item "bar_v"
      the _bar\_v_ evaluated expression
    * **frequency, freq, f**  
      .IX Item "frequency, freq, f"
      the frequency where it is evaluated
    * **timeclamp, tc**  
      .IX Item "timeclamp, tc"
      the value of _timeclamp_ option
      .Sp
      and functions:
    * **a\_weighting(f)**  
      .IX Item "a_weighting(f)"
      A-weighting of equal loudness
    * **b\_weighting(f)**  
      .IX Item "b_weighting(f)"
      B-weighting of equal loudness
    * **c\_weighting(f)**  
      .IX Item "c_weighting(f)"
      C-weighting of equal loudness.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW16.
* **bar_v, volume2**  
  .IX Item "bar_v, volume2"
  Specify the bargraph volume expression. It can contain variables:
    * **sono\_v**  
      .IX Item "sono_v"
      the _sono\_v_ evaluated expression
    * **frequency, freq, f**  
      .IX Item "frequency, freq, f"
      the frequency where it is evaluated
    * **timeclamp, tc**  
      .IX Item "timeclamp, tc"
      the value of _timeclamp_ option
      .Sp
      and functions:
    * **a\_weighting(f)**  
      .IX Item "a_weighting(f)"
      A-weighting of equal loudness
    * **b\_weighting(f)**  
      .IX Item "b_weighting(f)"
      B-weighting of equal loudness
    * **c\_weighting(f)**  
      .IX Item "c_weighting(f)"
      C-weighting of equal loudness.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`sono\_v\*(C'.
* **sono_g, gamma**  
  .IX Item "sono_g, gamma"
  Specify the sonogram gamma. Lower gamma makes the spectrum more contrast,
  higher gamma makes the spectrum having more range. Default value is \f(CW3.
  Acceptable range is \f(CW`[1, 7]\*(C'.
* **bar_g, gamma2**  
  .IX Item "bar_g, gamma2"
  Specify the bargraph gamma. Default value is \f(CW1. Acceptable range is
  \f(CW`[1, 7]\*(C'.
* **bar\_t**  
  .IX Item "bar_t"
  Specify the bargraph transparency level. Lower value makes the bargraph sharper.
  Default value is \f(CW1. Acceptable range is \f(CW`[0, 1]\*(C'.
* **timeclamp, tc**  
  .IX Item "timeclamp, tc"
  Specify the transform timeclamp. At low frequency, there is trade-off between
  accuracy in time domain and frequency domain. If timeclamp is lower,
  event in time domain is represented more accurately (such as fast bass drum),
  otherwise event in frequency domain is represented more accurately
  (such as bass guitar). Acceptable range is \f(CW`[0.002, 1]\*(C'. Default value is \f(CW0.17.
* **attack**  
  .IX Item "attack"
  Set attack time in seconds. The default is \f(CW0 (disabled). Otherwise, it
  limits future samples by applying asymmetric windowing in time domain, useful
  when low latency is required. Accepted range is \f(CW`[0, 1]\*(C'.
* **basefreq**  
  .IX Item "basefreq"
  Specify the transform base frequency. Default value is \f(CW20.01523126408007475,
  which is frequency 50 cents below E0. Acceptable range is \f(CW`[10, 100000]\*(C'.
* **endfreq**  
  .IX Item "endfreq"
  Specify the transform end frequency. Default value is \f(CW20495.59681441799654,
  which is frequency 50 cents above D#10. Acceptable range is \f(CW`[10, 100000]\*(C'.
* **coeffclamp**  
  .IX Item "coeffclamp"
  This option is deprecated and ignored.
* **tlength**  
  .IX Item "tlength"
  Specify the transform length in time domain. Use this option to control accuracy
  trade-off between time domain and frequency domain at every frequency sample.
  It can contain variables:
    * **frequency, freq, f**  
      .IX Item "frequency, freq, f"
      the frequency where it is evaluated
    * **timeclamp, tc**  
      .IX Item "timeclamp, tc"
      the value of _timeclamp_ option.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`384*tc/(384+tc*f)\*(C'.
* **count**  
  .IX Item "count"
  Specify the transform count for every video frame. Default value is \f(CW6.
  Acceptable range is \f(CW`[1, 30]\*(C'.
* **fcount**  
  .IX Item "fcount"
  Specify the transform count for every single pixel. Default value is \f(CW0,
  which makes it computed automatically. Acceptable range is \f(CW`[0, 10]\*(C'.
* **fontfile**  
  .IX Item "fontfile"
  Specify font file for use with freetype to draw the axis. If not specified,
  use embedded font. Note that drawing with font file or embedded font is not
  implemented with custom _basefreq_ and _endfreq_, use _axisfile_
  option instead.
* **font**  
  .IX Item "font"
  Specify fontconfig pattern. This has lower priority than _fontfile_.
  The : in the pattern may be replaced by | to avoid unnecessary escaping.
* **fontcolor**  
  .IX Item "fontcolor"
  Specify font color expression. This is arithmetic expression that should return
  integer value 0xRRGGBB. It can contain variables:
    * **frequency, freq, f**  
      .IX Item "frequency, freq, f"
      the frequency where it is evaluated
    * **timeclamp, tc**  
      .IX Item "timeclamp, tc"
      the value of _timeclamp_ option
      .Sp
      and functions:
    * **midi(f)**  
      .IX Item "midi(f)"
      midi number of frequency f, some midi numbers: E0(16), C1(24), C2(36), A4(69)
    * **r(x), g(x), b(x)**  
      .IX Item "r(x), g(x), b(x)"
      red, green, and blue value of intensity x.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`st(0, (midi(f)-59.5)/12);
      st(1, if(between(ld(0),0,1), 0.5-0.5*cos(2*PI*ld(0)), 0));
      r(1-ld(1)) + b(ld(1)).
* **axisfile**  
  .IX Item "axisfile"
  Specify image file to draw the axis. This option override _fontfile_ and
  _fontcolor_ option.
* **axis, text**  
  .IX Item "axis, text"
  Enable/disable drawing text to the axis. If it is set to \f(CW0, drawing to
  the axis is disabled, ignoring _fontfile_ and _axisfile_ option.
  Default value is \f(CW1.
* **csp**  
  .IX Item "csp"
  Set colorspace. The accepted values are:
    * **unspecified**  
      .IX Item "unspecified"
      Unspecified (default)
    * **bt709**  
      .IX Item "bt709"
      \s-1BT.709\s0
    * **fcc**  
      .IX Item "fcc"
      \s-1FCC\s0
    * **bt470bg**  
      .IX Item "bt470bg"
      \s-1BT.470BG\s0 or \s-1BT.601-6 625\s0
    * **smpte170m**  
      .IX Item "smpte170m"
      \s-1SMPTE-170M\s0 or \s-1BT.601-6 525\s0
    * **smpte240m**  
      .IX Item "smpte240m"
      \s-1SMPTE-240M\s0
    * **bt2020ncl**  
      .IX Item "bt2020ncl"
      \s-1BT.2020\s0 with non-constant luminance
* **cscheme**  
  .IX Item "cscheme"
  Set spectrogram color scheme. This is list of floating point values with format
  \f(CW`left\_r|left\_g|left\_b|right\_r|right\_g|right\_b\*(C'.
  The default is \f(CW`1|0.5|0|0|0.5|1\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Playing audio while showing the spectrum:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay -f lavfi amovie=a.mp3, asplit [a][out1]; [a] showcqt [out0]\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Same as above, but with frame rate 30 fps:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay -f lavfi amovie=a.mp3, asplit [a][out1]; [a] showcqt=fps=30:count=5 [out0]\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Playing at 1280x720:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffplay -f lavfi amovie=a.mp3, asplit [a][out1]; [a] showcqt=s=1280x720:count=4 [out0]\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Disable sonogram display:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          sono_h=0
  .Ve
* ·  
  A1 and its harmonics: A1, A2, (near)E3, A3:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          ffplay -f lavfi aevalsrc=0.1*sin(2*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(4*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(6*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(8*PI*55*t),
                           asplit[a][out1]; [a] showcqt [out0]
  .Ve
* ·  
  Same as above, but with more accuracy in frequency domain:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          ffplay -f lavfi aevalsrc=0.1*sin(2*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(4*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(6*PI*55*t)+0.1*sin(8*PI*55*t),
                           asplit[a][out1]; [a] showcqt=timeclamp=0.5 [out0]
  .Ve
* ·  
  Custom volume:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          bar_v=10:sono_v=bar_v*a_weighting(f)
  .Ve
* ·  
  Custom gamma, now spectrum is linear to the amplitude.
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          bar_g=2:sono_g=2
  .Ve
* ·  
  Custom tlength equation:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          tc=0.33:tlength=st(0,0.17); 384*tc / (384 / ld(0) + tc*f /(1-ld(0))) + 384*tc / (tc*f / ld(0) + 384 /(1-ld(0)))\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Custom fontcolor and fontfile, C-note is colored green, others are colored blue:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          fontcolor=if(mod(floor(midi(f)+0.5),12), 0x0000FF, g(1))\*(Aq:fontfile=myfont.ttf
  .Ve
* ·  
  Custom font using fontconfig:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          font=Courier New,Monospace,mono|bold\*(Aq
  .Ve
* ·  
  Custom frequency range with custom axis using image file:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          axisfile=myaxis.png:basefreq=40:endfreq=10000
  .Ve

<a name="showfreqs"></a>

### showfreqs

.IX Subsection "showfreqs"
Convert input audio to video output representing the audio power spectrum.
Audio amplitude is on Y-axis while frequency is on X-axis.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify size of video. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default is \f(CW`1024x512\*(C'.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set display mode.
  This set how each frequency bin will be represented.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **line**  
      .IX Item "line"
    * **bar**  
      .IX Item "bar"
    * **dot**  
      .IX Item "dot"
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`bar\*(C'.
* **ascale**  
  .IX Item "ascale"
  Set amplitude scale.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      Linear scale.
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      Square root scale.
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      Cubic root scale.
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      Logarithmic scale.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`log\*(C'.
* **fscale**  
  .IX Item "fscale"
  Set frequency scale.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      Linear scale.
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      Logarithmic scale.
    * **rlog**  
      .IX Item "rlog"
      Reverse logarithmic scale.
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`lin\*(C'.
* **win\_size**  
  .IX Item "win_size"
  Set window size.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **w16**  
      .IX Item "w16"
    * **w32**  
      .IX Item "w32"
    * **w64**  
      .IX Item "w64"
    * **w128**  
      .IX Item "w128"
    * **w256**  
      .IX Item "w256"
    * **w512**  
      .IX Item "w512"
    * **w1024**  
      .IX Item "w1024"
    * **w2048**  
      .IX Item "w2048"
    * **w4096**  
      .IX Item "w4096"
    * **w8192**  
      .IX Item "w8192"
    * **w16384**  
      .IX Item "w16384"
    * **w32768**  
      .IX Item "w32768"
    * **w65536**  
      .IX Item "w65536"
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`w2048\*(C'
* **win\_func**  
  .IX Item "win_func"
  Set windowing function.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **rect**  
      .IX Item "rect"
    * **bartlett**  
      .IX Item "bartlett"
    * **hanning**  
      .IX Item "hanning"
    * **hamming**  
      .IX Item "hamming"
    * **blackman**  
      .IX Item "blackman"
    * **welch**  
      .IX Item "welch"
    * **flattop**  
      .IX Item "flattop"
    * **bharris**  
      .IX Item "bharris"
    * **bnuttall**  
      .IX Item "bnuttall"
    * **bhann**  
      .IX Item "bhann"
    * **sine**  
      .IX Item "sine"
    * **nuttall**  
      .IX Item "nuttall"
    * **lanczos**  
      .IX Item "lanczos"
    * **gauss**  
      .IX Item "gauss"
    * **tukey**  
      .IX Item "tukey"
    * **dolph**  
      .IX Item "dolph"
    * **cauchy**  
      .IX Item "cauchy"
    * **parzen**  
      .IX Item "parzen"
    * **poisson**  
      .IX Item "poisson"
    * **bohman**  
      .IX Item "bohman"
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`hanning\*(C'.
* **overlap**  
  .IX Item "overlap"
  Set window overlap. In range \f(CW`[0, 1]\*(C'. Default is \f(CW1,
  which means optimal overlap for selected window function will be picked.
* **averaging**  
  .IX Item "averaging"
  Set time averaging. Setting this to 0 will display current maximal peaks.
  Default is \f(CW1, which means time averaging is disabled.
* **colors**  
  .IX Item "colors"
  Specify list of colors separated by space or by '|' which will be used to
  draw channel frequencies. Unrecognized or missing colors will be replaced
  by white color.
* **cmode**  
  .IX Item "cmode"
  Set channel display mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **combined**  
      .IX Item "combined"
    * **separate**  
      .IX Item "separate"
      .Sp
      Default is \f(CW`combined\*(C'.
* **minamp**  
  .IX Item "minamp"
  Set minimum amplitude used in \f(CW`log\*(C' amplitude scaler.

<a name="showspectrum"></a>

### showspectrum

.IX Subsection "showspectrum"
Convert input audio to a video output, representing the audio frequency
spectrum.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`640x512\*(C'.
* **slide**  
  .IX Item "slide"
  Specify how the spectrum should slide along the window.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **replace**  
      .IX Item "replace"
      the samples start again on the left when they reach the right
    * **scroll**  
      .IX Item "scroll"
      the samples scroll from right to left
    * **fullframe**  
      .IX Item "fullframe"
      frames are only produced when the samples reach the right
    * **rscroll**  
      .IX Item "rscroll"
      the samples scroll from left to right
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`replace\*(C'.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Specify display mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **combined**  
      .IX Item "combined"
      all channels are displayed in the same row
    * **separate**  
      .IX Item "separate"
      all channels are displayed in separate rows
      .Sp
      Default value is **combined**.
* **color**  
  .IX Item "color"
  Specify display color mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **channel**  
      .IX Item "channel"
      each channel is displayed in a separate color
    * **intensity**  
      .IX Item "intensity"
      each channel is displayed using the same color scheme
    * **rainbow**  
      .IX Item "rainbow"
      each channel is displayed using the rainbow color scheme
    * **moreland**  
      .IX Item "moreland"
      each channel is displayed using the moreland color scheme
    * **nebulae**  
      .IX Item "nebulae"
      each channel is displayed using the nebulae color scheme
    * **fire**  
      .IX Item "fire"
      each channel is displayed using the fire color scheme
    * **fiery**  
      .IX Item "fiery"
      each channel is displayed using the fiery color scheme
    * **fruit**  
      .IX Item "fruit"
      each channel is displayed using the fruit color scheme
    * **cool**  
      .IX Item "cool"
      each channel is displayed using the cool color scheme
    * **magma**  
      .IX Item "magma"
      each channel is displayed using the magma color scheme
    * **green**  
      .IX Item "green"
      each channel is displayed using the green color scheme
      .Sp
      Default value is **channel**.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Specify scale used for calculating intensity color values.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      linear
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      square root, default
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      cubic root
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      logarithmic
    * **4thrt**  
      .IX Item "4thrt"
      4th root
    * **5thrt**  
      .IX Item "5thrt"
      5th root
      .Sp
      Default value is **sqrt**.
* **saturation**  
  .IX Item "saturation"
  Set saturation modifier for displayed colors. Negative values provide
  alternative color scheme. \f(CW0 is no saturation at all.
  Saturation must be in [-10.0, 10.0] range.
  Default value is \f(CW1.
* **win\_func**  
  .IX Item "win_func"
  Set window function.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **rect**  
      .IX Item "rect"
    * **bartlett**  
      .IX Item "bartlett"
    * **hann**  
      .IX Item "hann"
    * **hanning**  
      .IX Item "hanning"
    * **hamming**  
      .IX Item "hamming"
    * **blackman**  
      .IX Item "blackman"
    * **welch**  
      .IX Item "welch"
    * **flattop**  
      .IX Item "flattop"
    * **bharris**  
      .IX Item "bharris"
    * **bnuttall**  
      .IX Item "bnuttall"
    * **bhann**  
      .IX Item "bhann"
    * **sine**  
      .IX Item "sine"
    * **nuttall**  
      .IX Item "nuttall"
    * **lanczos**  
      .IX Item "lanczos"
    * **gauss**  
      .IX Item "gauss"
    * **tukey**  
      .IX Item "tukey"
    * **dolph**  
      .IX Item "dolph"
    * **cauchy**  
      .IX Item "cauchy"
    * **parzen**  
      .IX Item "parzen"
    * **poisson**  
      .IX Item "poisson"
    * **bohman**  
      .IX Item "bohman"
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`hann\*(C'.
* **orientation**  
  .IX Item "orientation"
  Set orientation of time vs frequency axis. Can be \f(CW`vertical\*(C' or
  \f(CW`horizontal\*(C'. Default is \f(CW\*(C\`vertical\*(C'.
* **overlap**  
  .IX Item "overlap"
  Set ratio of overlap window. Default value is \f(CW0.
  When value is \f(CW1 overlap is set to recommended size for specific
  window function currently used.
* **gain**  
  .IX Item "gain"
  Set scale gain for calculating intensity color values.
  Default value is \f(CW1.
* **data**  
  .IX Item "data"
  Set which data to display. Can be \f(CW`magnitude\*(C', default or \f(CW\*(C\`phase\*(C'.
* **rotation**  
  .IX Item "rotation"
  Set color rotation, must be in [-1.0, 1.0] range.
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **start**  
  .IX Item "start"
  Set start frequency from which to display spectrogram. Default is \f(CW0.
* **stop**  
  .IX Item "stop"
  Set stop frequency to which to display spectrogram. Default is \f(CW0.
* **fps**  
  .IX Item "fps"
  Set upper frame rate limit. Default is \f(CW`auto\*(C', unlimited.
* **legend**  
  .IX Item "legend"
  Draw time and frequency axes and legends. Default is disabled.

The usage is very similar to the showwaves filter; see the examples in that
section.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Large window with logarithmic color scaling:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          showspectrum=s=1280x480:scale=log
  .Ve
* ·  
  Complete example for a colored and sliding spectrum per channel using **ffplay**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 2
          ffplay -f lavfi amovie=input.mp3, asplit [a][out1];
                       [a] showspectrum=mode=separate:color=intensity:slide=1:scale=cbrt [out0]
  .Ve

<a name="showspectrumpic"></a>

### showspectrumpic

.IX Subsection "showspectrumpic"
Convert input audio to a single video frame, representing the audio frequency
spectrum.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`4096x2048\*(C'.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Specify display mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **combined**  
      .IX Item "combined"
      all channels are displayed in the same row
    * **separate**  
      .IX Item "separate"
      all channels are displayed in separate rows
      .Sp
      Default value is **combined**.
* **color**  
  .IX Item "color"
  Specify display color mode.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **channel**  
      .IX Item "channel"
      each channel is displayed in a separate color
    * **intensity**  
      .IX Item "intensity"
      each channel is displayed using the same color scheme
    * **rainbow**  
      .IX Item "rainbow"
      each channel is displayed using the rainbow color scheme
    * **moreland**  
      .IX Item "moreland"
      each channel is displayed using the moreland color scheme
    * **nebulae**  
      .IX Item "nebulae"
      each channel is displayed using the nebulae color scheme
    * **fire**  
      .IX Item "fire"
      each channel is displayed using the fire color scheme
    * **fiery**  
      .IX Item "fiery"
      each channel is displayed using the fiery color scheme
    * **fruit**  
      .IX Item "fruit"
      each channel is displayed using the fruit color scheme
    * **cool**  
      .IX Item "cool"
      each channel is displayed using the cool color scheme
    * **magma**  
      .IX Item "magma"
      each channel is displayed using the magma color scheme
    * **green**  
      .IX Item "green"
      each channel is displayed using the green color scheme
      .Sp
      Default value is **intensity**.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Specify scale used for calculating intensity color values.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      linear
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      square root, default
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      cubic root
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      logarithmic
    * **4thrt**  
      .IX Item "4thrt"
      4th root
    * **5thrt**  
      .IX Item "5thrt"
      5th root
      .Sp
      Default value is **log**.
* **saturation**  
  .IX Item "saturation"
  Set saturation modifier for displayed colors. Negative values provide
  alternative color scheme. \f(CW0 is no saturation at all.
  Saturation must be in [-10.0, 10.0] range.
  Default value is \f(CW1.
* **win\_func**  
  .IX Item "win_func"
  Set window function.
  .Sp
  It accepts the following values:
    * **rect**  
      .IX Item "rect"
    * **bartlett**  
      .IX Item "bartlett"
    * **hann**  
      .IX Item "hann"
    * **hanning**  
      .IX Item "hanning"
    * **hamming**  
      .IX Item "hamming"
    * **blackman**  
      .IX Item "blackman"
    * **welch**  
      .IX Item "welch"
    * **flattop**  
      .IX Item "flattop"
    * **bharris**  
      .IX Item "bharris"
    * **bnuttall**  
      .IX Item "bnuttall"
    * **bhann**  
      .IX Item "bhann"
    * **sine**  
      .IX Item "sine"
    * **nuttall**  
      .IX Item "nuttall"
    * **lanczos**  
      .IX Item "lanczos"
    * **gauss**  
      .IX Item "gauss"
    * **tukey**  
      .IX Item "tukey"
    * **dolph**  
      .IX Item "dolph"
    * **cauchy**  
      .IX Item "cauchy"
    * **parzen**  
      .IX Item "parzen"
    * **poisson**  
      .IX Item "poisson"
    * **bohman**  
      .IX Item "bohman"
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`hann\*(C'.
* **orientation**  
  .IX Item "orientation"
  Set orientation of time vs frequency axis. Can be \f(CW`vertical\*(C' or
  \f(CW`horizontal\*(C'. Default is \f(CW\*(C\`vertical\*(C'.
* **gain**  
  .IX Item "gain"
  Set scale gain for calculating intensity color values.
  Default value is \f(CW1.
* **legend**  
  .IX Item "legend"
  Draw time and frequency axes and legends. Default is enabled.
* **rotation**  
  .IX Item "rotation"
  Set color rotation, must be in [-1.0, 1.0] range.
  Default value is \f(CW0.
* **start**  
  .IX Item "start"
  Set start frequency from which to display spectrogram. Default is \f(CW0.
* **stop**  
  .IX Item "stop"
  Set stop frequency to which to display spectrogram. Default is \f(CW0.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Extract an audio spectrogram of a whole audio track
  in a 1024x1024 picture using **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i audio.flac -lavfi showspectrumpic=s=1024x1024 spectrogram.png
  .Ve

<a name="showvolume"></a>

### showvolume

.IX Subsection "showvolume"
Convert input audio volume to a video output.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set video rate.
* **b**  
  .IX Item "b"
  Set border width, allowed range is [0, 5]. Default is 1.
* **w**  
  .IX Item "w"
  Set channel width, allowed range is [80, 8192]. Default is 400.
* **h**  
  .IX Item "h"
  Set channel height, allowed range is [1, 900]. Default is 20.
* **f**  
  .IX Item "f"
  Set fade, allowed range is [0, 1]. Default is 0.95.
* **c**  
  .IX Item "c"
  Set volume color expression.
  .Sp
  The expression can use the following variables:
    * **\s-1VOLUME\s0**  
      .IX Item "VOLUME"
      Current max volume of channel in dB.
    * **\s-1PEAK\s0**  
      .IX Item "PEAK"
      Current peak.
    * **\s-1CHANNEL\s0**  
      .IX Item "CHANNEL"
      Current channel number, starting from 0.
* **t**  
  .IX Item "t"
  If set, displays channel names. Default is enabled.
* **v**  
  .IX Item "v"
  If set, displays volume values. Default is enabled.
* **o**  
  .IX Item "o"
  Set orientation, can be horizontal: \f(CW`h\*(C' or vertical: \f(CW\*(C\`v\*(C',
  default is \f(CW`h\*(C'.
* **s**  
  .IX Item "s"
  Set step size, allowed range is [0, 5]. Default is 0, which means
  step is disabled.
* **p**  
  .IX Item "p"
  Set background opacity, allowed range is [0, 1]. Default is 0.
* **m**  
  .IX Item "m"
  Set metering mode, can be peak: \f(CW`p\*(C' or rms: \f(CW\*(C\`r\*(C',
  default is \f(CW`p\*(C'.
* **ds**  
  .IX Item "ds"
  Set display scale, can be linear: \f(CW`lin\*(C' or log: \f(CW\*(C\`log\*(C',
  default is \f(CW`lin\*(C'.
* **dm**  
  .IX Item "dm"
  In second.
  If set to &gt; 0., display a line for the max level
  in the previous seconds.
  default is disabled: \f(CW0.
* **dmc**  
  .IX Item "dmc"
  The color of the max line. Use when \f(CW`dm\*(C' option is set to &gt; 0.
  default is: \f(CW`orange\*(C'

<a name="showwaves"></a>

### showwaves

.IX Subsection "showwaves"
Convert input audio to a video output, representing the samples waves.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`600x240\*(C'.
* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set display mode.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **point**  
      .IX Item "point"
      Draw a point for each sample.
    * **line**  
      .IX Item "line"
      Draw a vertical line for each sample.
    * **p2p**  
      .IX Item "p2p"
      Draw a point for each sample and a line between them.
    * **cline**  
      .IX Item "cline"
      Draw a centered vertical line for each sample.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`point\*(C'.
* **n**  
  .IX Item "n"
  Set the number of samples which are printed on the same column. A
  larger value will decrease the frame rate. Must be a positive
  integer. This option can be set only if the value for _rate_
  is not explicitly specified.
* **rate, r**  
  .IX Item "rate, r"
  Set the (approximate) output frame rate. This is done by setting the
  option _n_. Default value is 25\*(R".
* **split\_channels**  
  .IX Item "split_channels"
  Set if channels should be drawn separately or overlap. Default value is 0.
* **colors**  
  .IX Item "colors"
  Set colors separated by '|' which are going to be used for drawing of each channel.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Set amplitude scale.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      Linear.
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      Logarithmic.
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      Square root.
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      Cubic root.
      .Sp
      Default is linear.
* **draw**  
  .IX Item "draw"
  Set the draw mode. This is mostly useful to set for high _n_.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **scale**  
      .IX Item "scale"
      Scale pixel values for each drawn sample.
    * **full**  
      .IX Item "full"
      Draw every sample directly.
      .Sp
      Default value is \f(CW`scale\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Output the input file audio and the corresponding video representation
  at the same time:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          amovie=a.mp3,asplit[out0],showwaves[out1]
  .Ve
* ·  
  Create a synthetic signal and show it with showwaves, forcing a
  frame rate of 30 frames per second:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          aevalsrc=sin(1*2*PI*t)*sin(880*2*PI*t):cos(2*PI*200*t),asplit[out0],showwaves=r=30[out1]
  .Ve

<a name="showwavespic"></a>

### showwavespic

.IX Subsection "showwavespic"
Convert input audio to a single video frame, representing the samples waves.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **size, s**  
  .IX Item "size, s"
  Specify the video size for the output. For the syntax of this option, check the
  **Video size\*(R" section in the ffmpeg-utils manual**.
  Default value is \f(CW`600x240\*(C'.
* **split\_channels**  
  .IX Item "split_channels"
  Set if channels should be drawn separately or overlap. Default value is 0.
* **colors**  
  .IX Item "colors"
  Set colors separated by '|' which are going to be used for drawing of each channel.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Set amplitude scale.
  .Sp
  Available values are:
    * **lin**  
      .IX Item "lin"
      Linear.
    * **log**  
      .IX Item "log"
      Logarithmic.
    * **sqrt**  
      .IX Item "sqrt"
      Square root.
    * **cbrt**  
      .IX Item "cbrt"
      Cubic root.
      .Sp
      Default is linear.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Extract a channel split representation of the wave form of a whole audio track
  in a 1024x800 picture using **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i audio.flac -lavfi showwavespic=split_channels=1:s=1024x800 waveform.png
  .Ve

<a name="sidedata-asidedata"></a>

### sidedata, asidedata

.IX Subsection "sidedata, asidedata"
Delete frame side data, or select frames based on it.

This filter accepts the following options:

* **mode**  
  .IX Item "mode"
  Set mode of operation of the filter.
  .Sp
  Can be one of the following:
    * **select**  
      .IX Item "select"
      Select every frame with side data of \f(CW`type\*(C'.
    * **delete**  
      .IX Item "delete"
      Delete side data of \f(CW`type\*(C'. If \f(CW\*(C\`type\*(C' is not set, delete all side
      data in the frame.
* **type**  
  .IX Item "type"
  Set side data type used with all modes. Must be set for \f(CW`select\*(C' mode. For
  the list of frame side data types, refer to the \f(CW`AVFrameSideDataType\*(C' enum
  in _libavutil/frame.h_. For example, to choose
  \f(CW`AV\_FRAME\_DATA\_PANSCAN\*(C' side data, you must specify \f(CW\*(C\`PANSCAN\*(C'.

<a name="spectrumsynth"></a>

### spectrumsynth

.IX Subsection "spectrumsynth"
Sythesize audio from 2 input video spectrums, first input stream represents
magnitude across time and second represents phase across time.
The filter will transform from frequency domain as displayed in videos back
to time domain as presented in audio output.

This filter is primarily created for reversing processed **showspectrum**
filter outputs, but can synthesize sound from other spectrograms too.
But in such case results are going to be poor if the phase data is not
available, because in such cases phase data need to be recreated, usually
its just recreated from random noise.
For best results use gray only output (\f(CW`channel\*(C' color mode in
**showspectrum** filter) and \f(CW`log\*(C' scale for magnitude video and
\f(CW`lin\*(C' scale for phase video. To produce phase, for 2nd video, use
\f(CW`data\*(C' option. Inputs videos should generally use \f(CW\*(C\`fullframe\*(C'
slide mode as that saves resources needed for decoding video.

The filter accepts the following options:

* **sample\_rate**  
  .IX Item "sample_rate"
  Specify sample rate of output audio, the sample rate of audio from which
  spectrum was generated may differ.
* **channels**  
  .IX Item "channels"
  Set number of channels represented in input video spectrums.
* **scale**  
  .IX Item "scale"
  Set scale which was used when generating magnitude input spectrum.
  Can be \f(CW`lin\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`log\*(C'. Default is \f(CW\*(C\`log\*(C'.
* **slide**  
  .IX Item "slide"
  Set slide which was used when generating inputs spectrums.
  Can be \f(CW`replace\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`scroll\*(C', \f(CW\*(C\`fullframe\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`rscroll\*(C'.
  Default is \f(CW`fullframe\*(C'.
* **win\_func**  
  .IX Item "win_func"
  Set window function used for resynthesis.
* **overlap**  
  .IX Item "overlap"
  Set window overlap. In range \f(CW`[0, 1]\*(C'. Default is \f(CW1,
  which means optimal overlap for selected window function will be picked.
* **orientation**  
  .IX Item "orientation"
  Set orientation of input videos. Can be \f(CW`vertical\*(C' or \f(CW\*(C\`horizontal\*(C'.
  Default is \f(CW`vertical\*(C'.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  First create magnitude and phase videos from audio, assuming audio is stereo with 44100 sample rate,
  then resynthesize videos back to audio with spectrumsynth:
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          ffmpeg -i input.flac -lavfi showspectrum=mode=separate:scale=log:overlap=0.875:color=channel:slide=fullframe:data=magnitude -an -c:v rawvideo magnitude.nut
          ffmpeg -i input.flac -lavfi showspectrum=mode=separate:scale=lin:overlap=0.875:color=channel:slide=fullframe:data=phase -an -c:v rawvideo phase.nut
          ffmpeg -i magnitude.nut -i phase.nut -lavfi spectrumsynth=channels=2:sample_rate=44100:win_func=hann:overlap=0.875:slide=fullframe output.flac
  .Ve

<a name="split-asplit"></a>

### split, asplit

.IX Subsection "split, asplit"
Split input into several identical outputs.

\f(CW`asplit\*(C' works with audio input, \f(CW\*(C\`split\*(C' with video.

The filter accepts a single parameter which specifies the number of outputs. If
unspecified, it defaults to 2.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Create two separate outputs from the same input:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          [in] split [out0][out1]
  .Ve
* ·  
  To create 3 or more outputs, you need to specify the number of
  outputs, like in:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          [in] asplit=3 [out0][out1][out2]
  .Ve
* ·  
  Create two separate outputs from the same input, one cropped and
  one padded:
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          [in] split [splitout1][splitout2];
          [splitout1] crop=100:100:0:0    [cropout];
          [splitout2] pad=200:200:100:100 [padout];
  .Ve
* ·  
  Create 5 copies of the input audio with **ffmpeg**:
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          ffmpeg -i INPUT -filter_complex asplit=5 OUTPUT
  .Ve

<a name="zmq-azmq"></a>

### zmq, azmq

.IX Subsection "zmq, azmq"
Receive commands sent through a libzmq client, and forward them to
filters in the filtergraph.

\f(CW`zmq\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`azmq\*(C' work as a pass-through filters. \f(CW\*(C\`zmq\*(C'
must be inserted between two video filters, \f(CW`azmq\*(C' between two
audio filters. Both are capable to send messages to any filter type.

To enable these filters you need to install the libzmq library and
headers and configure FFmpeg with \f(CW`--enable-libzmq\*(C'.

For more information about libzmq see:
&lt;**http://www.zeromq.org/**&gt;

The \f(CW`zmq\*(C' and \f(CW\*(C\`azmq\*(C' filters work as a libzmq server, which
receives messages sent through a network interface defined by the
**bind\_address** (or the abbreviation "**b**") option.
Default value of this option is _tcp://localhost:5555_. You may
want to alter this value to your needs, but do not forget to escape any
':' signs (see **filtergraph escaping**).

The received message must be in the form:

.Vb 1
        &lt;TARGET&gt; &lt;COMMAND&gt; [&lt;ARG&gt;]
.Ve

_\s-1TARGET\s0_ specifies the target of the command, usually the name of
the filter class or a specific filter instance name. The default
filter instance name uses the pattern **Parsed\_&lt;filter\_name&gt;\_&lt;index&gt;**,
but you can override this by using the **filter\_name@id** syntax
(see **Filtergraph syntax**).

_\s-1COMMAND\s0_ specifies the name of the command for the target filter.

_\s-1ARG\s0_ is optional and specifies the optional argument list for the
given _\s-1COMMAND\s0_.

Upon reception, the message is processed and the corresponding command
is injected into the filtergraph. Depending on the result, the filter
will send a reply to the client, adopting the format:

.Vb 2
        &lt;ERROR_CODE&gt; &lt;ERROR_REASON&gt;
        &lt;MESSAGE&gt;
.Ve

_\s-1MESSAGE\s0_ is optional.

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

Look at _tools/zmqsend_ for an example of a zmq client which can
be used to send commands processed by these filters.

Consider the following filtergraph generated by **ffplay**.
In this example the last overlay filter has an instance name. All other
filters will have default instance names.

.Vb 6
        ffplay -dumpgraph 1 -f lavfi "
        color=s=100x100:c=red  [l];
        color=s=100x100:c=blue [r];
        nullsrc=s=200x100, zmq [bg];
        [bg][l]   overlay     [bg+l];
        [bg+l][r] overlay@my=x=100 "
.Ve

To change the color of the left side of the video, the following
command can be used:

.Vb 1
        echo Parsed_color_0 c yellow | tools/zmqsend
.Ve

To change the right side:

.Vb 1
        echo Parsed_color_1 c pink | tools/zmqsend
.Ve

To change the position of the right side:

.Vb 1
        echo overlay@my x 150 | tools/zmqsend
.Ve

<a name="multimedia-sources"></a>

# Multimedia Sources

.IX Header "MULTIMEDIA SOURCES"
Below is a description of the currently available multimedia sources.

<a name="amovie"></a>

### amovie

.IX Subsection "amovie"
This is the same as **movie** source, except it selects an audio
stream by default.

<a name="movie"></a>

### movie

.IX Subsection "movie"
Read audio and/or video stream(s) from a movie container.

It accepts the following parameters:

* **filename**  
  .IX Item "filename"
  The name of the resource to read (not necessarily a file; it can also be a
  device or a stream accessed through some protocol).
* **format_name, f**  
  .IX Item "format_name, f"
  Specifies the format assumed for the movie to read, and can be either
  the name of a container or an input device. If not specified, the
  format is guessed from _movie\_name_ or by probing.
* **seek_point, sp**  
  .IX Item "seek_point, sp"
  Specifies the seek point in seconds. The frames will be output
  starting from this seek point. The parameter is evaluated with
  \f(CW`av\_strtod\*(C', so the numerical value may be suffixed by an \s-1IS\s0
  postfix. The default value is 0\*(R".
* **streams, s**  
  .IX Item "streams, s"
  Specifies the streams to read. Several streams can be specified,
  separated by +\*(R". The source will then have as many outputs, in the
  same order. The syntax is explained in the Stream specifiers\*(R"
  section in the ffmpeg manual. Two special names, dv\*(R" and \*(L"da\*(R" specify
  respectively the default (best suited) video and audio stream. Default
  is dv\*(R", or \*(L"da\*(R" if the filter is called as \*(L"amovie\*(R".
* **stream_index, si**  
  .IX Item "stream_index, si"
  Specifies the index of the video stream to read. If the value is -1,
  the most suitable video stream will be automatically selected. The default
  value is -1\*(R". Deprecated. If the filter is called \*(L"amovie\*(R", it will select
  audio instead of video.
* **loop**  
  .IX Item "loop"
  Specifies how many times to read the stream in sequence.
  If the value is 0, the stream will be looped infinitely.
  Default value is 1\*(R".
  .Sp
  Note that when the movie is looped the source timestamps are not
  changed, so it will generate non monotonically increasing timestamps.
* **discontinuity**  
  .IX Item "discontinuity"
  Specifies the time difference between frames above which the point is
  considered a timestamp discontinuity which is removed by adjusting the later
  timestamps.

It allows overlaying a second video on top of the main input of
a filtergraph, as shown in this graph:

.Vb 4
        input -----------&gt; deltapts0 --&gt; overlay --&gt; output
                                            ^
                                            |
        movie --&gt; scale--&gt; deltapts1 -------+
.Ve

_Examples_
.IX Subsection "Examples"

* ·  
  Skip 3.2 seconds from the start of the \s-1AVI\s0 file in.avi, and overlay it
  on top of the input labelled in\*(R":
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          movie=in.avi:seek_point=3.2, scale=180:-1, setpts=PTS-STARTPTS [over];
          [in] setpts=PTS-STARTPTS [main];
          [main][over] overlay=16:16 [out]
  .Ve
* ·  
  Read from a video4linux2 device, and overlay it on top of the input
  labelled in\*(R":
  .Sp
  .Vb 3
          movie=/dev/video0:f=video4linux2, scale=180:-1, setpts=PTS-STARTPTS [over];
          [in] setpts=PTS-STARTPTS [main];
          [main][over] overlay=16:16 [out]
  .Ve
* ·  
  Read the first video stream and the audio stream with id 0x81 from
  dvd.vob; the video is connected to the pad named video\*(R" and the audio is
  connected to the pad named audio\*(R":
  .Sp
  .Vb 1
          movie=dvd.vob:s=v:0+#0x81 [video] [audio]
  .Ve

_Commands_
.IX Subsection "Commands"

Both movie and amovie support the following commands:

* **seek**  
  .IX Item "seek"
  Perform seek using av_seek_frame\*(R".
  The syntax is: seek _stream\_index_|_timestamp_|_flags_
    * ·  
      _stream\_index_: If stream_index is -1, a default
      stream is selected, and _timestamp_ is automatically converted
      from \s-1AV_TIME_BASE\s0 units to the stream specific time_base.
    * ·  
      _timestamp_: Timestamp in AVStream.time_base units
      or, if no stream is specified, in \s-1AV_TIME_BASE\s0 units.
    * ·  
      _flags_: Flags which select direction and seeking mode.
* **get\_duration**  
  .IX Item "get_duration"
  Get movie duration in \s-1AV_TIME_BASE\s0 units.
